# RESEARCH OPTIONS & NUMBERS DETAIL (as of 2026-10-06)



# TOPIC: robot_physics
## Options
- **NVIDIA Newton (Linux Foundation)** [GPU multi-solver physics engine (primary candidate)] | status: v1.6.1 released 2026-10-05 (v1.0.0 GA 2026-03-10 at GTC 2026; 1.1 Apr, 1.2 May, 1.3 Jun, 1.4 Jul, 1.5 Aug, 1.6.0 Sep 2026). Donated to the Linux Foundation 2025-09-29 (beta at CoRL 2025). Initiated
  by NVIDIA, Google DeepMind and Disney Research. About 5.7k GitHub stars and 100+ examples. Linux x86/aarch64 and Windows on GPU; macOS CPU only. Requires an NVIDIA GPU (Maxwell or newer, driver 545+). | license: Apache-2.0 for code, CC-BY-4.0 for docs. Free, and can be embedded and redistributed
  in a commercial multi-tenant SaaS. Depends on Warp (Apache-2.0) and needs the NVIDIA CUDA driver. | strengths: One API over many solvers: SolverMuJoCo (MJWarp), Featherstone, SemiImplicit, XPBD, Kamino (NCP; closed loops and passive joints), VBD (cloth, cables, soft bodies; opt-in compliant ALM
  mode in 1.6), Style3D cloth, ImplicitMPM (granular, snow, viscous fluid, two-way rigid coupling).; Contact options include SDF collision, hydroelastic pressure-field contact (concept from TRI Drake) and a configurable CUDA broad/narrow phase (faster in 1.6).; OpenUSD-native; imports URDF, MJCF and
  USD; viewers for GL, USD, RTX, Rerun and Viser; differential-IK and operational-space controllers that work under CUDA-graph capture.; Speed: MJWarp up to 252x (locomotion) and 475x (manipulation) faster than MJX on RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell; in the beta, up to 65% faster in-hand manipulation than
  PhysX in Isaac Lab.; Real deployments: Newton-trained locomotion transferred to a Unitree G1; Skild AI trains GPU-rack assembly (connector insertion, fastening) with SDF and hydroelastic contact; Samsung and Lightwheel use its cable simulation for refrigerator hose insertion. Also used by ETH RSL,
  TUM, PKU and Style3D.; Neutral foundation governance, a weekly-to-monthly release cadence, and integration into both Isaac Lab 3.0 and Isaac Sim 6.1. | weaknesses: Young: 1.0 is only 7 months old, APIs still change monthly, and Isaac Lab's Newton path is beta/early access with a 'focused validated
  set' of classic RL and flat-terrain locomotion tasks.; Differentiability is only 'basic' and only in the Featherstone and SemiImplicit solvers; MJWarp is not differentiable.; GPU path is NVIDIA/CUDA only (vendor lock-in).; MJWarp is non-deterministic on GPU and runs in float32.; No built-in
  tactile sensor model; tactile currently comes via Isaac Lab TacSL or custom code. | best fit: Primary physics backend for robot RL/IL at scale, contact-rich manipulation and deformables (cloth, cables, granular) inside AICHEMIST's SaaS. | sources: https://github.com/newton-physics/newton
  https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases https://pypi.org/project/newton/ https://raw.githubusercontent.com/newton-physics/newton/main/docs/solvers/index.rst
- **MuJoCo Warp (MJWarp)** [GPU batched rigid/articulated solver (Warp port of MuJoCo; default Newton solver)] | status: mujoco-warp 3.15.0 released 2026-10-05; versions track MuJoCo. Officially released alongside MuJoCo 3.5.0 (Feb 2026) but the PyPI classifier is still 'Alpha'. Maintained by Google
  DeepMind and NVIDIA as part of Newton. About 1.5k stars. Results are benchmarked nightly. | license: Apache-2.0; free and embeddable. | strengths: Highest published batched throughput. Nightly RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell figures (PR #1748, 2026-10-05): humanoid 7.95M steps/s, Franka 36.97M, aloha_pot
  3.37M, unitree_g1_flat 3.79M, g1_hfield 2.49M, three_humanoids 1.88M, myoarm 2.09M, aloha_clutter 0.46M.; Uses MuJoCo's convex, soft-constraint contact model with pyramidal or elliptic friction cones and MJCF semantics, so the CPU MuJoCo reference runs the same model.; Callable from JAX through
  MJX (impl='warp') and from PyTorch through Newton/Isaac Lab/mjlab; supports multiple GPUs.; Batch renderer with textures, shadows, heightfields, flex and 3D Gaussian Splatting. | weaknesses: Not differentiable (tracked in issue #500).; Non-deterministic on GPU because of atomic operations; float32
  rather than MuJoCo's float64.; Struggles with single connected mechanisms above about 60 DoF; per-world latency is worse than CPU MuJoCo, so it is unsuitable for MPC or interactive use.; Gaps versus MuJoCo C: PGS and noslip solvers, plugins, some contact override parameters, flex marked
  experimental. | best fit: Default rigid/articulated solver for massively parallel RL: locomotion, humanoid whole-body control, dexterous hands. | sources: https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp https://pypi.org/project/mujoco-warp/ https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/pull/1748
  https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/main/doc/mjwarp/index.rst
- **MuJoCo 3.x (C/CPU) + MJX (JAX) + MuJoCo Playground** [Reference articulated-body engine, JAX GPU front end, and RL suite] | status: MuJoCo 3.15.0 released 2026-10-05; roughly 3-week cadence (3.5 on 2026-02-12 through 3.15). Recent additions: 3.14 experimental IPC (penetration-free) flex contact
  and 10x faster procedural model editing; 3.13 a 'discrete' integrator, cylinder multicontact and a rewritten plane-mesh collider; 3.15 experimental Stable Neo-Hookean flex and capsule multicontact; 3.11 conveyor surface velocity and adhesion; 3.12 PID actuator; 3.7 DC-motor model; 3.5 system-
  identification toolbox. MJX now exposes MJWarp as a backend. MuJoCo Playground won the RSS 2025 Outstanding Demo Paper award. | license: Apache-2.0; free and embeddable. Playground is also Apache-2.0. | strengths: The contact-fidelity standard for robot learning: convex soft-contact optimization,
  elliptic or pyramidal cones, float64, deterministic on CPU.; Strong sim2real record: Playground zero-shot transfer to Go1, Berkeley Humanoid, G1, Booster T1, LEAP hand (state and pixels via the Madrona renderer) and Franka.; Rich actuator and sensor library, including a touch-grid taxel plugin for
  tactile arrays.; MJCF is a de facto robot asset format with large community model zoos. | weaknesses: CPU single-thread per world, so it does not scale for RL without MJWarp.; Mesh collisions use convex hulls unless SDF plugins or newer colliders are used.; Deformables (flex) are less mature than
  dedicated FEM.; Rendering is basic (rasterizer), not photoreal. | best fit: Ground-truth reference backend, interactive/MPC and teleoperation backend, sysid and calibration, and CI regression for MJWarp runs. | sources: https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/releases
  https://pypi.org/project/mujoco/ https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/3.7.0/changelog.html https://roboticsproceedings.org/rss21/p020.html
- **mjlab** [Lightweight RL framework (Isaac Lab manager-based API on MuJoCo Warp)] | status: Active on PyPI (1.5.x series). arXiv 2601.22074 (2026-01-29) from UC Berkeley and Sorbonne (Zakka, Liao, Yi, Le Lay, Sreenath, Abbeel). About 3.2k stars. Pinned to MuJoCo Warp 3.11 at last check. | license:
  Apache-2.0; free. | strengths: Single-command install with minimal dependencies and direct access to native MuJoCo data.; Same manager-based API as Isaac Lab, so tasks port in either direction.; Reference tasks for velocity tracking, BeyondMimic motion imitation (Unitree G1 dance) and
  manipulation; multi-GPU training. | weaknesses: NVIDIA GPU required for training (macOS evaluation only).; Smaller ecosystem than Isaac Lab; no photoreal rendering or USD scene tooling. | best fit: Fast, low-friction humanoid/quadruped RL training front end and a template for AICHEMIST's own
  training SDK. | sources: https://github.com/mujocolab/mjlab https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22074
- **NVIDIA PhysX 5 SDK (+ ovphysx)** [GPU rigid/articulation/deformable engine] | status: PhysX SDK 5.11.0 is the latest. Full GPU source (500+ CUDA kernels) opened in 5.6 (April 2025). ovphysx 0.6.3 (2026-09-16, Alpha) is a pip-installable, Kit-free PhysX runtime with USD stage, DLPack/Warp tensors
  and environment cloning, early access as part of the 'Omniverse libraries' announced at SIGGRAPH 2026, with production release planned for later in 2026. | license: The PhysX SDK repo is BSD-3-Clause (embeddable and redistributable). ovphysx's PyPI license is 'LicenseRef-NVIDIA-Omniverse'
  (proprietary), so its SaaS terms need verification. | strengths: Most proven GPU robotics engine: the Isaac Gym/Isaac Lab lineage behind most published quadruped, humanoid and dexterous sim2real results.; TGS/PGS solvers, reduced-coordinate (Featherstone) articulations, tendons, GPU SDF collision
  for non-convex triangle meshes, FEM volume/surface deformables, particles; includes the Vehicle2 SDK.; Deterministic on identical hardware and PhysX version for rigid and articulated scenes (per Isaac Lab docs).; Highest independent fidelity on some tasks: GPUSimBench inclined-plane test
  (ManiSkill/PhysX had the lowest error); GAUGE (Isaac Sim lowest error on slope contact, non-smooth contact and turntable). | weaknesses: Friction is patch-based or approximate rather than an exact Coulomb cone.; No determinism guarantee for non-rigid bodies (cloth, soft bodies); results vary
  across GPU models.; Not differentiable.; The convenient Python and USD layers (Kit, ovphysx) carry proprietary NVIDIA licenses. | best fit: Compatibility backend for customers on the Isaac/SimReady ecosystem; vehicle dynamics via Vehicle2; validated legacy RL tasks. | sources:
  https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/PhysX https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/PhysX/releases https://pypi.org/project/ovphysx/ https://www.cgchannel.com/2025/04/nvidia-open-sources-physxs-gpu-simulation-code
- **NVIDIA Isaac Sim 5.x/6.x** [Full digital-twin application (Omniverse Kit, RTX rendering, sensors, SDG)] | status: Isaac Sim 5.0 source opened on GitHub in 2025 (Computex/SIGGRAPH 2025). 6.0 early developer preview 2026-01-05, GA 2026-06-08 (multi-backend PhysX and Newton physics, pluggable
  renderers, install without Kit, Isaac Sim MCP and agent skills, NuRec Gaussian splatting). 6.0.1 patch. 6.1.0 September 2026 (experimental Newton 1.5.0 with VBD, XPBD and hydroelastic contacts). About 4.2k stars. | license: Isaac Sim source is Apache-2.0, but it needs Omniverse Kit SDK and assets
  under the 'Isaac Sim Additional Software and Materials License'. Free for internal R&D. Redistributing it, or delivering Isaac Sim (with Kit) as a service to third parties (including turnkey installs), requires NVIDIA AI Enterprise. Selling only outputs (videos, reports, datasets) does not. List
  price about $4,500 per GPU per year for Omniverse/AI Enterprise (EU reseller about EUR 5,800 per GPU per year). | strengths: Best-in-class RTX sensor simulation (camera, lidar, radar) together with PhysX/Newton physics in one USD stage.; SimReady assets, Replicator synthetic data generation,
  teleoperation, ROS 2 bridge, NuRec 3DGS scenes.; Largest industrial ecosystem: ABB, FANUC, KUKA, Universal Robots and others at GTC 2026. | weaknesses: Heavy (Kit runtime); RTX rendering needs RT-core GPUs.; Hosting it as a service in a multi-tenant SaaS triggers AI Enterprise licensing.; Fast-
  moving major versions (5.0, 6.0, 6.1 within about 15 months). | best fit: Optional premium/BYOL workspace and internal synthetic-data factory for CEN (outputs-only use is license-exempt), not the embedded physics core. | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim
  https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/isaac-sim-6-0-general-availability/372621 https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/6.1.0/common/license-faq.html https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/6.1.0/overview/release_notes.html
- **NVIDIA Isaac Lab 2.x -> 3.0** [Robot-learning framework (RL, imitation learning, Mimic data generation) with multiple physics and render backends] | status: v2.3.2 (2026-02-02) was the final 2.x release. 3.0.0-beta at GTC (Mar 2026), beta2 (Jun 2026), beta2.patch1 for Isaac Sim 6.0.1, and
  v3.0.0-EA (2026-09-16; dates per the GitHub releases page) built for Isaac Sim 6.1. Physics backends: PhysX, Newton (MJWarp validated; Kamino beta; VBD experimental) and OVPhysX. Renderers: Isaac RTX, OVRTX, Newton Warp renderer. The Newton path runs without Kit or Isaac Sim. Production API
  stability promised for later in 2026. About 8.3k stars. Paper arXiv 2511.04831. | license: BSD-3-Clause (isaaclab_mimic is Apache-2.0); free. The Kit-less Newton path avoids Omniverse licensing; the PhysX/RTX-via-Kit paths inherit Isaac Sim terms. | strengths: Most complete training feature set:
  manager and direct task APIs, actuator models, multi-rate sensors, domain randomization, Mimic/teleop demonstration generation, visuo-tactile (TacSL) sensor, multi-GPU and multi-node.; Published PhysX throughput (RTX 4090): G1 rough terrain 4096 envs at 94k env-steps/s (82k with training); Shadow-
  hand cube repose 8192 envs at 200k; Cartpole 1.1M; Cartpole with RGB camera 50k; 16x L40 G1 rough at 1.2M.; Unified Volume/Surface/Cable deformable API across PhysX FEM and Newton VBD. | weaknesses: 3.0 is still early access, with breaking changes on the develop branch.; The Newton integration is
  limited to a validated subset of tasks.; Camera-based environments are memory-hungry (Cartpole-RGB uses 16.7 GB VRAM at 1024 envs). | best fit: Primary training front end (run without Kit, on Newton), with PhysX/RTX paths for customers who license Isaac Sim. | sources: https://github.com/isaac-
  sim/IsaacLab https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst https://isaac-sim.github.io/IsaacLab/release/3.0.0-beta2/source/overview/core-concepts/physical-
  backends/newton/index.html
- **Kamino (Disney Research Zurich, in Newton)** [GPU constrained multibody solver (maximal coordinates, nonlinear complementarity)] | status: Paper arXiv 2603.16536 (2026-03-17). Ships in Newton 1.x (Coulomb friction, joint limits and batched kinematics added in 1.6). Beta support in Isaac Lab 3.0.
  | license: Apache-2.0 (part of Newton). | strengths: Native kinematic loops and passive actuation; trained a walking policy for the DR Legs biped (6 nested loops) with 4096 parallel envs on one GPU.; Complementarity (hard contact) formulation, which complements MuJoCo's soft contact. | weaknesses:
  Newest solver with a limited validated task set. | best fit: Closed-chain mechanisms: parallel grippers, linkage legs, Delta robots, some dexterous hands. | sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16536 https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases
- **Genesis World (Genesis-Embodied-AI / Genesis AI)** [GPU multi-physics simulator + renderer + compiler] | status: genesis-world 1.4.3 released 2026-09-30 (1.4.0 on 2026-09-06; Genesis World 1.0 open-sourced May 2026 together with the Nyx path-tracer and the Quadrants compiler). 30k+ stars. The
  company (Genesis AI, $105M seed from Eclipse, Khosla and others) went full-stack with the GENE-26.5 manipulation model in May 2026. | license: Apache-2.0. Nyx ships as a separate gs-nyx package that was on an internal index pending public release, so verify its license before embedding. |
  strengths: Broadest unified multi-physics: rigid (MuJoCo-style), FEM, MPM, SPH, PBD, IPC (penetration-free, via libuipc) and a SAP coupler derived from Drake with hydroelastic rigid-FEM contact.; Built-in tactile, IMU, lidar, depth and contact-force sensors.; Quadrants compiler (forked from Taichi
  in June 2025) targets CUDA, ROCm, Metal, Vulkan and CPU, the main hedge against NVIDIA lock-in; vendor claims 10x faster launch and up to 4.6x runtime versus the initial release.; 2026 releases added a deterministic GPU rigid mode, contact-patch multicontact, hibernation and trajectory replay. |
  weaknesses: Credibility problem with headline speed: the 43M FPS Franka claim (RTX 4090) was static-scene and single-substep; Stone Tao's corrected benchmark was about 150x lower; Genesis later revised to 27M FPS with random actions and published a fuller report.; Rigid contact solver criticized
  as simple; accuracy was sensitive to solver tuning; GPUSimBench documents GPU non-determinism across simulators including Genesis.; Smaller sim2real record than Isaac Lab or MuJoCo; the company's priorities shifted toward its own foundation model. | best fit: Secondary or experimental backend for
  multi-physics (fluids, granular, IPC deformables) and tactile research, and an AMD/Apple-GPU hedge. | sources: https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/Genesis https://pypi.org/project/genesis-world/ https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/Genesis/releases https://stoneztao.substack.com/p/the-new-
  hyped-genesis-simulator-is
- **Drake (Toyota Research Institute)** [CPU model-based robotics toolbox with high-fidelity contact] | status: v1.57.0 released 2026-09-10 (monthly cadence). Python 3.12-3.14 wheels for Linux x86/ARM and macOS ARM. Used by TRI for the LBM Eval (49-task) simulation benchmark. | license: BSD-3-Clause
  core. PyPI lists 'BSD and Other/Proprietary', because bundled third-party solvers have their own terms; check before redistribution. | strengths: Hydroelastic (pressure-field) contact with distributed patches for non-convex meshes, which Newton adopted.; SAP convex compliant contact solver; FEM
  deformables with rigid-deformable contact.; Optimization, planning and system-analysis tools; rigorous, well-tested C++. | weaknesses: CPU only (the SAP/Cholesky parts parallelize poorly); far too slow for 10^4-env RL.; Steeper learning curve; no GPU rendering pipeline. | best fit: Offline gold-
  standard validation of contact-rich assembly and grasping, and reference for calibrating Newton hydroelastic parameters. | sources: https://github.com/RobotLocomotion/drake/releases https://pypi.org/project/drake/ https://drake.mit.edu/doxygen_cxx/group__hydroelastic__user__guide.html
- **ManiSkill3 / SAPIEN 3** [GPU manipulation simulator + benchmark (PhysX 5 backend)] | status: mani-skill 3.0.1 released 2026-04-21 (after 3.0.0b22 in Dec 2025); RSS 2025 paper; about 3.4k stars; one listed maintainer on PyPI. | license: Code Apache-2.0 (SAPIEN is MIT). Some assets are CC BY-NC
  4.0, so they cannot ship in a commercial product. | strengths: GPU heterogeneous scenes (different objects per environment); RGBD and segmentation at 30k+ FPS on an RTX 4090; more GPU-memory-efficient than Isaac Lab for camera environments (its own benchmark; rasterizer versus ray tracing, so not
  apples-to-apples).; Best fidelity on the GPUSimBench inclined-plane test (lowest error, 2.52 cm); real2sim evaluation of VLA policies. | weaknesses: Small team and bus-factor risk; inherits PhysX contact limits; limited deformables; NC asset licenses. | best fit: Reference for manipulation
  benchmarks and heterogeneous-scene batching; not a platform core. | sources: https://github.com/haosulab/ManiSkill https://pypi.org/project/mani-skill/ https://raw.githubusercontent.com/haosulab/ManiSkill/main/docs/source/user_guide/additional_resources/performance_benchmarking.md

  Playground. | weaknesses: Physics deprecated; do not adopt as an engine. | best fit: Only as a JAX training library on top of MJX/MJWarp. | sources: https://github.com/google/brax
- **Project Chrono (incl. Chrono::Vehicle, FSI/CRM, Sensor, ROS)** [Multibody + vehicle + terramechanics + FSI engine] | status: Chrono 10.0.0 (late March / early April 2026): refactored Chrono::FSI with SPH and TDPF fluid solvers, peridynamics, new camera sensor features, checkpointing for Chrono

  grade, low activity (unverified). Dojo.jl: MIT, 'no longer actively developed' since April 2023. | license: Taichi Apache-2.0; TDS Apache-2.0; Dojo MIT. | strengths: Gradient-based system identification and design research. | weaknesses: Not maintained or production-grade; Newton's differentiable

- **New 2025-2026 entrants: MotrixSim/MotrixLab (Motphys) + GS-Playground; RoboVerse/MetaSim** [New engines and multi-backend abstraction layers] | status: MotrixSim: a Rust generalized-coordinate engine with a proprietary constraint solver; docs and MotrixLab are Apache-2.0 on GitHub, but the core
  appears to be binary-only (unverified). Huawei Connect 2026 solution on Kunpeng CPUs reached a 362k steps/s peak. GS-Playground (RSS 2026) combines MotrixSim with batched 3D Gaussian Splatting: about 10^4 FPS across 2048 envs at 640x480, with 90% zero-shot real grasp success. RoboVerse/MetaSim
  (2025) wraps Isaac Sim, Isaac Gym, MuJoCo, SAPIEN 2/3, Genesis, PyBullet and Newton behind one API. | license: MotrixLab/docs Apache-2.0 (core license unclear); RoboVerse open source. | strengths: GS-Playground validates the 3DGS-plus-physics approach aligned with CEN's NeRF/3DGS asset
  generation.; MetaSim shows that a cross-simulator abstraction layer is feasible. | weaknesses: MotrixSim is immature, China-centric and has no GPU-scale evidence; 3DGS cannot represent deformables or relighting well. | best fit: Design reference for AICHEMIST's backend abstraction and 3DGS
  rendering; not a core dependency. | sources: https://motrixsim.readthedocs.io/en/latest/index.html https://autonews.gasgoo.com/articles/news/2102741730670886912 https://roboticsproceedings.org/rss22/p093.html https://roboverse.wiki/metasim/features/support_matrix
## Key facts
- [high] Newton 1.0.0 was released 2026-03-10 (GA announced at GTC 2026); the latest release is v1.6.1 on 2026-10-05, with monthly minor releases since. (https://pypi.org/project/newton/ ; https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases)
- [high] Newton is Apache-2.0, a Linux Foundation project initiated by Disney Research, Google DeepMind and NVIDIA; it was donated to the LF on 2025-09-29 as a beta. (https://github.com/newton-physics/newton ; https://www.therobotreport.com/nvidia-launches-newton-physics-engine-gr00t-ai-corl-2025/)
- [high] Newton solvers are Featherstone, MuJoCo (MJWarp), SemiImplicit, XPBD, Kamino, VBD, Style3D and ImplicitMPM. Only Featherstone and SemiImplicit have 'basic' differentiability, and only Featherstone and MuJoCo support generalized-coordinate articulations.
  (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/newton-physics/newton/main/docs/solvers/index.rst)
- [medium] MuJoCo Warp is up to 252x (locomotion) and 475x (manipulation) faster than MJX on RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (Newton 1.0 blog); the Newton beta figures were 152x and 313x on an RTX 4090. (https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/newton-adds-contact-rich-manipulation-and-locomotion-capabilities-for-
  industrial-robotics ; https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/train-a-quadruped-locomotion-policy-and-simulate-cloth-manipulation-with-nvidia-isaac-lab-and-newton)
- [high] MJWarp is not differentiable, is non-deterministic on GPU (atomics), uses float32, and struggles with single connected mechanisms above about 60 DoF; MJX exposes it as impl='warp'. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/main/doc/mjwarp/index.rst)
- [high] MuJoCo 3.15.0 and mujoco-warp 3.15.0 were released 2026-10-05; MuJoCo 3.14 added experimental IPC flex contact and 3.15 added experimental Stable Neo-Hookean flex elasticity. (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/releases ; https://pypi.org/project/mujoco-warp/)
- [medium] MuJoCo Warp was officially released with MuJoCo 3.5.0 (2026-02-12), which also added a Python system-identification toolbox. (https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/3.7.0/changelog.html)
- [medium] Isaac Sim 6.0 reached GA on 2026-06-08 with multi-backend physics (PhysX and Newton), pluggable renderers and Kit-less install; 6.1.0 (September 2026) updated the experimental Newton integration to 1.5.0 with VBD, XPBD and hydroelastic contacts.
  (https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/isaac-sim-6-0-general-availability/372621 ; https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/6.1.0/overview/release_notes.html)
- [high] Isaac Sim source is Apache-2.0, but delivering Isaac Sim (with Omniverse Kit) as a service to third parties, or redistributing it, requires an NVIDIA AI Enterprise license. Running it internally and selling only outputs (datasets, videos, reports) does not.
  (https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/6.1.0/common/license-faq.html)
- [high] Isaac Lab is BSD-3-Clause (isaaclab_mimic Apache-2.0). v3.0.0-EA (Sep 2026) supports PhysX, Newton/MJWarp and OVPhysX physics and Isaac RTX, OVRTX and Newton Warp renderers; Kit-less Newton workflows need no Isaac Sim. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab ; https://github.com/isaac-
  sim/IsaacLab/releases)
- [high] In Isaac Lab 3.0 the Newton backend's validated solver is MJWarp; Kamino is beta on selected classic tasks and VBD is experimental for cloth and soft bodies. Newton support is described as beta, with a focused set of classic RL and flat-terrain locomotion environments. (https://isaac-
  sim.github.io/IsaacLab/release/3.0.0-beta2/source/overview/core-concepts/physical-backends/newton/index.html)
- [high] The PhysX SDK, including full GPU source (500+ CUDA kernels), has been open source under BSD-3 since PhysX 5.6 (April 2025); the latest SDK is 5.11.0. (https://www.cgchannel.com/2025/04/nvidia-open-sources-physxs-gpu-simulation-code ; https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/PhysX)
- [high] ovphysx (a Kit-free PhysX Python runtime, v0.6.3, 2026-09-16, Alpha) is licensed under 'LicenseRef-NVIDIA-Omniverse', not BSD. (https://pypi.org/project/ovphysx/)
- [medium] Omniverse libraries (ovrtx, ovphysx, ovstage) were announced at SIGGRAPH 2026 in early access, with production release and API stability planned for later in 2026. (https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/integrate-physical-ai-capabilities-into-existing-apps-with-nvidia-omniverse-libraries/ ;
  https://www.uctoday.com/immersive-workplace-xr-tech/nvidia-omniverse-libraries-agent-toolkit-siggraph-2026/)
- [high] PhysX GPU simulation is deterministic for rigid and articulated scenes on the same hardware and software version, but not across hardware, and not for cloth or soft bodies. (https://isaac-sim.github.io/IsaacLab/main/source/features/reproducibility.html)
- [medium] The Genesis 43M FPS claim (Franka, RTX 4090) was criticized as misleading; corrected benchmarks were about 150x lower, and Genesis revised it to 27M FPS with random actions. (https://stoneztao.substack.com/p/the-new-hyped-genesis-simulator-is)
- [medium] Genesis World 1.0 was open-sourced in May 2026 together with Nyx (path tracer) and Quadrants (compiler supporting CUDA, ROCm, Metal, Vulkan and CPU); genesis-world 1.4.3 (2026-09-30) is Apache-2.0. (https://www.marktechpost.com/2026/05/30/genesis-ai-releases-nyx-quadrants-and-genesis-
  world-1-0-physics-platform-for-scalable-robotics-foundation-model-evaluation/ ; https://pypi.org/project/genesis-world/ ; https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/Genesis)
- [medium] GPUSimBench (arXiv 2607.13059, Jul 2026) finds that GPU-batched execution introduces significant run-to-run and inter-environment non-determinism in mainstream simulators (Isaac Lab, Genesis and others). On the inclined-plane fidelity test, ManiSkill (EMD 2.520 cm) and MJX (3.970 cm) were
  best. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13059)
- [medium] GAUGE (arXiv 2608.05948, Aug 2026) benchmarked Isaac Sim 6.0.0, Genesis and Newton 1.3.0 on 14 real-measurement task families and found no uniformly faithful engine; the largest gaps were in impulsive contact, fast textile motion and volumetric deformation.
  (https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05948)
- [medium] Newton production uses: Skild AI trains GPU-rack assembly RL with SDF and hydroelastic contacts via Isaac Lab; Samsung and Lightwheel use Newton cable simulation for refrigerator hose insertion; a Newton-trained locomotion policy has been deployed on a Unitree G1.
  (https://blockchain.news/news/nvidia-newton-physics-engine-industrial-robotics-gtc-2026 ; https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/train-a-quadruped-locomotion-policy-and-simulate-cloth-manipulation-with-nvidia-isaac-lab-and-newton)
- [high] Brax physics pipelines are deprecated; only brax/training is maintained since 0.13.0, and users are directed to MJX and MuJoCo Playground. (https://github.com/google/brax)
- [high] PyBullet's last release is 3.2.7 (2025-01-30), and the Bullet issue tracker is closed. (https://pypi.org/project/pybullet/ ; https://github.com/bulletphysics/bullet3)
- [high] The RaiSim v1 repo was archived on 2026-04-25; RaiSim requires a commercial license and activation key. (https://github.com/raisimTech/raisimLib)
- [high] Drake v1.57.0 was released 2026-09-10; it provides hydroelastic contact and the SAP solver, CPU only. (https://pypi.org/project/drake/ ; https://drake.mit.edu/doxygen_cxx/group__hydroelastic__user__guide.html)
- [medium] Project Chrono 10.0.0 (spring 2026) added a refactored FSI (SPH and TDPF), peridynamics, sensor updates and Chrono::Vehicle checkpointing; BSD-3. (https://projectchrono.org/news/ ; https://github.com/projectchrono/chrono/releases)
- [high] Kamino (Disney Research + NVIDIA, arXiv 2603.16536) solves constrained multibody dynamics as an NCP on GPU in Warp/Newton and trained the DR Legs biped (6 nested kinematic loops) with 4096 envs on one GPU. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16536)
- [high] mjlab (Apache-2.0, arXiv 2601.22074) combines the Isaac Lab manager-based API with MuJoCo Warp and ships velocity-tracking, BeyondMimic motion-imitation and manipulation tasks. (https://github.com/mujocolab/mjlab ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22074)
- [high] MuJoCo Playground (RSS 2025 Outstanding Demo Paper) achieved zero-shot sim2real on Berkeley Humanoid, Unitree Go1 and G1, Booster T1, LEAP hand and Franka, from state and pixels. (https://roboticsproceedings.org/rss21/p020.html ; https://arxiv.org/html/2502.08844v1)
- [high] ManiSkill3 code is Apache-2.0 but some assets are CC BY-NC 4.0; mani-skill 3.0.1 was released 2026-04-21. (https://github.com/haosulab/ManiSkill ; https://pypi.org/project/mani-skill/)
## Quantitative data
- MJWarp humanoid (dense) throughput, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell, nightly benchmark: 7,947,604 steps/s (2026-10-05; worlds per scene set by the benchmark config, not stated in the PR) (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/pull/1748)
- MJWarp Franka Panda (dense) throughput, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell: 36,965,163 steps/s (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/pull/1748)
- MJWarp Unitree G1 flat / heightfield throughput, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell: 3,793,923 / 2,493,617 steps/s (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/pull/1748)
- MJWarp ALOHA pot / ALOHA clutter / three humanoids / myoarm, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell: 3.37M / 0.46M / 1.88M / 2.09M steps/s (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/pull/1748)
- MJWarp sparse humanoid at 8,192 worlds, RTX 5090: about 6.47M steps/s (from search-result summary of an optimization PR; not directly verified) (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp (PR discussions via search))
- MJWarp speedup vs MJX (Newton 1.0, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell): up to 252x locomotion, 475x manipulation (https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/newton-adds-contact-rich-manipulation-and-locomotion-capabilities-for-industrial-robotics)
- MJWarp speedup vs MJX (Newton beta, RTX 4090): up to 152x locomotion, 313x manipulation (https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/train-a-quadruped-locomotion-policy-and-simulate-cloth-manipulation-with-nvidia-isaac-lab-and-newton)
- Newton (MJWarp) vs PhysX in Isaac Lab, in-hand dexterous manipulation: up to 65% faster (Newton beta) (https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/train-a-quadruped-locomotion-policy-and-simulate-cloth-manipulation-with-nvidia-isaac-lab-and-newton)
- Isaac Lab (PhysX) Isaac-Velocity-Rough-G1-v0, 4,096 envs, RTX 4090 + Ryzen 9 7950X: 94k env-steps/s (step only); 88k with inference; 82k with training. VRAM 6.1 GB. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- Isaac Lab (PhysX) Shadow-hand Repose-Cube, 8,192 envs, RTX 4090: 200k env-steps/s (170k with training) (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- Isaac Lab Cartpole state / RGB-camera, RTX 4090: 1.1M env-steps/s (4,096 envs) / 50k (1,024 envs; 16.7 GB VRAM) (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- Isaac Lab G1 rough, multi-GPU scaling: L40: 72k; 4x L40: 290k; 16x L40 (4 nodes): 1.2M env-steps/s (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- ManiSkill3 RGBD + segmentation rendering throughput, RTX 4090: 30,000+ FPS (https://github.com/haosulab/ManiSkill)
- Genesis headline claim vs critique: 43M FPS claimed (Franka, RTX 4090, static/self-collision only); 27M FPS with random actions; independent re-run about 150x lower than reported under realistic settings (https://stoneztao.substack.com/p/the-new-hyped-genesis-simulator-is)
- Genesis World 1.0 Quadrants compiler vs initial Genesis: 10x faster launch, up to 4.6x runtime (vendor claim) (https://www.marktechpost.com/2026/05/30/genesis-ai-releases-nyx-quadrants-and-genesis-world-1-0-physics-platform-for-scalable-robotics-foundation-model-evaluation/)
- GPUSimBench inclined-plane fidelity (EMD vs real): ManiSkill 2.520 cm; MJX 3.970 cm (best two) (https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13059)
- GAUGE relative RMSE examples (Isaac Sim vs Genesis): Turntable 0.17x vs 0.57x; fabric tension 0.73x vs 1.51x (normalized; lower is better). Newton 1.3.0 also evaluated. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05948)
- Taccel tactile simulation throughput, single H100: 915 FPS total with 4,096 envs (peg insertion, dual sensors; 18x wall-clock); 12.67 FPS with 256 envs for full-hand tactile grasping (https://papers.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/87d91d52272c3166315ca20b18519b9e-Abstract-Conference.html)
- TacSL visuotactile speedup: >200x faster than prior state of the art (https://research.nvidia.com/publication/2025-01_tacsl-library-visuotactile-sensor-simulation-and-learning)
- Kamino closed-loop biped RL: 4,096 parallel envs on a single GPU (DR Legs, 6 nested loops) (https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16536)
- GS-Playground (MotrixSim + batched 3DGS) rendering throughput / sim2real: about 10^4 FPS aggregate, 2,048 envs at 640x480; 90% zero-shot real grasp success (https://roboticsproceedings.org/rss22/p093.html)
- MotrixSim on Huawei Kunpeng CPU: 362,202 steps/s peak; 345,335 steady state (https://autonews.gasgoo.com/articles/news/2102741730670886912)
- NVIDIA AI Enterprise / Omniverse Enterprise license (needed to serve Isaac Sim with Kit to third parties): about $4,500 per GPU per year list; EU reseller about EUR 5,800 per GPU per year (https://pi3g.com/nvidia-ai-enterprise-subscription-cost-in-2026/ ; https://us.fitgap.com/products/omniverse)
- GitHub stars (community momentum, Oct 2026): Genesis 30k+; Bullet 14.8k; Jolt 11.7k; Isaac Lab 8.3k; Warp 7.2k; Newton 5.7k; Isaac Sim 4.2k; ManiSkill 3.4k; mjlab 3.2k; Brax 3.2k; Chrono 3.1k; MJWarp 1.5k; DART 1.2k (respective GitHub repos (fetched 2026-10-06))
- Release cadence 2026: Newton: 1.0 (Mar 10) to 1.6.1 (Oct 5); MuJoCo: 3.5 (Feb 12) to 3.15 (Oct 5); Warp 1.15 to 1.18 (Jul-Oct); Drake monthly (v1.57 on Sep 10) (https://pypi.org/project/newton/ ; https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/releases ; https://pypi.org/project/warp-lang/ ;
  https://pypi.org/project/drake/)


# TOPIC: vehicle_sim
## Options
- **CARLA** [Open-source AV simulator (Unreal Engine)] | status: Latest tag is 0.10.0 (19 Dec 2024): UE4.26 to UE5.5 migration, Lumen/Nanite, PhysX replaced by Chaos, native ROS 2, InvertedAI traffic. 0.9.16 (16 Sep 2025, UE4 line) added Cosmos Transfer1, NVIDIA NuRec, a SimReady OpenUSD/MDL
  converter, left-hand traffic and V2X. No 0.10.1 tag found. The ue5-dev branch is active (last push 2026-10-02). Directed by the Computer Vision Center (UAB Barcelona). Neya Systems partnered on the UE5 port. ~14.5k GitHub stars. | license: Code MIT; assets CC-BY. Free. Commercial products built on
  the UE fork carry Epic EULA obligations (non-game seat fee of $1,850/seat/yr for companies over $1M revenue applies to UE use). | strengths: De facto research standard, with a leaderboard, ScenarioRunner (OpenSCENARIO) and a huge community; Official NVIDIA integration of NuRec and Cosmos Transfer
  for neural and augmentation workflows; Native ROS 2 and OpenDRIVE maps; permissive license allows a white-label fork | weaknesses: Small team; split between UE4 (0.9.x) and UE5 lines, and the leaderboard and most papers stay on 0.9.x; UE5 build is heavy (RTX 3070+ with 16 GB+ VRAM, 32 GB+ RAM,
  Ubuntu 22.04/24.04 or Windows 11 only); Vehicle dynamics (Chaos) and sensor physics are not engineering-grade; no ISO 26262 tool qualification | best fit: Open front-end for academic and startup customers, OpenSCENARIO/leaderboard compatibility, and fast Korean-road perception-data PoCs | sources:
  https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla/releases https://carla.org/2024/12/19/release-0.10.0 https://carla.org/2025/09/16/release-0.9.16
- **NVIDIA Omniverse AV stack (Blueprint for AV Simulation + Sensor RTX + NuRec); legacy DRIVE Sim** [Physically based sensor simulation and neural reconstruction platform] | status: DRIVE Sim (Omniverse-based) was never made generally available. NVIDIA now ships the Omniverse Blueprint for AV
  simulation, Sensor RTX APIs (early access via partners Foretellix, Mcity, MITRE, Accenture) and Omniverse NuRec, which was declared GA on NGC at GTC 2026. NuRec uses 3DGUT Gaussians that handle fisheye and rolling shutter; NuRec Fixer is a transformer that repairs reconstruction artifacts. Instant
  NuRec (Jul 2026) reconstructs a 10–20 s multi-camera clip feed-forward in ~1.5 s. Partners include CARLA, Foretellix, Applied Intuition and 51Sim (China, exclusive). | license: NuRec libraries are on NGC; enterprise use is likely tied to NVIDIA AI Enterprise (exact terms unverified). 3DGRUT
  reference code is Apache-2.0. Offering Kit-based apps as a service requires NVAIE: $4,500/GPU/yr list, $1,125 with the 75% Inception discount. | strengths: Best-in-class RTX ray-traced camera, lidar and radar plus Gaussian neural rendering in one OpenUSD pipeline; Real logs can become closed-loop
  scenes in seconds (Instant NuRec), which helps cover the long tail; Large ecosystem; integration with Cosmos for appearance augmentation | weaknesses: Vendor lock-in to NVIDIA RTX GPUs and Omniverse licensing for SaaS; Neural scenes degrade off the recorded trajectory; physical guarantees are weak
  for safety arguments; Sensor RTX access has been partner and early-access based | best fit: Core rendering and neural-reconstruction layer of AICHEMIST's platform (integrate, do not rebuild) | sources: https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-omniverse-nurec-reaches-general-availability
  https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2607.14203 https://blogs.nvidia.com/blog/drive-sim-nvidia-omniverse
- **NVIDIA Cosmos (Predict 2.5 / Transfer 2.5 / Reason 2 / Cosmos 3 / Cosmos-Drive-Dreams)** [World foundation models for synthetic data and augmentation] | status: Cosmos-Predict2.5 (2B/14B) and Transfer2.5 were released 6 Oct 2025. Cosmos 3 launched 1 Jun 2026 at GTC Taipei: a two-tower mixture-
  of-transformers 'omnimodel' covering reasoning, generation and action, in Nano (16B), Super (64B, H200/B200) and Edge (4B, Jetson Orin; July 2026). Cosmos-Drive-Dreams released 81,802 synthetic clips with HDMap and LiDAR; its RDS training set is ~3.6M 20-second 6-view clips (~20k hours). |
  license: Cosmos 3 weights, code and data are under OpenMDW-1.1 (commercial use and derivatives allowed with attribution). Cosmos 2.5 code is Apache-2.0. Earlier models use the NVIDIA Open Model License: commercial use allowed, 'Built on NVIDIA Cosmos' attribution required, and rights terminate if
  guardrails are bypassed. | strengths: Free, commercially usable generative augmentation for weather, lighting and geography from one simulation run; Multi-view and LiDAR-conditioned AV variants exist; post-training recipes are public; Fits AICHEMIST's synthetic-data business directly | weaknesses:
  Heavy GPU requirements (Super needs H200/B200); Generated pixels have no guaranteed physical or label consistency; labels must come from conditioning inputs; Guardrail and attribution clauses must be honoured in a SaaS product | best fit: Domain-gap closing and long-tail augmentation of
  AICHEMIST's 3D-rendered synthetic datasets (vehicles, drones, ships) | sources: https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos https://research.nvidia.com/labs/cosmos-lab/cosmos-predict2.5 https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/agreements/enterprise-software/nvidia-open-model-license
- **NVIDIA AlpaSim + AlpaGym + OmniDreams + Alpamayo models** [Open closed-loop AV policy simulation and RL training] | status: Alpamayo 1 (10B VLA, formerly R1) at CES Jan 2026; Alpamayo 1.5 in Mar 2026; Alpamayo 2 Super (32B) on 1 Jun 2026. AlpaSim is a gRPC microservice simulator with a pluggable
  NuRec renderer or an OmniDreams video-model renderer. Press reports ~900 reconstructed scenes and a 1,700+ hour dataset. AlpaGym (Jun 2026) is high-throughput closed-loop RL on AlpaSim. OmniDreams (Jun 2026) is a real-time autoregressive generative world model post-trained from Cosmos on 21k hours
  of driving. | license: AlpaSim is Apache-2.0. Alpamayo inference code is Apache-2.0 and weights are OpenMDW-1.1 (the GitHub README says commercial use is permitted; an older HF card said non-commercial, so verify). The PhysicalAI-AV dataset uses NVIDIA's AV Dataset License (internal AV
  development). | strengths: Complete open reference stack for training and evaluating end-to-end driving policies; Pluggable driver interface (Alpamayo, Transfuser, VaVAM) and evaluation module; Generative closed loop covers events that reconstruction cannot (weather, novel agents) | weaknesses:
  Very new (2026); APIs churning; the alpamayo repo is already 'no longer under active development'; Requires a large GPU fleet; Generative closed-loop results are not yet accepted as homologation evidence | best fit: AICHEMIST's 'model training' feature for driving policies: wrap AlpaSim/AlpaGym as
  a managed service in the GPU cloud workspace | sources: https://github.com/NVlabs/alpasim https://github.com/NVlabs/alpamayo https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/omnidreams-blog/ https://www.automotiveworld.com/news/nvidia-launches-alpamayo-2-super-open-reasoning-model/
- **NVIDIA Isaac Sim 5.1 / 6.0 (+ Isaac Lab, Newton)** [General robotics simulator (OpenUSD/PhysX/Newton); base for drone, UGV and marine extensions] | status: Isaac Sim 6.0 GA on 8 Jun 2026, with expanded Newton physics backend, URDF/MJCF Importer 3.0, an Isaac Sim MCP server and agent skills, and
  teleoperation. Isaac Lab 3.0 is in early access. Isaac Sim source on GitHub is Apache-2.0. | license: Source is Apache-2.0. Kit SDK and assets are under NVIDIA terms: free for internal R&D, but redistribution or service delivery to third parties needs NVIDIA AI Enterprise. | strengths: OpenUSD
  scene core shared with the AV stack; RTX sensors; massive-parallel RL via Isaac Lab; Hosts Pegasus (drones), OceanSim/MarineGym (underwater) and custom vehicles; MCP and agent tooling fits AICHEMIST's LLM-command interface | weaknesses: SaaS licensing cost; Vehicle-dynamics fidelity below
  CarSim/Chrono without co-simulation; Fast API churn between major versions | best fit: Unified scene and runtime core for AICHEMIST across robots, drones, marine and custom vehicles | sources: https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/isaac-sim-6-0-general-availability/372621
  https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/6.0.0/overview/release_notes.html https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/latest/common/license-faq.html
- **Project Chrono (Chrono::Vehicle, SCM, CRM, Chrono::Sensor)** [Open-source multibody, vehicle-dynamics and terramechanics engine] | status: Chrono 10.0.0 was released ~27 Mar 2026 (GitHub tag dated 7 Apr): refactored Chrono::FSI with SPH and TDPF solvers, a new Peridynamics module, new camera-
  sensor features, checkpointing for Chrono and Chrono::Vehicle, VSG visualization plugins, modern CMake. Chrono::CRM (SPH, GPU) simulates up to 29 km of deformable terrain on one H100 at SCM-comparable speed (SBEL, Oct 2025). Maintained by UW-Madison SBEL. | license: BSD-3-Clause; free. |
  strengths: Templated wheeled and tracked vehicles; Pac89/Pac02, TMeasy, Fiala, rigid and FEA/ANCF tires; SCM, CRM/SPH and DEM deformable terrain, which is unique for off-road, defense and planetary use; Permissive license allows embedding in a commercial SaaS | weaknesses: Academic UX; rendering
  and sensors weak compared with RTX engines; No native FTire or MF 6.2 licensing; HIL real-time requires tuning; Small core team | best fit: Physics back-end for off-road UGV, construction/mining and tracked vehicles, co-simulated with an Omniverse renderer | sources:
  https://projectchrono.org/news/ https://github.com/projectchrono/chrono/releases/tag/10.0.0 https://api.projectchrono.org/wheeled_tire.html https://sbel.wisc.edu/2025/10/04/sbel-researchers-develop-chronocrm-simulator-for-fast-and-scalable-rover-terrain-interaction/
- **BeamNG.tech** [Soft-body vehicle physics simulator for ADAS, testing and research] | status: v0.39 '2026 Summer Release': native C++ ROS 2 integration, MCP support for LLM agents, rebuilt ACC/platooning, Traffic Signals API, Vehicle Signal Logger. BeamNGpy 1.36. Physics runs at 2,000 Hz. |
  license: BeamNG.tech is free for academic use on request; commercial license by quote (licensing@beamng.gmbh). BeamNGpy is MIT. | strengths: Unique node-beam soft-body physics for crashes, deformation and damage; Strong in software-testing research (ICST/SBFT tool competitions); MCP hook aligns
  with LLM-controlled scenes | weaknesses: Proprietary closed engine; commercial pricing opaque; Sensor physics basic (ideal sensors plus optional noise); Not an OEM homologation tool | best fit: Partner or plug-in for crash, damage and edge-case datasets; not a core engine | sources:
  https://beamng.tech/blog/beamng-tech-039/ https://listmonk.beamng.com/archive/2026-tech-fourth-newsletter-6236 https://github.com/BeamNG/BeamNGpy https://documentation.beamng.com/beamng_tech/
- **Applied Intuition (Simian/ADP, Sensor Sim, Neural Sim, CarSim/TruckSim/BikeSim)** [Commercial end-to-end AV toolchain and vehicle dynamics] | status: Acquired Mechanical Simulation (CarSim) on 14 Mar 2022, EpiSci in Feb 2025 (defense; launched Axion) and Reblika in Jul 2025 (digital humans).
  Series F of $600M at a $15B valuation (Jun 2025). Sacra estimates ~$830M ARR in 2025, 2x year on year. NVIDIA collaboration on L2+ and Cosmos (GTC Mar 2026). | license: Proprietary enterprise licenses, price on request (CarSim seats historically sold separately; unverified). | strengths:
  CarSim/TruckSim are the HIL vehicle-dynamics reference used by 200+ OEMs and Tier-1s; Full stack: scenarios, sensor sim, neural sim, validation, data engine, defense; Huge revenue and sales reach | weaknesses: Expensive and enterprise-only; not open; Direct competitor to any generic AV-sim startup
  | best fit: Integrate via FMI/co-sim adapters for customers who already own CarSim; avoid competing head-on | sources: https://www.appliedintuition.com/news/mechanical-simulation-corporation https://sacra.com/research/applied-intuition-at-830m-year/ https://www.cimdata.com/en/industry-summary-
  articles/item/26787-applied-intuition-acquires-episci-strengthening-position-as-leader-in-all-domain-autonomy-software-for-national-security https://finder.techleap.nl/news/feed/applied-intuition-raises-600m-at-15b-valuation
- **IPG Automotive CarMaker / TruckerMaker 15.0** [Commercial vehicle-dynamics and ADAS virtual test driving (MIL/SIL/HIL/VIL)] | status: CarMaker 15.0 released Nov 2025: virtual ECU integration with Synopsys Silver (SVB), ground-truth sensor data with glare and rain, improved junction and traffic
  models for endurance testing; Euro NCAP 2026 virtual-test webinars and packages. | license: Proprietary seat and node licenses, price on request. | strengths: Industry-standard real-time vehicle models and HIL integration; Strong Euro NCAP scenario coverage; Korean MOLIT VIL reference project |
  weaknesses: Visual and sensor realism behind RTX/UE engines; Closed, expensive, not cloud-native ML-training oriented | best fit: Interop target (OSI/FMI/OpenSCENARIO export) rather than competitor | sources: https://www.ipg-automotive.com/press/press-detail/ipg-automotive-sets-new-standards-in-
  virtual-vehicle-development-with-carmaker-150 https://www.vehicledynamicsinternational.com/news/simulation/ipg-automotive-releases-carmaker-15-0-for-virtual-vehicle-development.html https://ipg-automotive.com/fileadmin/data/applications/autonmous_vehicles/references/MOLIT_Autonomous_Vehicle-in-
  the-Loop.pdf
- **dSPACE AURELION (+ SIMPHERA, ModelDesk, HIL)** [Commercial physics-based sensor simulation integrated with HIL] | status: AURELION 25.x line. Migration to Unreal Engine 5 announced, with a UE plugin for OpenDRIVE import. OMNIVISION image-sensor models integrated (Nov 2025). Supports camera,
  radar and lidar in HIL, SIL and cloud. | license: Proprietary; price on request. | strengths: Tight coupling with dSPACE HIL rigs, which dominate OEM labs; Image-sensor-vendor-validated camera models | weaknesses: Closed; UE5 migration timeline unverified; Not oriented to ML training or synthetic-
  data markets | best fit: HIL partner channel for AICHEMIST synthetic sensor data and digital-twin assets | sources: https://www.unrealengine.com/spotlights/dspace-drives-advancements-in-autonomous-vehicle-testing https://www.dspace.com/en/pub/home/news/dspace_pressroom/press/dspace-
  omnivision-2025.cfm
- **Hexagon Virtual Test Drive (VTD) / VTDx** [Commercial OpenX-native scenario and sensor simulation] | status: VTD is widely used and natively supports OpenDRIVE, OpenCRG and OpenSCENARIO. VTDx, a cloud-native SaaS on Hexagon Nexus/Azure with Unreal Engine visuals and consumption-based pricing,
  launched 15 Jan 2025. | license: Proprietary; consumption-based SaaS for VTDx. | strengths: OpenX heritage and reference implementations; Cloud SaaS with pay-per-use | weaknesses: Corporate restructuring risk at Hexagon; less neural-sim innovation | best fit: Benchmark for OpenX compliance and
  consumption pricing models | sources: https://itbrief.co.uk/story/hexagon-launches-cloud-based-test-drive-software-vtdx https://hexagon.com/company/newsroom/press-releases/2025/hexagon-ramps-up-adas-software-innovation-with-cloud-native-quality-test-automation-solution
  https://nexus.hexagon.com/home/product/virtual-test-drive
- **aiSim 5 / aiSim 6 (aiMotive, a Stellantis subsidiary)** [Commercial deterministic sensor simulator with neural rendering] | status: aiSim 5 is re-certified ISO 26262 ASIL-D by TÜV Nord (determinism, physics-based). aiSim 6 adds relightable 'PBR splatting' Gaussians. A hybrid neural plus physics
  renderer was shown at CVPR 2025. aiMotive was acquired by Stellantis (deal announced Nov 2022). | license: Proprietary. | strengths: Only simulator with ASIL-D tool certification; Own engine (not UE), deterministic, with neural reconstruction | weaknesses: Captive to Stellantis strategy; limited
  openness | best fit: Reference for tool-qualification approach; not a likely partner | sources: https://www.aap.com.au/aapreleases/globenewswire1000969676/ https://radiancefields.com/aimotive-brings-relightable-gaussian-splatting-to-aisim-6-with-pbr-splatting https://aimotive.com/w/aimotive-

- **rFpro AV elevate** [Commercial high-fidelity ray-traced sensor simulation and digital twins] | status: Multi-path ray tracing for camera, lidar and radar (motion blur, rolling shutter); Sony image-sensor models; 180+ real-world digital twins with road surfaces accurate to 1 mm; HPC/cloud
  Simulation Manager. Owned by AB Dynamics. | license: Proprietary. | strengths: Engineering-grade sensor realism and survey-grade tracks | weaknesses: Costly; narrow OEM focus | best fit: Benchmark for sensor-fidelity claims; possible content partner | sources:

- **Ansys AVxcelerate Sensors 2026 R1 (Synopsys)** [Commercial GPU physics-based camera, radar, lidar and thermal simulation] | status: 2026 R1 integrates NVIDIA Omniverse: multispectral Light Propagation Engine (8-Mpix imagers), physics-based radar, NCAP 2026 virtual-test module (2,000+ auto-
  generated test combinations, 1,200+ scenarios). | license: Proprietary. | strengths: Multiphysics material models (SPEOS, HFSS heritage); Omniverse interop | weaknesses: Expensive; enterprise sales cycle | best fit: Sensor-model plug-in for premium customers via Omniverse | sources:

- **Foretellix Foretify** [Scenario-based V&V and coverage platform (OpenSCENARIO DSL)] | status: Co-author of OpenSCENARIO 2.x/DSL. Integrated with the Omniverse AV Blueprint and Cosmos Transfer; NVIDIA DRIVE AV integration (Oct 2025); Parallel Domain partnership (Nov 2025); MathWorks/Mazda (Feb
  2025). Raised $135M in total (Series C closed Dec 2023, NVIDIA among investors). Cut 29 staff in Jan 2026. | license: Proprietary. | strengths: Coverage-driven verification and the DSL abstraction layer; strong safety-case story | weaknesses: Relies on third-party simulators; financial pressure
  signals | best fit: Model for AICHEMIST's LLM-to-OpenSCENARIO-DSL scenario generator; potential partner | sources: https://www.foretellix.com/foretellix-raises-85-million-in-series-c-closing/ https://www.autonomousvehicleinternational.com/news/testing/foretellix-integrates-foretify-data-

  Germany. Chosen by KATRI as the virtual evaluation platform in the KATRI–Mcity agreement (May 2023). Expanding into defense (Korea Military Academy MOU) and unmanned systems. Hiring Omniverse developers. No 2025–26 funding round verified. | license: Proprietary. | strengths: Domestic incumbent;
  automatic HD map to digital twin; government relationships | weaknesses: Unreal-based classical sim; limited neural or world-model capability publicly | best fit: Main Korean competitor, or a partner for HD-map content; differentiate on neural, synthetic-data and marine work | sources:

  strengths: Proof that generative camera+lidar simulation is production-relevant | weaknesses: Not available | best fit: Market signal; sets the bar for neural sensor sim | sources: https://waymo.com/blog/2026/02/the-waymo-world-model-a-new-frontier-for-autonomous-driving-simulation https://the-

  only. | strengths: Fleet-scale data moat | weaknesses: Not available | best fit: Market signal | sources: https://www.humanoidsdaily.com/news/tesla-ai-chief-details-unified-world-simulator-for-fsd-and-optimus

  apply). | strengths: Photoreal RTX sensors plus PX4/ROS 2 SITL in the same USD world as the rest of the platform | weaknesses: Single-maintainer (PhD) project; lags Isaac versions | best fit: Drone module of the AICHEMIST platform (fork or extend) | sources:

  bridge. IAMAI sells enterprise support. | license: MIT; free (UE EULA applies). | strengths: Fixed-wing (JSBSim) plus multirotor plus UGV; permissive license | weaknesses: Small vendor; UE seat-fee exposure | best fit: Alternative drone back-end, especially fixed-wing and UAM | sources:

  paper). | license: BSD-3-Clause. | strengths: State-based policies train in under a minute; vision navigation in under an hour | weaknesses: Depends on deprecated Isaac Gym until the port lands | best fit: Drone RL training back-end in AICHEMIST's 'model training' feature | sources:

  Do not adopt | sources: https://github.com/uzh-rpg/flightmare

  SIH. ArduPilot uses the ardupilot_gazebo plugin. | license: Apache-2.0 (Gazebo); BSD-3 (PX4); GPL-3 (ArduPilot). | strengths: Reference SITL path for flight-stack certification workflows; huge community; VRX/DAVE marine built on it | weaknesses: Low visual realism; not suited to perception
  synthetic data | best fit: Flight-control SITL and CI layer beneath the photoreal layer | sources: https://discourse.openrobotics.org/t/ros-news-for-the-week-of-september-29th-2025/50409 https://docs.px4.io/v1.17/en/releases/1.16 https://docs.px4.io/v1.17/en/releases/1.17
  https://ardupilot.org/dev/docs/sitl-with-gazebo.html
- **Stonefish** [Open-source marine robotics simulator (C++)] | status: Active single-author project (P. Cieślak). Bullet-based with geometry-based hydrodynamics, OpenGL 4.3 renderer with wavelength-dependent underwater light absorption and scattering, sonar/DVL sensors, stonefish_ros. Linux only. |
  license: GPL-3.0. Copyleft is a risk if you distribute; serving it as SaaS is generally not distribution, but obtain legal review. | strengths: Best open hydrodynamics plus underwater optics | weaknesses: GPL; single maintainer; no RTX/USD | best fit: Reference physics for re-implementing marine
  dynamics (clean-room) or for internal research | sources: https://github.com/patrykcieslak/stonefish
- **HoloOcean** [Open-source UE underwater simulator (BYU FRoSt Lab)] | status: pip release 0.5.8 (Apr 2024). HoloOcean 2.0 preview paper (Oct 2025): UE 5.3, Fossen vehicle dynamics, ROS 2 bridge; ray-traced side-scan, FLS and bathymetric sonar plus waves in development; 2.0-dev Docker images; HIL
  use demonstrated (Nov 2025). | license: Open source (MIT believed; not verified). UE EULA applies. | strengths: Strong sonar and acoustic-comms modelling | weaknesses: 2.0 not formally released; UE dependency | best fit: Sonar and acoustic reference; candidate integration for AUV customers |
  sources: https://arxiv.org/html/2510.06160v1 https://holoocean.readthedocs.io/en/latest/changelog/changelog.html https://arxiv.org/html/2511.07687v1
- **DAVE and VRX (Gazebo maritime)** [Open-source underwater (DAVE) and surface-vessel (VRX) simulators] | status: DAVE: Apache-2.0, Gazebo based, ROS-2 migration status unverified. VRX 2.0 runs on Gazebo Sim plus ROS 2 (ONR/RoboNation competition). | license: Apache-2.0. | strengths: USV
  competition ecosystem; permissive license | weaknesses: Low visual realism | best fit: Control and SITL layer for USV/AUV customers | sources: https://github.com/Field-Robotics-Lab/dave https://github.com/osrf/vrx
- **OceanSim / MarineGym (Isaac Sim based)** [GPU underwater perception and RL simulation] | status: OceanSim (2025, to be open-sourced): RTX ray-traced imaging sonar, underwater camera models, fast synthetic data.   (license unverified); MarineGym closed. | strengths: Same Isaac/USD stack as the rest of the platform | weaknesses: Research-grade | best fit: Starting point for AICHEMIST's marine perception synthetic-data module | sources: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.01074
- **Remcom WaveFarer / RadaRays (radar physics)** [Specialized radar simulation] | status: WaveFarer: commercial near-field ray tracing with multipath, diffuse scattering and micro-Doppler. RadaRays: open-source FMCW radar ray tracing with a Gazebo plugin. | license: WaveFarer proprietary; RadaRays
  open source (license unverified). | strengths: Radar realism beyond ray-cast approximations | weaknesses: Slow (WaveFarer); research code (RadaRays) | best fit: Radar-fidelity add-on for automotive and maritime radar datasets | sources: https://www.remcom.com/articles-and-papers/auto-radar-drive-
  scenario-simulation-increasing-realism-with-multipath-diffuse-scattering-and-micro-doppler https://github.com/uos/radarays
- **Korean ship digital twins (HD Hyundai / Avikus; Samsung Heavy SAS)** [Market and customer reference (marine)] | status: HD Hyundai: Blackwell-GPU Omniverse plus Siemens digital twin at the Samho shipyard, Palantir partnership, cited by Jensen Huang at CES 2026; 'Future of Shipyard' aims for 30%
  shorter production time by 2030; Avikus HiNAS on ~350 vessels, HiNAS Control on 40 HMM ships. Samsung Heavy: SAS autonomous navigation steered a 15,000 TEU Evergreen ship ~10,000 km Oakland to Kaohsiung (25 Aug–6 Sep 2025) without crew intervention; NAPA voyage-optimization digital twin. |
  license: N/A (potential customers). | strengths: Deep-pocketed domestic buyers; Autonomous Ship Act in force since 3 Jan 2025 needs simulation-based performance verification | weaknesses: Large incumbents build in-house with NVIDIA/Siemens; long sales cycles | best fit: Primary target customers
  for AICHEMIST's marine and port synthetic-data and digital-twin niche | sources: https://www.hellenicshippingnews.com/hd-hyundai-drives-koreas-shipbuilding-future-with-digital-twins-autonomy-ai-robots/ https://www.koreatimes.co.kr/business/companies/20250228/hd-hyundai-collaborates-with-palantir-
  siemens-for-ai-based-shipbuilding https://thedigitalship.com/news/electronics-navigation/samsung-ai-ship-crosses-pacific-on-its-own/ https://www.newsseoul.co.kr/news/view/1065579620501557
## Key facts
- [high] CARLA 0.10.0 (19 Dec 2024) migrated to UE 5.5 with Lumen/Nanite, swapped PhysX for Chaos, and added native ROS 2 and InvertedAI traffic. No 0.10.x follow-up tag exists as of Oct 2026, but the ue5-dev branch was pushed 2026-10-02. (https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla/releases ;
  https://carla.org/2024/12/19/release-0.10.0)
- [high] CARLA 0.9.16 (16 Sep 2025) integrated NVIDIA Cosmos Transfer1, NuRec neural reconstruction, a SimReady OpenUSD/MDL converter, native ROS 2 and left-hand traffic. (https://carla.org/2025/09/16/release-0.9.16)
- [high] CARLA code is MIT and assets are CC-BY. The UE5 build needs an RTX 3070-class GPU with 16 GB+ VRAM and 32 GB+ RAM. (https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla)
- [medium] NVIDIA DRIVE Sim was never made generally available. AV simulation is now delivered via the Omniverse Blueprint for AV simulation, Sensor RTX APIs (early access via partners such as Foretellix and Mcity), NuRec and Cosmos. (https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/drive-sim-access-or-
  omniverse-truck-sim/289147 ; https://blogs.nvidia.com/?p=76844)
- [medium] Omniverse NuRec was declared generally available at GTC 2026 via NGC. Its 3DGUT Gaussians support fisheye and rolling-shutter cameras. (https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-omniverse-nurec-reaches-general-availability)
- [medium] Instant NuRec (Jul 2026) reconstructs a 10–20 s multi-camera driving clip into a simulatable 3DGS world in ~1.5 s, versus hours of per-scene optimization, and is compatible with AlpaSim. (https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2607.14203)
- [high] Cosmos 3 (announced 1 Jun 2026 at GTC Taipei) comes in Nano 16B, Super 64B and Edge 4B variants and is released under OpenMDW-1.1. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos ; https://www.axios.com/2026/06/01/nvidia-ai-push-cosmos-3-world-model)
- [high] Cosmos-Predict2.5 and Transfer2.5 were released 6 Oct 2025; their GitHub code is Apache-2.0. Earlier Cosmos weights use the NVIDIA Open Model License (commercial use allowed, attribution required, guardrail clause). (https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos ; https://www.nvidia.com/en-
  us/agreements/enterprise-software/nvidia-open-model-license)
- [high] AlpaSim is Apache-2.0, built from gRPC microservices, and supports NuRec or OmniDreams rendering plus Alpamayo, Transfuser and VaVAM drivers. Alpamayo weights are OpenMDW-1.1; Alpamayo 1.5 came out Mar 2026 and Alpamayo 2 Super (32B) on 1 Jun 2026. (https://github.com/NVlabs/alpasim ;
  https://github.com/NVlabs/alpamayo ; https://www.automotiveworld.com/news/nvidia-launches-alpamayo-2-super-open-reasoning-model/)
- [medium] OmniDreams is a real-time, action-conditioned, autoregressive multi-view generative world model post-trained from Cosmos on 21k hours of driving and used closed-loop with AlpaSim. AlpaGym is open-source closed-loop RL on AlpaSim. (https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/omnidreams-
  blog/ ; https://www.autonomousvehicleinternational.com/news/software/nvidia-launches-alpamayo-2-super-for-reasoning-based-autonomous-driving.html)
- [medium] Project Chrono 10.0.0 (~27 Mar 2026) adds checkpointing, a refactored FSI (SPH and TDPF), Peridynamics and new camera-sensor features. Chrono::Vehicle tire models include Pac89/Pac02, TMeasy, Fiala and FEA/ANCF. (https://projectchrono.org/news/ ;
  https://api.projectchrono.org/wheeled_tire.html)
- [high] Chrono::CRM (GPU SPH terrain) handles up to 29 km of terrain on a single H100 at performance comparable to SCM. (https://sbel.wisc.edu/2025/10/04/sbel-researchers-develop-chronocrm-simulator-for-fast-and-scalable-rover-terrain-interaction/)
- [high] Applied Intuition acquired Mechanical Simulation (CarSim, TruckSim, BikeSim) on 14 Mar 2022 and EpiSci in Feb 2025. It raised $600M at a $15B valuation in Jun 2025. (https://www.appliedintuition.com/news/mechanical-simulation-corporation ; https://finder.techleap.nl/news/feed/applied-
  intuition-raises-600m-at-15b-valuation)
- [high] BeamNG.tech v0.39 (2026 summer release) adds native C++ ROS 2 and MCP support; physics runs at 2,000 Hz. It is free for academia, with commercial licenses by quote. (https://beamng.tech/blog/beamng-tech-039/ ; https://github.com/BeamNG/BeamNGpy)
- [high] aiSim 5 holds an ISO 26262:2018 ASIL-D certificate (TÜV Nord) for its development process and determinism. aiSim 6 adds relightable PBR Gaussian splatting. (https://www.aap.com.au/aapreleases/globenewswire1000969676/ ; https://radiancefields.com/aimotive-brings-relightable-gaussian-
  splatting-to-aisim-6-with-pbr-splatting)
- [medium] Current ASAM versions: OpenSCENARIO DSL 2.1.0 (Mar 2024), OpenSCENARIO XML 1.3.0, OpenDRIVE 1.8.0, OSI 3.7.0 (3 Jul 2024), OpenLABEL 1.0.0 (Nov 2021), OpenMATERIAL 3D 1.0.0 (3 Apr 2025; 1.1.0 targeted Oct 2026). (https://openscenario.asam.net/ASAM_OpenSCENARIO_DSL/latest/introduction.html
  ; https://www.asam.net/standards/detail/openmaterial/ ; https://www.asam.net/standards/detail/openlabel/)
- [medium] The UN ADS regulation was adopted by GRVA (19–23 Jan 2026) and approved by WP.29 in late June 2026. It requires a safety management system, a safety case, and credibility of validation methods including virtual-testing toolchains. (https://news.un.org/en/story/2026/06/1167797 ;
  https://www.globalpolicywatch.com/2026/05/un-regulation-and-gtr-on-automated-driving-systems-current-state-of-play/)
- [high] ISO 34505:2025 (scenario evaluation and test-case generation for L3+ ADS) was published 11 Jun 2025. ISO/PAS 8800:2024 covers AI safety in road vehicles. ISO 26262 Ed. 3 is expected around 2027 with ML alignment. UL 4600 Ed. 3 was published 17 Mar 2023 and adds autonomous trucking.
  (https://www.iso.org/standard/78954.html ; https://www.iso.org/standard/83303.html ; https://www.kuglermaag.com/news/iso-26262-v3/ ; https://www.ul.com/news/ul-4600-edition-3-updates-incorporate-autonomous-trucking)
- [medium] Euro NCAP's 2026 protocols restructure ratings into four stages (safe driving, crash avoidance, crash protection, post-crash), each scored out of 100, with expanded crash-avoidance scenario variants and simulation-supported assessment. (https://thebrakereport.com/euro-ncap-unveils-
  major-2026-safety-testing-protocols/ ; https://www.ansys.com/resource-center/webinar/leveraging-virtual-testing-for-ncap-2026-crash-avoidance-and-beyond)
- [medium] Korea's amended Autonomous Vehicle Act creates a Level-4 performance-certification route (in force 20 Mar 2025). MOLIT opened the Hwaseong AI Autonomous Driving Hub on 20 Mar 2026. Korea's Autonomous Ship Act took effect 3 Jan 2025. (https://www.newsseoul.co.kr/news/view/1065579017176859
  ; https://news.mtn.co.kr/news-detail/2026031917191428069 ; https://www.newsseoul.co.kr/news/view/1065579620501557)
- [medium] K-City (KATRI, Hwaseong) covers 360,000 m² with 5G and a weather facility. Its virtual twin agreed with real driving 92.5% of the time within a 0.288 m error band. (https://dgist.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/ ; https://www.dt.co.kr/article/11034257)
- [medium] Offering Isaac Sim or Omniverse Kit to third parties as a service or redistributed app requires an NVIDIA AI Enterprise license, priced at $4,500/GPU/yr list and $1,125 for Inception startups at 75% off. (https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/latest/common/license-faq.html ;
  https://pi3g.com/nvidia-ai-enterprise-subscription-cost-in-2026/)
- [high] Unreal Engine charges non-game companies with more than $1M annual revenue $1,850 per seat per year (since UE 5.4, Apr 2024). (https://www.unrealengine.com/en-US/blog/we-are-updating-unreal-engine-twinmotion-and-realitycapture-pricing-in-late-april)
- [high] Waymax and the Waymo Open Dataset are licensed for non-commercial use only. (https://github.com/waymo-research/waymax ; https://waymo.com/open/about)
- [high] Pegasus Simulator v5.1.0 (26 Oct 2025) targets Isaac Sim 5.1 and PX4 1.14.3 (ArduPilot experimental) under BSD-3. (https://github.com/PegasusSimulator/PegasusSimulator)
- [high] Project AirSim is MIT-licensed, maintained by IAMAI with enterprise support, and supports PX4/ArduPilot and JSBSim fixed-wing. (https://github.com/iamaisim/ProjectAirSim)
- [high] Gazebo Jetty (LTS, Sep 2025) is supported until May 2031. PX4 1.16 uses Gazebo Harmonic and PX4 1.17 adds Jetty support. (https://discourse.openrobotics.org/t/ros-news-for-the-week-of-september-29th-2025/50409 ; https://docs.px4.io/v1.17/en/releases/1.17)
- [high] Stonefish is GPL-3.0 (Bullet plus OpenGL 4.3, geometry-based hydrodynamics). HoloOcean 2.0 (UE 5.3, Fossen dynamics, ROS 2) is a preview; the latest pip release is 0.5.8 (Apr 2024). (https://github.com/patrykcieslak/stonefish ; https://arxiv.org/html/2510.06160v1 ;
  https://holoocean.readthedocs.io/en/latest/changelog/changelog.html)
- [high] The Waymo World Model (6 Feb 2026), built on DeepMind Genie 3, generates both camera and lidar and is controllable via actions, layout and text. (https://waymo.com/blog/2026/02/the-waymo-world-model-a-new-frontier-for-autonomous-driving-simulation)
- [medium] Wayve GAIA-3 (2 Dec 2025) is a 15B-parameter model used as an offline evaluation pipeline. GAIA-2 (26 Mar 2025) is 8.4B and covers up to 5 cameras. (https://www.automotiveworld.com/news/wayve-unveils-gaia-3-for-autonomous-driving-tests/)
- [medium] Isaac Sim 6.0 reached GA on 8 Jun 2026 with Newton physics support, an MCP server and agent skills, and Isaac Lab 3.0 EA. (https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/isaac-sim-6-0-general-availability/372621)
- [medium] FTire models tire dynamics up to ~100 Hz including short-wavelength obstacles. MF-Swift (rigid ring) is valid to ~60–100 Hz. Magic Formula 6.2 is the steady-state empirical standard. (https://functionbay.com/documentation/onlinehelp/Documents/mfswift.htm ;
  https://www.mathworks.com/help/vdynblks/ref/combinedslipwheelcpi.html)
## Quantitative data
- Applied Intuition valuation / Series F: $15B / $600M (Jun 2025) (https://finder.techleap.nl/news/feed/applied-intuition-raises-600m-at-15b-valuation)
- Applied Intuition ARR (estimate): ~$830M in 2025 (from ~$415M in 2024) (https://sacra.com/research/applied-intuition-at-830m-year/)
- Waabi Series C: $750M, plus up to $250M from Uber tied to a 25,000-robotaxi target (https://www.electrive.com/2026/02/02/autonomy-startup-waabi-secures-750-million-and-partners-with-uber/)
- Foretellix total funding / layoffs: $135M total (Series C closed Dec 2023); 29 staff cut Jan 2026 (https://www.foretellix.com/foretellix-raises-85-million-in-series-c-closing/)
- MORAI funding / customers: Series B KRW 25B (~$20.8M, Feb 2022); ~$24.9M total; 100+ customers (https://www.therobotreport.com/morai-raises-208m-autonomous-driving-simulation/)
- Cosmos 3 model sizes: Nano 16B (RTX PRO 6000/H100), Super 64B (H200/B200), Edge 4B (Jetson AGX Orin) (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos)
- Cosmos-Predict2.5 sizes: 2B and 14B (released 6 Oct 2025) (https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos/cosmos-predict2.5)
- Cosmos-Drive-Dreams released dataset / training data: 81,802 synthetic clips with HDMap and LiDAR; RDS ~3.6M 20-s 6-view clips (~20,000 h) (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.09042)
- Alpamayo model sizes: Alpamayo 1 / 1.5: 10B; Alpamayo 2 Super: 32B (https://github.com/NVlabs/alpamayo ; https://www.automotiveworld.com/news/nvidia-launches-alpamayo-2-super-open-reasoning-model/)
- AlpaSim bundled scenes / NVIDIA AV dataset: ~900 reconstructed scenes; 1,700+ hours of driving data (press reports) (https://techcrunch.com/?p=3079883)
- OmniDreams training data: 21k hours of driving scenarios (https://www.emergentmind.com/papers/2606.03159)
- Instant NuRec reconstruction time: ~1.5 s for a 10–20 s multi-camera clip (vs hours of per-scene optimization) (https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2607.14203)
- NVIDIA Physical AI dataset (Cosmos clips): 40,000 Cosmos-generated clips (https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/accelerating-av-simulation-with-neural-reconstruction-and-world-foundation-models/)
- Wayve GAIA-3 / GAIA-2 parameters: 15B (Dec 2025) / 8.4B (Mar 2025) (https://www.automotiveworld.com/news/wayve-unveils-gaia-3-for-autonomous-driving-tests/)
- Waymo autonomous miles (Feb 2026): ~200M fully autonomous miles; billions of simulated miles (https://waymo.com/blog/2026/02/the-waymo-world-model-a-new-frontier-for-autonomous-driving-simulation)
- Tesla world-simulator claim: '500 years of human driving experience per day' (https://www.humanoidsdaily.com/news/tesla-ai-chief-details-unified-world-simulator-for-fsd-and-optimus)
- Chrono::CRM scale: 29 km of deformable terrain on 1x H100 (https://sbel.wisc.edu/2025/10/04/sbel-researchers-develop-chronocrm-simulator-for-fast-and-scalable-rover-terrain-interaction/)
- BeamNG physics rate: 2,000 Hz (https://documentation.beamng.com/beamng_tech/)
- rFpro digital-twin library: 180+ real-world locations; road surface accurate to 1 mm (https://www.eenewseurope.com/en/rfpro-launches-simulator-for-autonomous-vehicle-development/)
- Ansys AVxcelerate NCAP 2026 module: 2,000+ auto-generated test combinations; 1,200+ scenarios; 8-Mpix imager support (https://www.ansys.com/resource-center/webinar/leveraging-virtual-testing-for-ncap-2026-crash-avoidance-and-beyond)
- CARLA GitHub adoption: ~14.5k stars, ~4.7k forks; CARLA cited as used by 150,000+ developers (https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla ; https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-omniverse-nurec-reaches-general-availability)
- CARLA UE5 minimum hardware: RTX 3070+ with 16 GB+ VRAM, 32 GB+ RAM, 9th-gen i7 / Ryzen 7+ (https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla)
- K-City size / virtual-real agreement: 360,000 m²; 92.5% agreement within 0.288 m (https://www.dt.co.kr/article/11034257 ; https://dgist.elsevierpure.com/)
- NVIDIA AI Enterprise price: $4,500/GPU/yr list; $18,000 for 5 years; $1,125/GPU/yr with Inception 75% discount; NVIDIA Enterprise bundle ~EUR 5,800/GPU/yr (https://pi3g.com/nvidia-ai-enterprise-subscription-cost-in-2026/)
- Unreal Engine non-game seat fee: $1,850/seat/yr for companies over $1M revenue (https://www.unrealengine.com/en-US/blog/we-are-updating-unreal-engine-twinmotion-and-realitycapture-pricing-in-late-april)
- Gazebo Jetty LTS support window: Sep 2025 to May 2031 (https://discourse.openrobotics.org/t/ros-news-for-the-week-of-september-29th-2025/50409)
- Avikus deployments: ~350 vessels; HiNAS Control on 40 HMM ships, 100+ large retrofits (https://www.hellenicshippingnews.com/hd-hyundai-drives-koreas-shipbuilding-future-with-digital-twins-autonomy-ai-robots/)
- Samsung Heavy SAS autonomous voyage: 15,000 TEU ship, ~10,000 km, 25 Aug–6 Sep 2025, no crew intervention (https://thedigitalship.com/news/electronics-navigation/samsung-ai-ship-crosses-pacific-on-its-own/)
- HD Hyundai shipyard target: 30% production-time reduction by 2030 (Future of Shipyard) (https://www.koreatimes.co.kr/business/companies/20250228/hd-hyundai-collaborates-with-palantir-siemens-for-ai-based-shipbuilding)
- Tire-model frequency validity: FTire up to ~100 Hz; MF-Swift ~60–100 Hz (https://functionbay.com/documentation/onlinehelp/Documents/mfswift.htm)
- Euro NCAP 2026 rating structure: 4 stages x 100 points each (https://thebrakereport.com/euro-ncap-unveils-major-2026-safety-testing-protocols/)
- Proposed AICHEMIST vehicle/drone/marine vertical team (estimate): 12–18 FTE by month 12; ~KRW 1.8–2.5B/yr personnel plus ~$0.5–1.0M/yr GPU (L40S/RTX PRO 6000 for RTX sensors, burst H100/H200 for Cosmos fine-tuning). Low-confidence planning estimate. (Analyst estimate (no external source))


# TOPIC: realism
## Options
- **NVIDIA Omniverse RTX Renderer inside Isaac Sim 6.x (RTX Real-Time 2.0 + Interactive Path Tracing)** [Simulation renderer (primary)] | status: Isaac Sim 6.0 GA ~June 2026 (radiancefields report: June 8, 2026; GitHub tag v6.0.0 June 4), 6.0.1 (June 22), 6.1.0 GA (Sep 10, 2026), 7.0.0a1 pre-release
  (Sep 18, 2026). The fetch tool printed some GitHub tag years inconsistently, but the sequence matches 2026. RTX Real-Time 2.0 is a path-tracing-based real-time mode using the DLSS suite; Interactive (Path Tracing) mode is available for higher fidelity. Isaac Sim 6.0 adds 3DGS rendering via Fabric
  Scene Delegate, multi-GPU, light interaction with mesh scenes, and MaterialX. | license: Isaac Sim source on GitHub is Apache-2.0, but Omniverse Kit/RTX runtime requires accepting the NVIDIA Omniverse License Terms on first launch. Free to use; commercial SaaS redistribution should be confirmed
  with NVIDIA. Needs RT-core GPUs: datacenter minimum A40, recommended L40S, best RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server. H100/A100 are not on the list. | strengths: Physically based path tracing with MDL + MaterialX/OpenPBR materials, fully OpenUSD-native; Same scene hosts RTX camera, lidar, radar and
  acoustic/ultrasonic sensors (isaacsim.sensors.experimental.rtx); Native Gaussian-splat (NuRec/3DGUT) rendering composited with meshes, and lighting interaction since 6.0; Direct coupling to Isaac Lab 3.0 (RL/IL), Newton/PhysX physics, Cosmos augmentation, Replicator domain randomization; Largest
  robotics ecosystem; NVIDIA is shipping the Real2Sim tooling (NuRec, 3DGRUT, gsplat 3DGUT) into it | weaknesses: Vendor lock-in to NVIDIA RTX GPUs and the Omniverse license; Heavy (Kit) runtime; Isaac Lab 3.0 adds kit-less OVRTX/Newton renderers partly to work around this; Splats still need proxy
  meshes for physics; lidar/radar on splat content is less mature than on meshes (gsplat added lidar rasterization separately); Fast API churn (e.g., isaacsim.sensors.rtx deprecated for experimental.rtx in 6.0) | best fit: Default rendering + sensor + Real2Sim runtime for robots,
  factories/warehouses, drones and the core of CEN's GPU workspace | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/releases https://docs.omniverse.nvidia.com/materials-and-rendering/latest/rtx-renderer_rt_overview.html https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-s-isaac-
  sim-6.0-ships-with-nurec-gaussian-splatting
- **Isaac Lab 3.0 rendering backends (Isaac RTX, OVRTX kit-less, Newton Warp renderer, Newton GL/RTX) + physically-plausible ISP** [Training-time renderer for RL/IL] | status: Isaac Lab 3.0 beta at GTC (Mar 17, 2026), beta2 (Jun 17, 2026), 3.0.0-EA (Sep 16, 2026). Built on Newton 1.0 + PhysX, with
  kit-less Newton backend (MuJoCo-Warp solvers), pluggable visualizers (Kit, Newton, Rerun, Viser), a renderer-neutral physically plausible ISP (HDR->LDR) and a visuo-tactile sensor (TacSL). | license: Isaac Lab is BSD-3/Apache-style open source. The Isaac RTX backend inherits Omniverse terms.
  cuRobo-based SkillGen has proprietary terms. | strengths: Massively parallel tiled camera rendering for vision-based RL; Choice of cheap raster (Newton Warp) vs full RTX per experiment; ISP model shared across renderers reduces camera-appearance gap | weaknesses: Early-access APIs; TacSL tactile
  works only with the PhysX backend, not Newton; Kit-less renderers are lower fidelity than full RTX | best fit: CEN 'train' tab: vision-based RL/IL at scale with switchable fidelity | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases https://www.robotics247.com/article/nvidia-gtc-2026-nvidia-
