  global-robotics-leaders-look-to-take-physical-ai-to-the-real-world
- **Unreal Engine 5.7 / 5.8 (Lumen, Nanite, MegaLights, Substrate), incl. CARLA** [Game-engine renderer (secondary)] | status: UE 5.7 (Nov 2025): MegaLights Beta, Substrate Production-Ready, Nanite Foliage (Nanite Voxels) Experimental, PCG Production-Ready. UE 5.8 (June 17, 2026): Lumen Lite (~2x
  faster GI), MegaLights/Movie Render Graph production-ready, Mesh Terrain. 5.8 is the last UE5. UE6 early access targeted by late 2027. 3DGS via third-party plugins (YaGS open source, UE 5.5-5.7; NanoGS; Volinga 4DGS relighting). CARLA integrates NuRec. | license: Source-available EULA.
  Royalty/seat model for non-game commercial use. Exact 2026 terms not verified in this session (low confidence). | strengths: Best-in-class real-time artist-driven visuals, huge environment marketplace; Strong for AV/off-road/large open worlds (PCG, Nanite foliage); CARLA ecosystem for driving |
  weaknesses: Not physically based for sensors (raster/Lumen approximations), weak robotics physics/articulation; No native USD-physics/Isaac Lab-grade RL tooling; UE6 transition in 2027 means engine-level rework risk; Splat support only via plugins | best fit: Optional AV/driving and visualization
  front-end when customers already use UE/CARLA. Not the core. | sources: https://www.unrealengine.com/news/unreal-engine-5-7-is-now-available https://gamedev.net/news/3999-unreal-engine-58-released/ https://labs.invenglobal.com/articles/22900/unreal-engine-6-to-feature-fundamental-overhaul-
  targeting-early-access-release-by-late-2027 https://radiancefields.com/yags-yandex-open-sources-a-3dgs-plugin-for-unreal-engine-5.5–5.7
- **Unity 6 HDRP** [Game-engine renderer] | status: Unity's 2026 render-pipeline strategy puts HDRP in maintenance (no new features, stability fixes only) and prioritizes URP. Built-in pipeline deprecation starts in 6.5. A unified renderer is a long-term goal only. Splats via XGRIDS LCC Unity SDK
  2.2.0 (real-time lights + shadows on splats). | license: Unity Pro/Industry subscription (per-seat). | strengths: Large dev community, good XR/mobile deployment; Third-party splat SDKs with lighting | weaknesses: HDRP is frozen, so the photoreal path has no roadmap; Weak robotics physics/sensor
  ecosystem compared with Isaac | best fit: Avoid for new high-fidelity simulation. Acceptable only for XR/mobile viewers. | sources: https://unity.com/topics/render-pipelines-strategy-for-2026 https://80.lv/articles/unity-unveils-2026-render-pipelines-strategy/ https://radiancefields.com/xgrids-
  adds-real-time-lights-and-shadows-to-gaussian-splats-in-lcc-unity-sdk-2.2.0
- **Blender 5.x Cycles / EEVEE (with BlenderProc-style SDG)** [Offline path tracer / asset tool] | status: Blender 5.0 (Nov 2025): ACES 1.3/2.0, null-scattering volumes, thin-film. Blender 5.1 (Mar 11, 2026): up to 10% faster GPU Cycles, HIP RT default on AMD. | license: GPL (free). Rendered outputs
  are unrestricted. | strengths: Ground-truth-quality path tracing that runs on any CUDA/OptiX/HIP GPU, including non-RT H100s via CUDA; Excellent for offline image dataset generation (CEN's current core) and asset cleanup/retopology; Scriptable, huge asset community | weaknesses: Not real-time and
  no robotics physics loop; GPL constraints if code is linked/distributed | best fit: Offline photoreal dataset rendering and the asset-cleanup step in the Real2Sim factory | sources: https://alternativeto.net/news/2025/11/blender-5-0-launches-with-aces-color-enhanced-rendering-and-tighter-vfx-
  integration/ https://gamedev.net/news/2274-blender-51-release/
- **Godot 4.5/4.6/4.7** [Open-source game engine] | status: 4.5 (Sep 2025) and 4.6 (Jan 2026: Jolt physics default, SSR rewrite). 4.7 beta (Apr 2026) adds HDR output and Vulkan ray-tracing work. | license: MIT, free | strengths: Fully permissive, lightweight, embeddable (LibGodot) | weaknesses:
  SDFGI/raster GI far below path-traced realism; No sensor-grade simulation or robotics ecosystem | best fit: Not recommended for realism. Possibly lightweight embedded viewers. | sources: https://digitalproduction.com/2026/01/28/godot-4-6-arrives-with-major-cg-friendly-updates/
  https://www.phoronix.com/search/Godot%204
- **WebGPU web renderers (PlayCanvas engine + SuperSplat 3.0, World Labs Spark 2.x, Babylon.js, CesiumJS)** [Browser rendering / viewer] | status: WebGPU on by default in Chrome, Edge, Firefox (Windows Jul 2025, macOS Nov 2025) and Safari 26 (Sep 2025, incl. iOS/visionOS). SuperSplat 3.0 (June 3,
  2026) is WebGPU-only and streams 24M-Gaussian scans at ~60 fps with LOD. Spark 2.x (World Labs, MIT, WebGL2, LoD paging for 100M+ splat worlds). KHR_gaussian_splatting shipped in Cesium, Babylon.js and PlayCanvas. | license: PlayCanvas engine MIT, Spark MIT. Free. | strengths: Zero-install
  previews for CEN marketplace and browser workspace; Splat streaming at world scale on mobile/VR; Standards-based (glTF KHR_gaussian_splatting, SPZ) | weaknesses: No physically based sensor simulation and no ground-truth labels at sensor fidelity; Browser GPU limits | best fit: CEN marketplace
  asset/scene previews, lightweight annotation/editing, sharing digital-twin views. Heavy simulation stays server-side (Omniverse streaming). | sources: https://app.cinevva.com/news/2026-01-05-webgpu-era https://radiancefields.com/playcanvas-releases-supersplat-3.0
  https://github.com/sparkjsdev/spark https://aecmag.com/reality-capture-modelling/khronos-announces-gltf-gaussian-splatting-extension/
- **gsplat (nerfstudio-project)** [3DGS training/rendering library] | status: v1.6.0 on main (not yet on PyPI). 3DGUT integration (Apr 2025), external distortion + lidar rasterization (Mar 2026), NCore v4 (Apr 2026), fisheye/FTheta/lidar camera models and ~30% faster MCMC (Jun 2026), multi-GPU +
  PPISP (Jul 2026), SH optimizations (Aug 2026). 5.8k stars. | license: Apache-2.0, free | strengths: Commercial-safe replacement for the Inria non-commercial code; Up to 4x less GPU memory and 15% less time than the official implementation; Rolling-shutter/distortion (3DGUT), lidar, batching across
  scenes, 2DGS mode; Used by NVIDIA NuRec as renderer | weaknesses: Library only: needs our own pipeline, UI and QA; No built-in physics/collision geometry | best fit: Core reconstruction engine of the CEN Real2Sim factory | sources: https://github.com/nerfstudio-project/gsplat
  https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/revolutionizing-neural-reconstruction-and-rendering-in-gsplat-with-3dgut/
- **NVIDIA 3DGRUT (3DGRT ray-traced + 3DGUT unscented-transform splats)** [Neural reconstruction / renderer] | status: v1.0.0 (Apr 3, 2025), PPISP (Jan 2026), NCore v4 (Mar 2026), v1.1.0 (Jun 10, 2026: USD/USDZ/PLY/NuRec export with mesh injection, path-traced playground, Blackwell/CUDA 13, DGX
  Spark), v2.0.0 (Jun 2026: Neural Harmonic Textures). 3DGUT was a CVPR 2025 oral; 3DGRT was SIGGRAPH Asia 2024. | license: Apache-2.0, free. Needs RT-core GPU for 3DGRT. | strengths: Handles distorted, fisheye and rolling-shutter cameras, which matters for robots/AVs; Secondary rays
  (reflections/refractions, shadows) via ray tracing; Direct export to Isaac Sim / NuRec USDZ and OpenUSD ParticleField; RTX 5090: 3DGUT 846 FPS (NeRF-Synthetic), 317 FPS (MipNeRF360) | weaknesses: Research-grade tooling; 3DGRT slower (68 FPS on MipNeRF360) | best fit: High-fidelity sensor-correct
  scene reconstruction for robot/AV digital twins | sources: https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut/releases
- **Omniverse NuRec + AlpaSim (+CARLA integration)** [Neural reconstruction in simulation] | status: NuRec is a set of 3DGS libraries that reconstruct camera/lidar data into OpenUSD and render via gsplat inside Isaac Sim, AlpaSim and CARLA (announced Aug 2025). AlpaSim is open source (Apache-2.0,
  Oct 2025), runs as gRPC microservices, uses NuRec 3DGUT as default renderer (plus OmniDreams video-model rendering), ships PhysicalAI-AV-NuRec 26.01 dataset (~900 x 20 s long-tail scenes) and reports up to 83% variance reduction in real-world metrics. | license: AlpaSim Apache-2.0. NuRec
  container/library terms not verified (docs.nvidia.com blocked): low confidence. | strengths: Production path from capture to closed-loop sim; AV-ready (multi-camera, lidar, trajectories); Standardized on OpenUSD ParticleField | weaknesses: Reconstructed scenes are mostly static and limited in
  novel-trajectory extrapolation; Actors/dynamic objects need insertion and relighting; NVIDIA-centric | best fit: Driving digital twins and site-specific robot test environments | sources: https://developer.nvidia.com/omniverse/nurec https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-unveils-alpasim-at-ces
  https://github.com/NVlabs/alpasim https://www.edge-ai-vision.com/2025/08/nvidia-opens-portals-to-world-of-robotics-with-new-omniverse-libraries-cosmos-physical-ai-models-and-ai-computing-infrastructure/
- **fVDB Reality Capture (NVIDIA / OpenVDB)** [Large-scale splat training + mesh extraction] | status: Active (fVDB 0.4 around GTC 2026). Toolbox for sensor loading, radiance-field reconstruction, feature learning, mesh/point-cloud extraction, USD export. | license: Apache-2.0 | strengths: Claims
  ~50% better end-to-end throughput than gsplat and 30% lower runtime; Commercial-safe mesh extraction path; Sparse VDB scales to large sites | weaknesses: Small community (69 stars); Young API | best fit: Factory/warehouse-scale capture and commercial-safe mesh extraction | sources:
  https://github.com/openvdb/fvdb-reality-capture https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-advances-fvdb-with-major-0.4-release-ahead-of-gtc
- **NeRF ecosystem (nerfstudio/nerfacto, Instant-NGP, Zip-NeRF)** [Neural radiance fields] | status: nerfstudio is active (Apache-2.0, 12k stars, splatfacto uses gsplat). Instant-NGP is effectively legacy. Zip-NeRF is high quality but takes seconds per frame. Isaac Sim can still render NeRF USDZ. |
  license: nerfstudio Apache-2.0. Instant-NGP under Nvidia Source Code License-NC (non-commercial; commercial requires NVIDIA research licensing). | strengths: Better extrapolation to views far from training cameras; Compact neural fields, good for offline novel-view data | weaknesses: 100-200x
  slower rendering than 3DGS; not real-time for multi-sensor RL; Not standardized in USD/glTF like splats; ecosystem momentum has moved to 3DGS; Instant-NGP licensing blocks commercial use | best fit: Keep only as an offline fallback. Migrate CEN's NeRF pipeline to 3DGUT/gsplat. | sources:
  https://github.com/nerfstudio-project/nerfstudio https://github.com/NVlabs/instant-ngp https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.06706 https://metavert.io/compare/gaussian-splatting-vs-neural-radiance-fields
- **Splat-to-mesh / surface reconstruction for collision (2DGS, PGSR, SuGaR, MILo, Neuralangelo, AnyGS2Mesh)** [Geometry extraction] | status: 2DGS (SIGGRAPH 2024), PGSR (TVCG; ~1 h on 1 GPU vs NeRF-SDF 8 GPUs x 2 days), MILo (SIGGRAPH Asia 2025, order of magnitude fewer vertices, 250K-14M
  configurable), Neuralangelo (CVPR 2023). Feed-forward GS-to-mesh appearing (AnyGS2Mesh, arXiv 2609.03304). | license: 2DGS and MILo: Gaussian-Splatting License (Inria, research/evaluation only). PGSR: ZJU license, 'educational, research and non-profit purposes only'. Neuralangelo: NVIDIA research
  license (business via licensing form). SuGaR: likely Inria-derived (not verified; low confidence). Commercial-safe alternatives: gsplat 2DGS mode, fVDB, own re-implementation from papers. | strengths: Watertight-ish surfaces suitable for convex decomposition / SDF collision; Planar priors (PGSR)
  good for indoor/industrial | weaknesses: Almost all reference code is non-commercial; Thin structures, transparent/reflective surfaces still fail; Meshes need retopology/simplification before physics | best fit: Re-implement the best ideas (2DGS/PGSR/MILo) on Apache gsplat/fVDB inside CEN |
  sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hbb1/2d-gaussian-splatting/main/LICENSE.md https://raw.githubusercontent.com/zju3dv/PGSR/main/LICENSE.md https://github.com/Anttwo/MILo https://github.com/NVlabs/neuralangelo
- **Physics-aware splats & real-to-sim evaluation (PhysGaussian, PhysTwin, OmniPhysGS, soft-body GS eval, PolaRiS, SplatSim)** [Neural + physics hybrid] | status: PhysGaussian (CVPR 2024 Highlight; MPM via warp-mpm, no meshing). PhysTwin (ICCV 2025; spring-mass + GS from sparse videos; ropes, cloth,
  plush, packages). OmniPhysGS (2025, constitutive Gaussians). Real-to-Sim soft-body GS policy evaluation (arXiv 2511.04665; ACT/DP/pi0/SmolVLA sim-real correlation). PolaRiS (arXiv 2512.16881; short video -> GS eval env with strong real correlation and co-training recipe). SplatSim (86.25% zero-
  shot real success vs 97.5% real-data-trained). | license: Mostly academic code with varying licenses (verify individually; PhysGaussian license not stated on repo page). | strengths: Closes visual gap and deformable-physics gap together; Proven value for policy evaluation (sim success predicts
  real success) | weaknesses: Per-object/scene optimization, not yet general; MPM/spring-mass parameters are object-specific; slow for large-scale RL | best fit: Phase-3 'deformables + evaluation twin' feature; the PolaRiS-style evaluation hub is a strong product template for CEN | sources:
  https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.12198 https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025/html/Jiang_PhysTwin_Physics-Informed_Reconstruction_and_Simulation_of_Deformable_Objects_from_Videos_ICCV_2025_paper.html https://arxiv.org/html/2501.18982v1 https://arxiv.org/html/2511.04665v2
- **Feed-forward 3D foundation models (VGGT, MapAnything, Depth Anything 3, AnySplat)** [Pose/depth/geometry from few images] | status: VGGT (CVPR 2025 Best Paper; commercial checkpoint Jul 29, 2025; VGGT-Omega May 18, 2026). MapAnything (Sep 2025, 3DV 2026; metric multi-modal inputs). Depth
  Anything 3 (Nov 14, 2025; outperforms VGGT on pose/geometry; any-view feed-forward 3DGS). | license: VGGT-1B-Commercial: commercial OK (application form; no military). VGGT-1B: non-commercial. MapAnything: code Apache-2.0; weights 'map-anything-apache' Apache-2.0 vs default CC-BY-NC. DA3:
  Small/Base/Metric-Large/Mono-Large Apache-2.0; Large/Giant/Nested CC-BY-NC-4.0. | strengths: Replaces slow/fragile COLMAP for consumer captures; Metric scale (MapAnything, DA3-Metric), which is essential for physics-correct assets; Seconds instead of hours | weaknesses: Best-quality weights are
  often NC; Accuracy still below calibrated SfM for large sites | best fit: Front end of CEN capture pipeline (phone video -> poses, metric depth -> splat init) | sources: https://github.com/facebookresearch/vggt https://github.com/facebookresearch/map-anything https://github.com/ByteDance-
  Seed/Depth-Anything-3 https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.10647
- **Microsoft TRELLIS / TRELLIS.2-4B** [Open image-to-3D generator] | status: TRELLIS.2-4B released ~Dec 2025: O-Voxel sparse structure + flow-matching transformers, full PBR (base color, roughness, metallic, opacity). H100 timings: 512³ ~3 s, 1024³ ~17 s, 1536³ ~60 s. Needs ≥24 GB GPU. Full
  training code (Objaverse-XL). | license: MIT (code and weights). However, dependencies nvdiffrast (Nvidia Source Code License, 1-way commercial: non-commercial only) and nvdiffrec have separate licenses. These must be replaced or licensed for commercial use. | strengths: Best permissively licensed
  open generator; trainable on our own data; Handles open surfaces/non-manifold and internal structure; PBR output useful for RTX rendering | weaknesses: Outputs are not watertight/simulation-ready and have no scale, mass or articulation; nvdiffrast dependency is a commercial-license trap | best
  fit: Shape/texture completion of occluded parts in the Real2Sim factory and a text/image-to-asset generator in CEN | sources: https://github.com/microsoft/TRELLIS.2 https://github.com/microsoft/TRELLIS https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVlabs/nvdiffrast/main/LICENSE.txt https://comfyui-
  wiki.com/news/2025-12-18-microsoft-trellis2-3d-generation
- **Tencent Hunyuan3D 2.1 (open) / 2.5-3.1 (API)** [Image-to-3D generator] | status: 2.1 open-sourced Jun 13, 2025 with PBR texture; VRAM 10 GB shape, 21 GB texture, 29 GB combined. 2.5/3.0/3.1 Pro are closed (Tencent Cloud API, global English API from Nov 25, 2025; 3.0 = 1536³ geometry). | license:
  Tencent Hunyuan 3D 2.1 Community License. Territory = worldwide EXCLUDING the EU, UK and SOUTH KOREA; use of outputs outside the Territory is unlicensed; plus a >1M MAU approval clause. A Korean company cannot legally use the open weights. API terms for Korea not verified. | strengths: Strong
  quality, good PBR | weaknesses: License explicitly excludes South Korea: disqualifying for AICHEMIST; Newer versions closed | best fit: Do NOT use (benchmark reference only, done by a non-Korean entity if at all) | sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Tencent-
  Hunyuan/Hunyuan3D-2.1/main/LICENSE https://github.com/Tencent-Hunyuan/Hunyuan3D-2.1 https://help.scenario.com/articles/5967392966-hunyuan-3d-models-the-essentials
- **Meta SAM 3D Objects** [Single-image object reconstruction in context] | status: Released Nov 19, 2025 with SAM 3. Predicts geometry, texture and layout/pose of a prompted object (mask/point/box) from one image, outputting Gaussian splats (.ply) and meshes. Encoder weights Jun 1, 2026; 3D Artist
  Object Set + HF leaderboard Jun 2, 2026. Companion SAM 3D Body. | license: SAM License (custom): royalty-free and worldwide, commercial use allowed with restrictions (e.g., military). Medium confidence on full terms. | strengths: Works on cluttered in-the-wild photos with occlusion, outputs object
  pose/layout (scene-consistent); Pairs naturally with SAM 3 segmentation for scene decomposition | weaknesses: Single-view hallucination; scale ambiguous; No physics/articulation | best fit: Object instancing from a single photo of a real scene (Real2Sim of clutter), complementary to multi-view
  splats | sources: https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam-3d-objects https://ai.meta.com/blog/sam-3d/ https://playground.roboflow.com/models/meta/sam-3d-objects
- **Stability SPAR3D** [Fast image-to-3D] | status: Point-cloud-conditioned editing then mesh in ~0.3 s (2025). No notable 2026 update found. | license: Stability AI Community License: free for orgs <$1M annual revenue; enterprise license above that. | strengths: Very fast, editable point cloud
  stage | weaknesses: Lower quality than TRELLIS.2; revenue-capped license | best fit: Low priority; interactive preview only | sources: https://stability.ai/license https://www.fast.io/resources/stability-ai-review-2026.md
- **Commercial 3D generators (Meshy 6, Tripo 3.0, Rodin Gen-2.5)** [SaaS text/image-to-3D] | status: Meshy 6 (Pro $20/mo, Max $60/mo, Enterprise/API ~$90/mo; free outputs CC BY 4.0 non-commercial). Tripo 3.0 (Ultra up to 2M polys; Pro $12/mo; commercial rights all tiers). Rodin Gen-2.5 (Nov 2025;
  ~$0.50-1.50 per model). | license: Paid tiers grant commercial rights; per-asset costs as listed (medium confidence, aggregator sources). | strengths: Fast, polished, game-ready topology | weaknesses: Not sim-ready (no mass/collision/articulation); vendor dependency; competitors to CEN's own
  generator | best fit: Benchmark baselines and optional marketplace import connectors | sources: https://costbench.com/software/ai-3d-generation/meshy/ https://dupple.com/reviews/rodin-ai https://www.krea.ai/blog/best-ai-3d-model-generators-2026
- **World Labs Marble (+ World API)** [Generative 3D worlds] | status: Beta Sep 2025, GA Nov 12, 2025, World API Jan 2026, Marble 1.1 Apr 2026. Outputs Gaussian splats (SPZ/PLY 2M/500K), collider GLB mesh, HQ GLB mesh, 360 panos. Export to Unreal, Unity, Blender, Houdini. | license: Free tier cannot
  export. Standard plan exports splats + collider meshes. Pro plan required for HQ mesh and commercial rights. API from ~$0.12 per Draft world (medium confidence). | strengths: Only commercial generator that outputs splat + collider mesh = near sim-ready environments; Spark renderer (MIT) for web |
  weaknesses: Closed model; physics-agnostic beyond collider; scale/semantics not guaranteed | best fit: Long-tail environment generation for CEN scenes (backgrounds) via API; also a competitive threat | sources: https://docs.worldlabs.ai/marble/export/gaussian-splat.md
  https://invideo.io/blog/world-labs-marble-3d-worlds/ https://github.com/sparkjsdev/spark
- **Sim-ready conversion toolchain (CoACD/CuACD, V-HACD, PhysX-Anything, Articulate-Anything, Real2Code, URDFormer, ManiTwin, SLAT-Phys, Phys2Real)** [Geometry -> physics asset conversion] | status: CoACD (SIGGRAPH 2022; MIT) with new GPU CuACD (Sep 2026, ~100x faster, ~0.25 s/mesh on RTX 4090).
  PhysX-Anything (CVPR 2026; Qwen2.5-VL; single image -> URDF/MJCF with mass, friction, joints; tested in MuJoCo; deformables unstable). Articulate-Anything (ICLR 2025; MIT; text/image/video -> URDF with actor-critic self-correction). Real2Code (ICLR 2025; up to 10 parts via LLM code). ManiTwin
  (arXiv 2603.16866; 100K assets with VLM-estimated OBB, mass, friction). SLAT-Phys (2603.23973; material property fields from single image). Phys2Real (2510.11689; VLM priors + online adaptation). | license: CoACD MIT. V-HACD BSD (from prior knowledge; low confidence). Articulate-Anything MIT.
  PhysX-Anything S-Lab License (restrictive, non-commercial-style). Others vary. | strengths: Every stage of image->URDF/USD-physics now has a working research method; CoACD emits fewer hulls (typically 4-24 vs V-HACD 10-64), so simulation runs faster | weaknesses: CoACD inflates collision geometry
  (floating objects when stacked) and is 10-100x slower than V-HACD on CPU; VLM physical priors are coarse; articulation inference fails on unusual mechanisms; PhysX-Anything license blocks direct commercial use | best fit: Backbone of CEN's 'sim-ready certification' stage: re-implement/fine-tune on
  own data with permissive bases | sources: https://github.com/SarahWeiii/CoACD https://simready.artlabs.ai/compare/v-hacd-vs-coacd-vs-manual-hulls https://github.com/ziangcao0312/PhysX-Anything https://github.com/vlongle/articulate-anything
- **Physically based materials: MDL, MaterialX 1.39.x, OpenPBR 1.0, UE Substrate** [Material standards] | status: OpenPBR 1.0 (ASWF, Adobe+Autodesk; reference implementation inside MaterialX). MaterialX 1.39.2 (Jan 21, 2025; validated with OpenUSD 25.02). Isaac Sim 6.0 adds MaterialX support; MDL is
  native to Omniverse RTX; UE 5.7 Substrate production-ready. | license: Apache-2.0 (MaterialX/OpenPBR); MDL SDK open (BSD-3, prior knowledge; medium confidence). | strengths: Portable PBR across Omniverse, Blender, UE and USD; Measured-material workflows possible | weaknesses: Generative 3D outputs
  only basic metallic-roughness and need upgrading; Spectral/sensor-band (NIR lidar/radar) material properties are not covered by OpenPBR | best fit: Make OpenPBR/MaterialX the CEN marketplace canonical material, with MDL for RTX and per-band sensor material tables added | sources:
  https://80.lv/articles/aswf-adobe-autodesk-released-openpbr-1-0 https://cgworld.jp/flashnews/202501-MaterialX1392.html https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-s-isaac-sim-6.0-ships-with-nurec-gaussian-splatting
- **Sensor models: Isaac Sim RTX sensors, camera ISP (PPISP), event cameras (v2e/ESIM/EVIS), tactile (TacSL, Taccel, DIGIT sim)** [Sensor realism] | status: Isaac Sim 6.x isaacsim.sensors.experimental.rtx: OmniLidar, OmniRadar, Acoustic/ultrasonic, camera (docs Jun 22, 2026). Physically-plausible
  ISP in 3DGRUT (Jan 2026), gsplat (Jul 2026) and Isaac Lab 3.0. Event cameras: v2e (pixel model with threshold mismatch, bandwidth, noise), ESIM; CARLA native DVS; Isaac Sim lacks native DVS but EVIS (arXiv 2607.08098, Jul 2026) adds real-time event simulation. Tactile: TacSL (>200x faster than
  prior SOTA; in Isaac Lab, PhysX only); Taccel (NeurIPS 2025, MIT, IPC+ABD, 915 FPS at 4096 envs). DIGIT/TACTO sim not re-verified (low confidence). | license: Mostly free/open (Isaac Lab BSD-style, Taccel MIT, v2e MIT per prior knowledge). | strengths: Full multi-modal sensor suite possible in one
  USD scene; GPU-parallel tactile now practical for dexterous RL | weaknesses: Radar realism (multipath, RCS) and lidar intensity need per-material calibration data; Event/tactile sims not unified in one engine | best fit: CEN sensor library with per-device calibrated profiles (a dataset/marketplace
  product in itself) | sources: https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/latest/py/source/extensions/isaacsim.sensors.experimental.rtx/docs/index.html https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut https://arxiv.org/pdf/2006.07722 https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.08098
- **NVIDIA Cosmos Transfer 2.5 / Predict 2.5** [Generative world model: sim-to-real domain adaptation] | status: Released Oct 6, 2025. Transfer2.5-2B is a multi-ControlNet (depth, edge, blur, segmentation) with auto control maps, multiview AV and robot multiview variants. Image2Image Dec 2025;
  distilled low-latency edge model Feb 23, 2026. Predict2.5 2B/14B, action-conditioned and policy variants (Feb 2026). Repos now get only limited maintenance as development moves to Cosmos 3. | license: Code Apache-2.0; weights NVIDIA Open Model License (commercial use allowed). GPU-heavy: Predict1
  7B took ~383 s per clip on 1 H100 (indicative only). | strengths: Label-preserving appearance transfer (keeps geometry via depth/seg control), so sim labels remain valid; Cookbook post-training recipes; AV multiview | weaknesses: Expensive per frame; temporal/physical hallucinations; may shift
  small objects and break labels; Superseded by Cosmos 3 within ~8 months | best fit: Post-render augmentation layer in CEN SDG pipelines (sim RGB -> photoreal variants) with automatic label-consistency QA | sources: https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos/cosmos-transfer2.5 https://github.com/nvidia-
  cosmos/cosmos-predict2.5 https://research.nvidia.com/labs/cosmos-lab/cosmos-predict2.5/ https://docs.api.nvidia.com/nim/re/reference/nvidia-cosmos-1_0-diffusion-7b
- **NVIDIA Cosmos 3 (omnimodel)** [Generative world model / world-action model] | status: Initial release May 2026, publicly launched Jun 1, 2026 (Computex). Edge variant Jul 2026. Mixture-of-transformers over text/image/video/audio/action. Reasoner + Generator surfaces; Transfer notebook (layout ->
  photoreal driving video). GitHub lists Super 64B (H200/B200/GB200), Nano 16B (RTX PRO 6000/H100/B200), Edge 4B (Jetson Thor/Orin), while press reports 32B/8B; discrepancy unresolved. Reported best open T2I/I2V on Artificial Analysis and best policy on RoboArena at report time. | license:
  OpenMDW-1.1 (permissive; covers weights; no output restrictions). Press also cites NVIDIA Open Model License. Medium confidence. | strengths: Permissive license, fine-tunable on CEN data; One model for synthetic data, reasoning/QA (auto-labeling, scene critique) and action simulation | weaknesses:
  Very large compute footprint (Super needs H200/B200 class); Young; physics consistency unproven for contact-rich manipulation | best fit: Strategic successor for CEN augmentation, auto-QA and world-model policy evaluation; fine-tune Nano on Korean domains | sources:
  https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos/blob/main/LICENSE https://research.nvidia.com/labs/cosmos-lab/cosmos3/technical-report.pdf https://pasqualepillitteri.it/en/news/3945/nvidia-cosmos-3-physical-ai
- **Closed generative world models (Google DeepMind Genie 3, Wayve GAIA-2/3/4, Runway GWM-1, Decart Oasis 3, Odyssey)** [Interactive/neural simulators] | status: Genie 3 (Aug 2025; 720p, 24 fps, minutes of consistency). Project Genie for US AI Ultra subscribers from Jan 29, 2026, 60-s sessions, no
  developer API. Wayve GAIA-3 (Dec 2, 2025; 15B; offline AV evaluation) and GAIA-4 (Aug 3, 2026; closed-loop with Wayve driver, adds radar). Runway GWM-1 (Dec 11, 2025; Worlds/Avatars/Robotics, action-conditioned, autoregressive on Gen-4.5). Decart Oasis 3 (Jun 10, 2026; real-time photoreal driving
  via API at $0.02/s; $300M Series C May 2026 at ~$4B). Odyssey: not verified (low). | license: Proprietary; API-metered where available (Decart $0.02/s ≈ $72/hour of video). | strengths: Real-time, highly photoreal long-tail scenario generation; Useful as policy evaluators and rare-event generators
  | weaknesses: No ground-truth geometry/labels/physics guarantees; not reproducible or controllable enough for certification; Vendor-owned; Genie and GAIA not purchasable as APIs | best fit: Benchmark against; optionally integrate Decart/Runway APIs for long-tail video. Do not base the platform on
  them. | sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genie_(world_model) https://yourstory.com/ai-story/google-deepmind-project-genie-launch https://www.autonomousvehicleinternational.com/news/ai-sensor-fusion/wayves-gaia-3-generative-world-model-now-available-for-autonomous-driving-validation.html
  https://aiwiki.ai/wiki/gaia_3_wayve
- **Sim-to-real gap metric suite (FID/KID/CMMD, SDQM, SADGE, IPD, mAP-transfer, policy success correlation, trajectory error)** [Evaluation methodology] | status: FID often fails to predict detector mAP. SDQM (2025) Pearson r=0.8719 with YOLOv11n mAP50 without training. SADGE (structure +
  appearance) Pearson 0.88 / Spearman 0.77 across 15 dataset variants and 79k image pairs. Instance Performance Difference for camera sim (arXiv 2411.07375). Real-to-sim policy evals (PolaRiS, soft-body GS) use paired sim/real success correlation. | license: Methods; implement in-house | strengths:
  Enables a quantitative 'realism SLA' and dataset pricing by measured utility | weaknesses: Requires paired real data per customer domain; No single metric covers physics + sensors + appearance | best fit: CEN Sim2Real Gap Scorecard product feature | sources:
  https://www.emergentmind.com/papers/2605.22467 https://arxiv.org/html/2602.18525v1 https://arxiv.org/html/2411.07375v1 https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.16881
## Key facts
- [medium] Isaac Sim 6.0 (GA June 2026) supports 3D Gaussian Splatting via Fabric Scene Delegate with multi-GPU rendering, light interaction with mesh scenes, and MaterialX; splat export moved to the OpenUSD ParticleField3DGaussianSplat schema (.usdc default, .usdz opt-in).
  (https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-s-isaac-sim-6.0-ships-with-nurec-gaussian-splatting ; https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/6.0.0/overview/release_notes.html)
- [medium] Isaac Sim 6.1.0 GA and 7.0.0a1 pre-release tags exist (Sep 10 and Sep 18); the fetch tool printed the tag years inconsistently, but the 2026 timing follows from 6.0 GA in June 2026. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/releases)
- [high] Isaac Sim recommended datacenter GPUs are A40 (min), L40S (recommended), RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server (best). These are RT-core GPUs; H100/A100 are not listed. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim)
- [medium] OpenUSD 26.03 adds a native UsdVolParticleField schema family, including UsdVolParticleField3DGaussianSplat. (https://radiancefields.com/openusd-26.03-adds-native-gaussian-splat-support)
- [medium] Khronos released KHR_gaussian_splatting as a Release Candidate on Feb 3, 2026 (ratification targeted Q2 2026); Cesium, Babylon.js and PlayCanvas ship support. (https://aecmag.com/reality-capture-modelling/khronos-announces-gltf-gaussian-splatting-extension/)
- [high] 3DGRUT is Apache-2.0; v1.1.0 (Jun 10, 2026) added USD/USDZ/PLY/NuRec export with mesh injection; v2.0.0 (Jun 2026) added Neural Harmonic Textures; PPISP (physically-plausible ISP) added Jan 2026; supports fisheye and rolling shutter. (https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut ;
  https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut/releases)
- [high] gsplat is Apache-2.0, v1.6.0 on main, with 3DGUT, spinning-lidar rasterization (Mar 2026), NCore v4 (Apr 2026), fisheye/FTheta/lidar camera models (Jun 2026), multi-GPU and PPISP (Jul 2026). (https://github.com/nerfstudio-project/gsplat)
- [high] The original Inria 3DGS code (Gaussian-Splatting License) is research/evaluation only; commercial use requires Inria consent. 2DGS and MILo use this license; PGSR's license allows 'educational, research and non-profit purposes only'. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/graphdeco-
  inria/gaussian-splatting/main/LICENSE.md ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hbb1/2d-gaussian-splatting/main/LICENSE.md ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/zju3dv/PGSR/main/LICENSE.md ; https://github.com/Anttwo/MILo)
- [high] Instant-NGP is under Nvidia Source Code License-NC (non-commercial); nvdiffrast is under Nvidia Source Code License (1-Way Commercial), usable non-commercially only except by NVIDIA. (https://github.com/NVlabs/instant-ngp ;
  https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVlabs/nvdiffrast/main/LICENSE.txt)
- [high] Tencent Hunyuan 3D 2.1 Community License excludes the EU, UK and South Korea from its Territory and forbids use of the Works or their outputs outside the Territory; it also has a >1M MAU approval clause. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Tencent-Hunyuan/Hunyuan3D-2.1/main/LICENSE)
- [high] TRELLIS.2-4B is MIT-licensed, outputs GLB with PBR (base color, roughness, metallic, opacity), needs ≥24 GB VRAM, and generates 512³/1024³/1536³ in ~3/17/60 s on H100; it depends on nvdiffrast/nvdiffrec with separate licenses. (https://github.com/microsoft/TRELLIS.2)
- [high] SAM 3D Objects released Nov 19, 2025 under the SAM License; outputs Gaussian splats (.ply) and pose/layout; encoder weights released Jun 1, 2026. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam-3d-objects)
- [high] VGGT-1B-Commercial (Jul 29, 2025) is licensed for commercial use (no military); the original VGGT-1B checkpoint is non-commercial; VGGT-Omega released May 18, 2026. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/vggt)
- [high] Depth Anything 3 (Nov 14, 2025): Small/Base/Metric-Large/Mono-Large under Apache-2.0; Large/Giant/Nested-Giant under CC-BY-NC-4.0. (https://github.com/ByteDance-Seed/Depth-Anything-3)
- [high] MapAnything code is Apache-2.0; weights come in a CC-BY-NC default and an Apache-2.0 'map-anything-apache' variant. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/map-anything)
- [high] Cosmos-Transfer2.5 and Predict2.5 released Oct 6, 2025; Transfer controls are depth, edge, blur and segmentation with AV multiview and robot multiview variants; a distilled edge model was released Feb 23, 2026; weights are under the NVIDIA Open Model License (commercial use allowed).
  (https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos/cosmos-transfer2.5)
- [high] The Cosmos-Predict2.5 repo now receives only limited maintenance; development moved to Cosmos 3 at github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos. (https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos/cosmos-predict2.5)
- [medium] Cosmos 3 is released under OpenMDW-1.1 (permissive, covers weights, no output restrictions); GitHub lists Super 64B, Nano 16B, Edge 4B (Edge released Jul 2026), while press reported 32B/8B. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos ; https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos/blob/main/LICENSE ;
  https://pasqualepillitteri.it/en/news/3945/nvidia-cosmos-3-physical-ai)
- [medium] At GTC 2026 (Mar 17) NVIDIA introduced Newton 1.0 (open source; co-developed with Google DeepMind and Disney Research), Isaac Lab 3.0 early access and GR00T N1.7 early access with commercial licensing. (https://www.robotics247.com/article/nvidia-gtc-2026-nvidia-global-robotics-leaders-
  look-to-take-physical-ai-to-the-real-world)
- [medium] Isaac Lab 3.0 offers Isaac RTX, OVRTX (kit-less), Newton Warp, Newton GL and experimental Newton RTX renderers plus a renderer-neutral physically plausible ISP. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases)
- [medium] AlpaSim is Apache-2.0 (Oct 2025), uses NuRec (3DGUT) as default renderer with OmniDreams video-model rendering as an alternative, and ships about 900 reconstructed 20-second long-tail scenes. (https://github.com/NVlabs/alpasim ; https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-unveils-alpasim-at-ces)
- [medium] Unreal Engine 5.8 released Jun 17, 2026 with Lumen Lite and MegaLights production-ready; it is the last UE5 release, and UE6 early access is targeted for late 2027. (https://gamedev.net/news/3999-unreal-engine-58-released/ ; https://labs.invenglobal.com/articles/22900/unreal-engine-6-to-
  feature-fundamental-overhaul-targeting-early-access-release-by-late-2027)
- [high] Unity's 2026 strategy puts HDRP in maintenance (no new features) and prioritizes URP. (https://unity.com/topics/render-pipelines-strategy-for-2026)
- [medium] WebGPU is on by default in Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Safari 26 (Sep 2025, incl. iOS). (https://app.cinevva.com/news/2026-01-05-webgpu-era)
- [medium] Genie 3 is not available as a developer API; Project Genie (Genie 3 + Nano Banana Pro + Gemini) is a research prototype for US Google AI Ultra subscribers with 60-second sessions. (https://yourstory.com/ai-story/google-deepmind-project-genie-launch ;
  https://letsdatascience.com/news/deepmind-opens-project-genie-for-interactive-worlds-eb2fec7e)
- [medium] Wayve GAIA-3 (Dec 2, 2025) is a 15B latent-diffusion multi-camera world model for AV evaluation; GAIA-4 (Aug 3, 2026) is closed-loop and generates radar alongside cameras. (https://aiwiki.ai/wiki/gaia_3_wayve ; https://www.automotiveworld.com/news/wayve-unveils-gaia-3-for-autonomous-
  driving-tests/)
- [high] PhysX-Anything (CVPR 2026) generates sim-ready URDF/MJCF with geometry, articulation, mass and friction from a single image using Qwen2.5-VL, but uses the restrictive S-Lab License and deformables are unstable in MuJoCo. (https://github.com/ziangcao0312/PhysX-Anything ;
  https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.13648)
- [high] CoACD is MIT-licensed; GPU CuACD (Sep 2026) is ~100x faster (~0.25 s/mesh on RTX 4090). (https://github.com/SarahWeiii/CoACD)
- [medium] CoACD tends to inflate collision geometry (visual meshes float when stacked) and is 1-2 orders of magnitude slower than V-HACD. (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.09153)
- [medium] FID often does not predict detector mAP; SDQM reaches Pearson 0.8719 with YOLOv11n mAP50 and SADGE reaches Pearson 0.88 / Spearman 0.77 with downstream transfer. (https://www.emergentmind.com/papers/2605.22467 ; https://arxiv.org/html/2602.18525v1)
- [medium] TacSL simulates visuotactile images and force fields >200x faster than prior SOTA and is integrated into Isaac Lab (PhysX backend only). (https://research.nvidia.com/publication/2025-01_tacsl-library-visuotactile-sensor-simulation-and-learning ; https://isaac-
  sim.github.io/IsaacLab/release/3.0.0/source/experimental-features/visuo_tactile_sensor.html)
- [high] Taccel (NeurIPS 2025, MIT) uses IPC + Affine Body Dynamics to reach 915 FPS total with 4096 parallel environments for vision-based tactile sensors. (https://papers.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/87d91d52272c3166315ca20b18519b9e-Abstract-Conference.html ; https://github.com/Taccel-
  Simulator/Taccel)
- [medium] World Labs Marble exports splats (SPZ/PLY) plus collider and HQ GLB meshes; GA Nov 12, 2025; World API Jan 2026; Marble 1.1 Apr 2026; commercial rights need the Pro plan. (https://docs.worldlabs.ai/marble/export/gaussian-splat.md ; https://invideo.io/blog/world-labs-marble-3d-worlds/)
- [medium] Decart launched Oasis 3 (Jun 10, 2026), a real-time photoreal driving world model via API at $0.02/s, after a $300M Series C (May 2026, ~$4B valuation). (https://www.kucoin.com/news/flash/decart-launches-oasis-3-for-photorealistic-driving-simulations-via-api ;
  https://startupfortune.com/decart-opens-its-world-model-to-developers-for-two-cents-a-second-betting-it-can-own-physical-ais-simulation-layer-before-the-big-players-build-one-themselves/)
- [medium] Event camera simulation is not native in Isaac Sim; EVIS (arXiv 2607.08098, Jul 2026) adds real-time event simulation there, while CARLA has native DVS. (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.08098 ; https://arxiv.org/html/2508.17643v1)
- [medium] fVDB Reality Capture (Apache-2.0, NVIDIA/OpenVDB) claims ~50% better end-to-end training throughput than gsplat and exports meshes/USD. (https://github.com/openvdb/fvdb-reality-capture)
## Quantitative data
- 3DGUT rendering speed / quality (RTX 5090): NeRF-Synthetic 33.88 PSNR @ 846 FPS; MipNeRF360 27.43 PSNR @ 317 FPS (https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut)
- 3DGRT (ray-traced splats) speed / quality (RTX 5090): NeRF-Synthetic 33.87 PSNR @ 347 FPS; MipNeRF360 27.22 PSNR @ 68 FPS (https://github.com/nv-tlabs/3dgrut)
- gsplat efficiency vs official 3DGS: Up to 4x less GPU memory, up to 15% less training time at equal PSNR/SSIM/LPIPS; ~30% faster 3DGUT-MCMC path on A100 (Jun 2026) (https://github.com/nerfstudio-project/gsplat)
- NeRF vs 3DGS render speed: 3DGS 100-200x faster than original NeRF implementations; Zip-NeRF needs multiple seconds per high-res frame (https://metavert.io/compare/gaussian-splatting-vs-neural-radiance-fields ; https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.06706)
- TRELLIS.2-4B generation time on H100: 512³ ~3 s; 1024³ ~17 s; 1536³ ~60 s; ≥24 GB VRAM (https://github.com/microsoft/TRELLIS.2)
- Hunyuan3D 2.1 VRAM: 10 GB shape, 21 GB texture, 29 GB combined (https://github.com/Tencent-Hunyuan/Hunyuan3D-2.1)
- CuACD convex decomposition speed: ~0.25 s per mesh on RTX 4090 (~100x faster than CoACD) (https://github.com/SarahWeiii/CoACD)
- Convex hull counts: CoACD typically 4-24 hulls vs V-HACD 10-64 for similar fidelity (https://simready.artlabs.ai/compare/v-hacd-vs-coacd-vs-manual-hulls)
- SplatSim zero-shot sim-to-real manipulation success: 86.25% average (vs 97.5% for policies trained on real data) (https://arxiv.org/html/2511.04665v2 (search summary))
- Zero-shot sim-to-real of world-action model (Cosmos Policy, ~800 synthetic demos/task, no real demos, Franka): 35% average success (https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31101)
- Learned augmentation effect on sim-to-real picking/stacking: Cube pick 19/20 vs 3/20 without augmentation; stacking 18/20 vs 1/20 (https://docs.nvidia.com/learning/physical-ai/sim-to-real-so-101/latest/14-strategy3-cosmos.html (search summary))
- AlpaSim effect on evaluation variance: Up to 83% reduction in variance of key real-world metrics; ~900 reconstructed 20-s scenes (https://radiancefields.com/nvidia-unveils-alpasim-at-ces)
- Synthetic-data quality metric correlation with detector mAP: SDQM Pearson r=0.8719 (YOLOv11n mAP50); SADGE Pearson 0.88, Spearman 0.77 over 15 variants / 79k image pairs (https://www.emergentmind.com/papers/2605.22467 ; https://arxiv.org/html/2602.18525v1)
- TacSL speedup: >200x faster than prior visuotactile simulation SOTA (https://research.nvidia.com/publication/2025-01_tacsl-library-visuotactile-sensor-simulation-and-learning)
- Taccel throughput: 915 FPS total with 4096 parallel environments (https://papers.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/87d91d52272c3166315ca20b18519b9e-Abstract-Conference.html)
- Cosmos 3 model sizes: Super 64B / Nano 16B / Edge 4B (GitHub) vs 32B / 8B (press); trained on ~20T multimodal tokens (press) (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos ; https://pasqualepillitteri.it/en/news/3945/nvidia-cosmos-3-physical-ai)
- Cosmos video WFM inference cost (indicative, older Predict1): ~383 s (7B) and ~593 s (14B) per Video2World clip on one H100 (https://docs.api.nvidia.com/nim/re/reference/nvidia-cosmos-1_0-diffusion-7b)
- Wayve GAIA-3 scale: 15B params; ~5x compute and ~10x data vs GAIA-2; 9 countries (https://aiwiki.ai/wiki/gaia_3_wayve)
- Genie 3 output: 720p @ 24 fps, multi-minute consistency; Project Genie sessions capped at 60 s (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genie_(world_model) ; https://letsdatascience.com/news/deepmind-opens-project-genie-for-interactive-worlds-eb2fec7e)
- Decart Oasis 3 API price: $0.02 per second of generated video (~$72/hour) (https://startupfortune.com/decart-opens-its-world-model-to-developers-for-two-cents-a-second-betting-it-can-own-physical-ais-simulation-layer-before-the-big-players-build-one-themselves/)
- World Labs Marble API price: From ~$0.12 per Draft world (https://invideo.io/blog/world-labs-marble-3d-worlds/)
- Web splat streaming: SuperSplat 3.0: 24M Gaussians streamed at ~60 fps (WebGPU); Spark 2.0: 100M+ splat worlds with LoD paging (https://radiancefields.com/playcanvas-releases-supersplat-3.0 ; https://cgworld.jp/flashnews/01-202603-Spark2.html)
- Commercial 3D-gen pricing: Meshy Pro $20/mo, Max $60/mo, API/Enterprise ~$90/mo; Tripo Pro $12/mo; Rodin ~$0.50-1.50/model (https://costbench.com/software/ai-3d-generation/meshy/ ; https://dupple.com/reviews/rodin-ai)
- Blender 5.1 Cycles speedup: Up to 10% faster GPU, up to 20% faster CPU on Windows (https://gamedev.net/news/2274-blender-51-release/)
- VGGT-1B-Commercial vs original: Co3D AUC@30 90.37 vs 89.98 (https://github.com/facebookresearch/vggt)


# TOPIC: platform_arch
## Options
- **OpenUSD + AOUSD Core Specification** [Canonical scene description / interchange standard] | status: AOUSD Core Spec v1.0.1 published 2025-12-12. It is the only normative AOUSD spec so far and covers the data model, composition and value resolution. UsdPhysics and other domain schemas are not yet
  normative. AOUSD reported member/industrial momentum in March 2026. Pixar OpenUSD 26.08 (2026-07-20): composition load up to 80% faster on heavy rigs, LOD schemas, UsdProfiles. 26.05 (2026-04-24): Hydra 2 scene-index path is the default. 26.03 (2026-02-24): wasm32/wasm64 builds,
  UsdVolParticleField (3D Gaussian splats), UsdPhysics nested rigid bodies and kinematic articulation. 25.11: OpenExec moved into core. | license: OpenUSD code is under the Tomorrow Open Source Technology License (Apache-2.0-derived), free. AOUSD spec text is CC-BY-ND-4.0. AOUSD membership is
  optional and paid. | strengths: De-facto industry standard: NVIDIA, Apple, Autodesk, Adobe, Siemens and Trimble ecosystems; Non-destructive layer composition maps naturally to multi-user edits, live-state overlays and scene versioning; UsdPhysics is the common physics vocabulary read by
  PhysX/ovphysx, Newton and MuJoCo converters; WASM builds and a Babylon.js OpenUSD loader enable a browser path; UsdValidation framework with fixers can serve as a CI gate for agent-authored scenes | weaknesses: Steep learning curve, and composition semantics are hard for app developers; Physics,
  sensor and actuator semantics are still vendor-namespaced (physx*, newton*, mjc*), with no normative physics spec yet; Large-stage streaming to browsers still needs conversion or LOD (glTF or splats) | best fit: Canonical internal representation for all scenes, robots, vehicles, factories,
  materials and semantic labels. | sources: https://github.com/aousd/specifications-public https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PixarAnimationStudios/OpenUSD/release/CHANGELOG.md https://github.com/PixarAnimationStudios/OpenUSD/releases https://www.linuxfoundation.org/press/aousd_prmarch2026
- **Newton physics engine + newton-usd-schemas + URDF/MJCF->USD converters** [Physics backend (GPU, open source) + USD physics schema extension] | status: Newton 1.0 GA at GTC in March 2026, after a beta first shown in Sept 2025. Latest is v1.6.1 (2026-10-05). Earlier steps: v1.6.0 (2026-09-10) made
  hydroelastic contacts 33-37% faster and added OpenUSD particles. v1.4.0 (2026-07-16) added coupled solvers (MuJoCo, Kamino, VBD) and deterministic paths for bit-exact repeated rollouts. v1.2.0 added a composable actuator subsystem with USD schema support. The project is hosted by the Linux
  Foundation, co-developed by Disney Research, Google DeepMind and NVIDIA. newton-usd-schemas is v0.x and marked experimental (codeless schemas extending UsdPhysics). The org also ships urdf-usd-converter and mujoco-usd-converter. | license: Apache-2.0 for code, CC-BY-4.0 for docs. Free. |
  strengths: Multi-solver (MuJoCo-Warp, VBD cloth/cables, implicit MPM, Kamino) behind one model API; Built on Warp, so it runs batched on GPU and integrates differentiably with PyTorch/JAX via Warp; Vendor-neutral governance under the Linux Foundation; Native backend in Isaac Lab 3.0
  (newton_mjwarp), with a Kit-less path | weaknesses: Fast release cadence (monthly minors) and schema v0.x means API churn; Requires NVIDIA CUDA GPUs; no AMD or Apple path; Vehicle, tire and powertrain models are thinner than PhysX vehicle or dedicated AV simulators | best fit: Default physics
  backend for robot learning (locomotion, manipulation, deformables) and batched simulation twins. | sources: https://github.com/newton-physics/newton https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases https://github.com/newton-physics/newton-usd-schemas https://github.com/newton-physics
- **NVIDIA Omniverse libraries: ovphysx / ovrtx / ovstorage** [Headless modular physics / RTX sensor rendering / storage libraries] | status: Announced at GTC 2026 as headless-first C APIs with C++ and Python bindings, early access on GitHub and NGC, with production API stability promised 'later
  this year'. The SIGGRAPH 2026 coverage was not fetchable here. ovphysx 0.6 maps to PhysX SDK 5.11.0 (0.5 to 5.10) and is installed with pip install ovphysx; it offers USD physics with DLPack tensor interop. ovrtx is pre-release: a C11 + Python 3.10-3.13 SDK for camera, lidar and radar on Windows
  x86_64 and Linux x86_64/aarch64, shipped as PyPI wheels. ovstorage is pre-release and not enterprise-supported: a plugin storage client for file, HTTP, S3, GCS, Azure, OpenDAL, Nucleus and Omniverse Storage Service, with an MCP server and agent skills. | license: ovphysx/PhysX: BSD-3-Clause.
  ovstorage: Apache-2.0 (agent skills CC-BY-4.0; vendored service material proprietary). ovrtx: NVIDIA Software License Agreement + Product-Specific Terms for NVIDIA AI Products, which is proprietary, so hosted-SaaS terms must be confirmed. | strengths: Lets you embed NVIDIA physics and RTX sensors
  into your own services without the Kit app framework, which suits CI/CD and K8s; Same backends Isaac Lab 3.0 selects (physics=ovphysx, renderer=ovrtx); ovstorage treats Nucleus as just one backend, which aligns with an object-storage-first design | weaknesses: Pre-1.0, and NVIDIA states APIs may
  change between releases; ovrtx is closed-license and needs RTX (RT-core) GPUs; ovstorage is not accepting external contributions and is not enterprise-supported | best fit: ovphysx: USD-native industrial and vehicle rigid/articulated physics. ovrtx: high-fidelity sensor simulation and SDG workers.
  ovstorage: optional client abstraction. | sources: https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/integrate-physical-ai-capabilities-into-existing-apps-with-nvidia-omniverse-libraries/ https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/ovrtx https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/PhysX https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-
  Omniverse/PhysX/main/README.md
- **NVIDIA Omniverse Kit SDK / Kit App Template / Kit App Streaming / NVCF / Nucleus** [Full application framework + streaming + collaboration server] | status: Kit App Template changelog shows 110.3.0 (2026-08-28, with a kit-upgrade agent skill for 106-110 migrations), 110.1.0 (2026-04-06,
  versioned packages), 109.0.1 (2025-12-04, ARM64, raised driver floor) and 108.0.0 (2025-08-12, 'Omniverse Cloud Streaming' renamed 'NVCF Streaming', omni.kit.livestream.app). Templates: Kit Service, Base Editor, USD Composer, USD Explorer, USD Viewer (built for streaming). Kit App Streaming (OKAS)
  is a self-managed K8s/Helm reference; NVCF is the managed option. Launcher deprecated 2025-10-01. web-viewer-sample deprecated in favour of the OV Web SDK WebRTC sample. Nucleus continues, but the 26H1 storage stack and ovstorage reposition it as one backend. | license: NVIDIA Software License +
  Omniverse Product-Specific Terms (proprietary). Enterprise production deployment historically needed Omniverse Enterprise per-GPU licensing. Current price not verified (low confidence). | strengths: Fastest route to a polished RTX editor, viewer and streamed app; Reference blueprints exist: the
  DSX AI-factory twin (React portal + Kit WebRTC livestream + OKAS/NVCF, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell) and the CAE fluid twin (React + WebRTC + NIM/Triton surrogate); Mature extension ecosystem (400+ extensions) | weaknesses: Heavy runtime and a GPU per session, which makes streaming costly; Proprietary,
  with frequent major versions (108 to 110 in about 12 months); Lock-in to NVIDIA hardware and licensing; template is based on a Kit 'feature branch' | best fit: Premium high-fidelity editing and viewing sessions and Isaac Sim-based workflows. Not suited to the always-on default UX or batch workers.
  | sources: https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-app-template https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-app-template/main/CHANGELOG.md https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/web-viewer-sample https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/omniverse-launcher-update/321326
- **Isaac Sim 6.1 / Isaac Lab 3.0** [Robotics simulator app + robot-learning framework (reference for backend abstraction)] | status: Isaac Sim v6.0.0 GA (June 2026), v6.0.1 (June 22), v6.1.0 GA (Sept 10, 2026), v7.0.0a1 (Sept 18, 2026). Python 3.12, Ubuntu 22.04/24.04 or Windows 11. Minimum RTX
  4080 or A40; L40S recommended. Isaac Lab 3.0.0-EA (Sept 16, 2026) has multi-backend physics (isaacsim_physx, ovphysx, newton_mjwarp), renderers (isaacsim_rtx, ovrtx, newton_renderer), five visualizers (Newton GL, Newton RTX, Kit, Viser, Rerun) and a unified 'isaaclab train'. Breaking change:
  quaternions go from (w,x,y,z) to (x,y,z,w). The IsaacSim-ros_workspaces README lists only Humble and Jazzy. | license: Isaac Sim source is Apache-2.0, but it depends on the Kit SDK (NVIDIA proprietary). Isaac Lab is BSD-3; isaaclab_mimic is Apache-2.0. Free. | strengths: Most complete open
  robotics sim stack: RTX sensors, ROS bridge, Replicator SDG, importers, RL/IL (Mimic); Isaac Lab 3.0's factory pattern is a ready blueprint for an engine-agnostic sim kernel; Kit-less Newton mode allows light containers | weaknesses: Major-version churn (5.x, then 6.x, then 7.0 alpha within about
  15 months) and breaking API changes; No official ROS 2 Lyrical workspace yet, per the repo README; Full app needs RT-core GPUs, so it cannot render on H100/B200 | best fit: High-fidelity robot sensor simulation, SDG and RL/IL training environments. Wrap it as one backend of AICHEMIST's Sim Kernel,
  not as the platform itself. | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/releases https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-EA
- **MuJoCo 3.x / MuJoCo Warp / MJCF** [Physics backend + robot description format] | status: MuJoCo 3.15.0 (2026-10-05). 3.14.0 added archive resource providers. 3.13.0 added Python 3.15 and a 'discrete' integrator. 3.12.0 added a PID actuator. MuJoCo Warp is version-locked (3.15.0). The repo has
  src/experimental/usd with an mjcPhysics schema, an MJCF plugin and a USD writer. | license: Apache-2.0, free. | strengths: Deterministic CPU simulation is the golden reference for replay and regression; Huge model zoo (Menagerie) and MJCF is the lingua franca of RL research; MuJoCo Warp powers
  Newton's main solver | weaknesses: USD support is experimental; Limited rendering and sensor realism | best fit: Golden-reference and validation backend, plus MJCF import/export for customer compatibility. | sources: https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/releases https://github.com/google-
  deepmind/mujoco/tree/main/src/experimental/usd https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/releases
- **URDF / SDFormat / glTF 2.0 + converter plugins** [Edge interchange formats] | status: SDFormat 16 ships with Gazebo Jetty. glTF 2.0: KHR_physics_rigid_bodies is still 'Review Draft'; the extensions index lists KHR_interactivity and KHR_gaussian_splatting among ratified extensions (medium
  confidence). adobe/USD-Fileformat-plugins (Apache-2.0) covers glTF/GLB, FBX, OBJ, PLY, SBSAR, SPZ and STL. NVIDIA usd-exchange SDK (Apache-2.0) is for writing USD connectors. | license: Open specs. Converter plugins are Apache-2.0. | strengths: glTF/GLB is the best format for web delivery; URDF is
  universal in ROS; SPZ/PLY carry Gaussian splats from NeRF/3DGS capture, matching CEN's photo-to-3D pipeline | weaknesses: URDF has no closed loops and weak sensor/material semantics; glTF physics is not ratified; Round-trips lose information | best fit: Import/export edges only. Compile USD to
  glTF/splat LOD bundles for browser viewing. | sources: https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions https://github.com/adobe/USD-Fileformat-plugins https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/usd-exchange https://github.com/gazebosim/sdformat/releases
- **ROS 2 (Humble / Jazzy / Kilted / Lyrical) + rmw_zenoh + rosbag2/MCAP** [Robot middleware / live-twin connectivity / logging] | status: Lyrical Luth released 2026-05-22, LTS to May 2031, the 12th release. It adds native buffers in the Fast DDS RMW for GPU/accelerated memory transport. Jazzy EOL
  May 2029, Humble EOL May 2027, Kilted EOL Dec 2026. The next release, 'Makoa Mata-mata', is due May 2027. rmw_zenoh needs a Zenoh router (multicast discovery off by default) and is not interoperable with zenoh-plugin-ros2dds. rosbag2's default storage is MCAP. | license: Apache-2.0, free. |
  strengths: Universal robot integration; sim and real share the same interfaces (use_sim_time /clock); Zenoh suits WAN and cloud links from customer sites to the twin cloud better than DDS multicast; MCAP is indexable, compressible and multi-language | weaknesses: Version fragmentation across
  customers (Humble, Jazzy and Lyrical all coexist); DDS discovery is hostile to NAT/cloud, so a bridge or Zenoh is needed | best fit: Robot live-twin ingest, HIL/SIL, and canonical robot log format (MCAP). | sources: https://github.com/ros2/ros2_documentation/blob/rolling/source/Releases.rst
  https://docs.ros.org/en/lyrical/Releases/Release-Lyrical-Luth.html https://discourse.openrobotics.org/t/ros-2-lyrical-luth-and-11-years-of-fast-dds-as-ros-2-default-middleware/55062 https://github.com/ros2/rmw_zenoh
- **Gazebo Jetty (gz-sim 10) + ros_gz** [Open-source ROS-native simulator (alternative backend)] | status: Jetty (gz-sim 10, SDFormat 16) is the current LTS, released around late 2025 (exact date unverified). ros_gz pairings: Humble-Fortress, Jazzy-Harmonic, Kilted-Ionic, Lyrical-Jetty, Rolling-
  Jetty. | license: Apache-2.0, free. | strengths: ROS-native, and many customers already have SDF worlds; Runs CPU-only, which is cheap for CI and logic tests | weaknesses: Lower visual and sensor fidelity; Not GPU-batched for RL at scale | best fit: Optional plug-in backend for ROS customers and
  CI smoke tests. Not a flagship backend. | sources: https://github.com/gazebosim/ros_gz https://github.com/gazebosim/gz-sim/releases https://github.com/gazebosim/sdformat/releases
- **FMI 3.0 / SSP / Modelica (OpenModelica)** [System-level co-simulation standards (vehicles, powertrain, HVAC, PLC/process models)] | status: FMI 3.0.2 (2024-11-27) is the latest maintenance release, adding clocks, arrays, terminals and icons, and layered standards. No FMI 3.1 was found. SSP 1.0
  and 2.0 maintenance branches exist (2.0 release date not verified). OpenModelica 1.27.x (2026) is still FMI 2-focused; FMI 3 support is promised for 1.28.0 (planned end of 2026). | license: FMI/SSP standards are free (SSP schemas BSD-2, docs CC-BY-SA). OpenModelica is OSMC-PL/GPL. Commercial tools
  (Dymola, Simulink) export FMUs. | strengths: Lets customers bring their existing validated vehicle and plant models as black-box FMUs; SSP describes system wiring and parameters tool-independently | weaknesses: Fixed-step co-simulation with rigid-body physics needs a careful master algorithm;
  OpenModelica lags on FMI 3 | best fit: Vehicle dynamics/powertrain, factory process and energy models plugged into the co-simulation orchestrator alongside the physics backend. | sources: https://github.com/modelica/fmi-standard/releases https://github.com/modelica/ssp-standard
  https://github.com/OpenModelica/OpenModelica/releases
- **OPC UA (open62541) / MQTT / Kafka / Eclipse Ditto** [Industrial live-twin ingestion and twin-state management] | status: open62541 v1.5.9, v1.4.21 and v1.3.22 released together in October 2026. It supports PubSub with encryption and TLS MQTT PubSub. Eclipse Ditto 3.9.7 (2026, fixing
  CVE-2026-84175 and CVE-2026-82958); 3.9.0 added WoT Thing Description support plus MQTT/Kafka connectivity. Kafka 4.x is KRaft-only (not verified this session). | license: open62541 MPL-2.0. Ditto EPL-2.0. Kafka Apache-2.0. MQTT brokers (Mosquitto EPL/EDL, EMQX) are open source; managed options
  are paid. | strengths: Standard factory connectivity (OPC UA) plus lightweight IoT (MQTT); Ditto provides twin shadow state, a policy/ACL model and search out of the box | weaknesses: Ditto is JVM/MongoDB-heavy; Mapping the OPC UA information model to USD prims needs a custom mapping layer | best
  fit: Live-twin state sync for factories, warehouses, ships and fleets. | sources: https://github.com/open62541/open62541/releases https://github.com/eclipse-ditto/ditto/releases
- **Time-series DBs: InfluxDB 3 / TimescaleDB** [Live-twin history and telemetry store] | status: InfluxDB 3 v3.11.4 (2026-09-04) uses an object-storage and Parquet architecture. TimescaleDB 2.x offers hypertables, a columnstore with 90%+ compression, and continuous aggregates. | license: InfluxDB 3
  Core is open source (MIT/Apache, not re-verified); Enterprise is paid. TimescaleDB is Apache-2.0 core plus the Timescale License (TSL) for advanced features, so check SaaS-hosting restrictions. | strengths: Parquet/object storage (Influx 3) fits a data lake; Timescale is plain Postgres, so it can
  share the platform's metadata DB skills | weaknesses: The TSL restricts offering Timescale as a DBaaS; InfluxDB 3 Core is young | best fit: TimescaleDB for an MVP (Postgres ops reuse); evaluate InfluxDB 3 or Iceberg for large fleets. | sources: https://github.com/influxdata/influxdb/releases
  https://github.com/timescale/timescaledb
- **Dataset/log formats: MCAP, rosbag2, LeRobotDataset v3, Rerun** [Episode logging, training-data export, debugging visualization] | status: MCAP (MIT; libraries in C++, Go, Python, TS, Swift, Rust) is the rosbag2 default. LeRobotDataset v3.0 stores many episodes per Parquet/MP4 file, with
  meta/info.json, stats.json, chunked episode metadata, StreamingLeRobotDataset and a v2.1 converter; dataset.finalize() is required. LeRobot v0.6.1 (Aug 2026). Rerun 0.38.1 (2026-09-17) has a ROS 2 MCAP timeline, experimental Gaussian splats and a catalog for larger-than-RAM files. Lichtblick
  (MPL-2.0, open-core) is a fork of Foxglove Studio. | license: MCAP MIT. LeRobot Apache-2.0. Rerun MIT/Apache (not explicitly verified). Lichtblick MPL-2.0. | strengths: MCAP to LeRobot v3 conversion links real and sim logs to VLA/IL training; LeRobot v3 streaming from the Hub matches the
  marketplace distribution model | weaknesses: LeRobot API churn (breaking module renames in 0.6.x); Video codec settings affect training fidelity | best fit: MCAP is canonical for raw multi-sensor logs (sim and real). LeRobot v3 is the canonical export for robot-learning datasets. Rerun or
  Lichtblick serve as embedded debug viewers. | sources: https://github.com/foxglove/mcap https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/lerobot/main/docs/source/lerobot-dataset-v3.mdx https://github.com/huggingface/lerobot/releases https://github.com/rerun-io/rerun/releases
- **Kubernetes + NVIDIA GPU Operator + KAI Scheduler (Run:ai OSS core) + DRA** [GPU cluster orchestration and multi-tenant scheduling] | status: GPU Operator v26.7.1 (Sept 2026), released on a 26.3/26.7 cadence. KAI Scheduler v0.18.2 (Oct 2026): gang scheduling, NvFractions GPU-memory fractional
  sharing, hierarchical queues with DRF and reclaim, topology-aware placement, DRA accounting. NVIDIA DRA driver (Apache-2.0): ComputeDomains (MNNVL/IMEX) are supported; DRA-based GPU allocation is still 'not yet officially supported'. | license: All Apache-2.0 and free. Run:ai commercial control
  plane is paid (price not verified). | strengths: Fractional GPUs and per-tenant queues are exactly what interactive sim sessions plus batch jobs need; Scheduling for RL and distributed training is gang-safe | weaknesses: Fractional and time-sliced GPUs do not isolate memory or faults, so they are
  unsafe across tenants; DRA GPU path not yet GA in NVIDIA's driver | best fit: Core scheduler for the AICHEMIST cluster: queues per tenant and tier, fractions only within a tenant. | sources: https://github.com/NVIDIA/gpu-operator/releases https://github.com/NVIDIA/KAI-Scheduler
  https://github.com/NVIDIA/KAI-Scheduler/releases https://github.com/NVIDIA/k8s-dra-driver-gpu
- **NVIDIA OSMO** [Physical-AI workflow orchestrator (SDG, sim, train, HIL DAGs)] | status: Open source on GitHub. Release 6.3.1 (June 2026) brought ConfigMap configuration, gateway TLS and deploy scripts for AKS, EKS and microk8s. 6.2.8 added OAuth2 RBAC and NVLink topology-aware scheduling. Runs
  locally on KIND or on cloud K8s. | license: Apache-2.0, free. | strengths: Purpose-built for Isaac SDG, training, RL and HIL pipelines in YAML; Supports heterogeneous compute, including edge devices | weaknesses: Overlaps with Argo, Flyte and Ray; NVIDIA-centric roadmap | best fit: Optional
  internal pipeline engine for SDG to train to evaluate. Alternatively use Argo Workflows plus Ray if you want vendor neutrality. | sources: https://github.com/NVIDIA/OSMO https://github.com/NVIDIA/OSMO/releases
- **Ray (KubeRay) / SkyPilot / Slurm via Slinky** [Distributed compute, multi-cloud burst, HPC-style training] | status: Ray 2.59.0 (Oct 2026): Ray Data LLM and Serve LLM are GA; 2.58 added an experimental gVisor-based Ray Sandbox and TPU support in Ray Train. SkyPilot (Apache-2.0) covers K8s, Slurm
  and 20+ clouds with spot failover, autostop and binpacking. Slinky slurm-operator 1.3.x (Apache-2.0, SchedMD) supports K8s 1.35-1.37 and Slurm 25.11 or newer. | license: Apache-2.0, free. Anyscale is paid. | strengths: Ray suits RL rollouts plus SDG data pipelines; SkyPilot cuts cost by chasing
  spot capacity across clouds, including Korean data residency choices; Slinky gives ML researchers familiar sbatch on the same K8s cluster | weaknesses: Three schedulers add operational complexity, so pick one primary (KAI) and treat the others as job frontends | best fit: Ray for RL and data,
  SkyPilot for burst, Slinky only if the research team demands Slurm. | sources: https://github.com/ray-project/ray/releases https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot https://github.com/SlinkyProject/slurm-operator
- **Server-side pixel streaming: Omniverse Kit livestream (WebRTC) / UE Pixel Streaming 2 / Selkies** [Remote rendering to the browser] | status: Kit uses omni.kit.livestream.app (WebRTC) with OKAS on K8s or NVCF; the old web-viewer-sample is deprecated in favour of the OV Web SDK. UE Pixel
  Streaming 2 (introduced UE 5.5): the infrastructure repo supports UE5.8 (current) and UE5.7, UE5.6 is end-of-life; it includes the Cirrus signalling server and an SFU, MIT licensed. Selkies (MPL-2.0): WebSocket by default with WebRTC opt-in, Ubuntu 26.04 desktop containers; the project is asking
  for maintainers. | license: Software is free (Kit is proprietary). Cost is GPU-hours: roughly 1 L40S-class GPU per heavy RTX session, about $1.86-2.29/h on-demand on AWS. | strengths: Full RTX fidelity on any thin client; Selkies can stream CEN's existing browser virtual OS/desktop workspace |
  weaknesses: Highest per-user cost; Latency from Korea to US regions; Needs TURN/SFU infrastructure | best fit: Premium, on-demand high-fidelity sessions and full desktop workspaces, with auto-suspend. | sources: https://github.com/EpicGamesExt/PixelStreamingInfrastructure
  https://github.com/selkies-project/selkies https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/web-viewer-sample https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-app-template/main/CHANGELOG.md
- **Client-side web 3D: three.js / Babylon.js / PlayCanvas / Spark / Viser** [Browser rendering (WebGPU/WebGL) for the default UX] | status: three.js r186 (Sept 2026): WebGPU compute (compileComputeAsync) and TSL improvements. Babylon.js 9.29.0 (Oct 1, 2026); 9.26 (Sept 2026) added an OpenUSD
  WebAssembly loader, with Gaussian-splat streaming/LOD and KHR_interactivity. PlayCanvas 2.23.0 (Oct 2026): distance-based splat LOD and streamed loading. Spark 2.x (MIT, World Labs): a three.js 3DGS renderer supporting PLY, SPZ, SPLAT, KSPLAT and SOG. Viser (Apache-2.0): Python-driven web 3D, one
  of Isaac Lab 3.0's visualizers. | license: MIT (three.js, PlayCanvas engine, Spark) and Apache-2.0 (Babylon.js, Viser). No server GPU cost. | strengths: Zero marginal GPU cost and instant load, the right default for dashboards, live-twin monitoring, annotation and marketplace previews; 3DGS lets
  photoreal captured environments run on laptops and phones | weaknesses: No RTX path tracing or physically accurate sensor simulation; Large USD stages need server-side baking to glTF and splat LODs | best fit: Default UI. Babylon.js if the browser reads USD directly; three.js + Spark if you want
  the largest ecosystem and splats. | sources: https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/releases https://github.com/BabylonJS/Babylon.js/releases https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases https://github.com/sparkjsdev/spark
- **Scene and data versioning: USD layers + content-addressed object store; Git LFS; Perforce; lakeFS; DVC/Iceberg** [Version control and reproducibility] | status: lakeFS v1.87.0 (Sept 2026) changed its license from Apache-2.0 to Business Source License 1.1 and removed custom IAM plugin and ACL
  reference server support; v1.88.0 shipped Oct 2026. Git LFS and Perforce are unchanged (not re-verified). | license: lakeFS BSL 1.1 restricts competing hosted offerings (review the grant). Git LFS is MIT. Perforce is commercial. DVC is Apache-2.0. Iceberg is Apache-2.0. | strengths: USD layer
  stacks diff and merge at layer granularity; Content hashes give exact replay pins | weaknesses: lakeFS license change is a risk for a SaaS that embeds it; Perforce licensing cost | best fit: Build a thin in-house 'scene commit' service (USD layers + blob hashes in S3 + Postgres metadata). Use
  Iceberg or DVC snapshots for datasets. Offer Perforce/Git connectors for enterprise customers. | sources: https://github.com/treeverse/lakeFS/releases/tag/v1.87.0 https://github.com/treeverse/lakeFS/releases
- **MCP servers for LLM/agent control (NVIDIA kit-usd-agents, isaac-sim-mcp, blender-mcp, unreal-mcp, ros-mcp-server)** [Agentic scene authoring and sim control] | status: MCP spec schema version 2026-07-28. NVIDIA kit-usd-agents (Apache-2.0) provides Kit MCP (12 tools), USD Code MCP (7), OmniUI MCP
  (10), Isaac Sim MCP (5) and Chat USD, and is not accepting contributions; Omniverse libraries also ship agent skills. Community servers: omni-mcp/isaac-sim-mcp (MIT, Isaac Sim 4.2+, 14 commits, arbitrary Python exec); blender-mcp (MIT, about 30k stars, now the PyPI package 'mcp-for-blender',
  execute_blender_code); chongdashu/unreal-mcp (MIT, UE 5.5+, experimental, 2.1k stars); robotmcp/ros-mcp-server (Apache-2.0, 1.5k stars, rosbridge-based, ROS 1 and ROS 2). | license: Mostly MIT or Apache-2.0, free. LLM token cost is passed through to the CEN token model. | strengths: Lets CEN's
  existing LLM text-command interface drive scenes, jobs and robots through a standard protocol; NVIDIA's MCPs ground code generation in correct Kit/USD APIs | weaknesses: Community runtime MCPs execute arbitrary code, a prompt-injection and RCE risk in multi-tenant SaaS; Thin maintenance (isaac-
  sim-mcp) | best fit: Build a first-party typed MCP server over the platform APIs. Use NVIDIA's doc/code MCPs inside the authoring agent. Use community MCPs only in sandboxed, single-tenant dev workspaces. | sources: https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol
  https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-usd-agents https://github.com/omni-mcp/isaac-sim-mcp https://github.com/ahujasid/blender-mcp
- **AV-specific: CARLA + ASAM OSI / OpenDRIVE / OpenSCENARIO** [Autonomous driving simulator and scenario standards (vehicle vertical)] | status: CARLA 0.10.0 (Dec 2024) moved to UE 5.5 (Nanite/Lumen), added native ROS 2 and switched physics from PhysX to Chaos. The 0.9.x line continues on UE4. ASAM
  OSI v3.8.0 is the latest listed release (date not verified). OpenDRIVE and OpenSCENARIO versions were not verified this session. | license: CARLA MIT (assets CC-BY). OSI MPL-2.0. ASAM standards free or paid depending on membership (not verified). | strengths: Industry-expected scenario and road
  formats for OEM and Tier-1 customers | weaknesses: UE-based: a second rendering stack outside USD; Vehicle physics fidelity is below dedicated vehicle-dynamics FMUs | best fit: Support OpenDRIVE/OpenSCENARIO import and OSI sensor-data export in the vehicle module. Treat CARLA as an optional
  backend, not the core. | sources: https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla/releases https://github.com/OpenSimulationInterface/open-simulation-interface/releases
## Key facts
- [high] AOUSD Core Specification v1.0.1 was published 2025-12-12 under CC-BY-ND-4.0. It is the only published AOUSD spec and covers the data model, composition and value resolution. No physics spec has been published yet. (https://github.com/aousd/specifications-public ;
  https://aousd.org/uncategorized/foundations-of-open-3d-development-introducing-aousd-core-specification-1-0/)
- [high] OpenUSD releases: 26.08 (2026-07-20), 26.05 (2026-04-24) and 26.03 (2026-02-24). 26.03 added wasm32/wasm64 support and the UsdVolParticleField schema family (3D Gaussian splats). 26.05 made the Hydra 2 scene-index path the default. UsdPhysics gained nested rigid bodies and kinematic
  articulations. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PixarAnimationStudios/OpenUSD/release/CHANGELOG.md)
- [medium] NVIDIA announced modular, headless-first Omniverse libraries at GTC 2026 (ovrtx, ovphysx, ovstorage) as C APIs with C++ and Python bindings, in early access on GitHub and NGC, with production API stability planned later in 2026. (https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/integrate-physical-ai-
  capabilities-into-existing-apps-with-nvidia-omniverse-libraries/ (via search snippet; page egress-blocked))
- [high] ovphysx: 'C API with Python bindings for USD physics simulation with DLPack tensor interop', installed via pip. Release 0.6 corresponds to PhysX SDK 5.11.0, under BSD-3-Clause. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/PhysX)
- [high] ovrtx is pre-release, licensed under the NVIDIA Software License Agreement and Product-Specific Terms for NVIDIA AI Products, with Python 3.10-3.13 on Windows x86_64 and Linux x86_64/aarch64. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/ovrtx)
- [high] ovstorage is Apache-2.0 and pre-release/early access, 'not enterprise-supported'. It has backends for file, HTTP(S), S3, GCS, Azure, OpenDAL, Nucleus and Omniverse Storage Service. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/ovstorage)
- [high] Kit SDK and kit-app-template releases: 110.3.0 (2026-08-28), 110.2.0 (2026-07-20), 110.1.0 (2026-04-06), 109.0.1 (2025-12-04, ARM64) and 108.0.0 (2025-08-12, which renamed 'Omniverse Cloud Streaming' to 'NVCF Streaming'). (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-app-
  template/main/CHANGELOG.md)
- [high] The Omniverse Launcher was deprecated on 2025-10-01. The Omniverse web-viewer-sample is no longer developed; users are directed to the OV Web SDK WebRTC sample. (https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/omniverse-launcher-update/321326 ; https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/web-viewer-sample)
- [high] Newton 1.0 launched in March 2026 (GTC). The latest release is v1.6.1 (2026-10-05). v1.4.0 added deterministic execution paths for bit-exact repeated rollouts and coupled solvers. The code is Apache-2.0 and the project is hosted by the Linux Foundation. (https://github.com/newton-
  physics/newton/releases ; https://github.com/newton-physics/newton ; https://dataconomy.com/2026/03/17/nvidia-launches-newton-1-0-physics-engine-for-industrial-robot-training/)
- [high] newton-usd-schemas are codeless extensions of UsdPhysics (Apache-2.0, v0.x, experimental). Solver-specific parameters go through schemas such as mjcPhysics. (https://github.com/newton-physics/newton-usd-schemas)
- [high] Isaac Lab 3.0.0-EA (2026-09-16) targets Isaac Sim 6.1, Newton 1.5.2, PyTorch 2.11 and Warp 1.16. Physics backends: isaacsim_physx, ovphysx, newton_mjwarp. Renderers: isaacsim_rtx, ovrtx, newton_renderer. It supports Kit-less mode, and its quaternion convention changed to (x,y,z,w).
  (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-EA)
- [high] Isaac Sim releases: 6.0.0 GA (June 2026), 6.1.0 GA (Sept 10, 2026), 7.0.0a1 (Sept 18, 2026). Source is Apache-2.0. Minimum GPU is RTX 4080 or A40; L40S is recommended. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/releases ; https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim)
- [high] ROS 2 Lyrical Luth was released 2026-05-22 as an LTS with EOL May 2031. Kilted EOL is Dec 2026, Jazzy May 2029, Humble May 2027. The next release, Makoa Mata-mata, is due May 2027. (https://github.com/ros2/ros2_documentation/blob/rolling/source/Releases.rst ;
  https://docs.ros.org/en/lyrical/Releases/Release-Lyrical-Luth.html)
- [medium] Lyrical adds Native Buffers support in the Fast DDS RMW for GPU-oriented transport. (https://discourse.openrobotics.org/t/ros-2-lyrical-luth-and-11-years-of-fast-dds-as-ros-2-default-middleware/55062 (search snippet))
- [high] ros_gz pairs Lyrical with Gazebo Jetty and Kilted with Ionic. Jetty is gz-sim 10 / SDFormat 16. (https://github.com/gazebosim/ros_gz ; https://github.com/gazebosim/sdformat/releases)
- [medium] The Isaac Sim ROS workspaces repo provides only humble_ws and jazzy_ws; Lyrical is not listed. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/IsaacSim-ros_workspaces)
- [high] The rosbag2 default storage plugin is MCAP. MCAP is MIT-licensed with C++, Go, Python, JS/TS, Swift and Rust libraries. (https://github.com/ros2/rosbag2 ; https://github.com/foxglove/mcap)
- [high] LeRobotDataset v3.0 puts many episodes per Parquet/MP4 file and reconstructs episodes from metadata. It supports StreamingLeRobotDataset from the Hub, and dataset.finalize() is required before publishing. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/lerobot/main/docs/source/lerobot-
  dataset-v3.mdx)
- [high] FMI 3.0.2 (2024-11-27) is the latest FMI release; no FMI 3.1 has been published. OpenModelica plans FMI 3 support for 1.28.0. (https://github.com/modelica/fmi-standard/releases ; https://github.com/OpenModelica/OpenModelica/releases)
- [high] NVIDIA OSMO is open source under Apache-2.0 and Kubernetes-native, covering Isaac SDG, training, RL and HIL workflows. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/OSMO)
- [medium] KAI Scheduler (Apache-2.0) offers gang scheduling, GPU sharing/fractions, hierarchical queues with DRF, and topology-aware scheduling. v0.18.x (Sept-Oct 2026) added NvFractions GPU-memory sharing and DRA accounting. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/KAI-Scheduler ; https://github.com/NVIDIA/KAI-
  Scheduler/releases)
- [high] In the NVIDIA DRA GPU driver, ComputeDomains are officially supported, but DRA GPU allocation is 'not yet officially supported' and is disabled by default. It requires K8s 1.32 or newer. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/k8s-dra-driver-gpu)
- [high] lakeFS v1.87.0 changed its license from Apache 2.0 to Business Source License 1.1. (https://github.com/treeverse/lakeFS/releases/tag/v1.87.0)
- [high] UE Pixel Streaming 2 was introduced in UE 5.5. The infrastructure repo marks UE5.8 current, UE5.7 supported, UE5.6 end-of-life; it is MIT with a signalling server and SFU. (https://github.com/EpicGamesExt/PixelStreamingInfrastructure)
