- [medium] Babylon.js 9.26 added an OpenUSD WebAssembly loader; the latest is 9.29.0 (Oct 2026). PlayCanvas 2.23.0 adds distance-based Gaussian-splat LOD and streamed loading. (https://github.com/BabylonJS/Babylon.js/releases ; https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases)
- [high] The NVIDIA DSX AI-factory digital-twin blueprint uses a Kit app with WebRTC livestream (omni.kit.livestream.app), a React portal over a messaging extension, and an AI agent HTTP API. It deploys on Omniverse Kit App Streaming (K8s) or NVCF and requires an RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell.
  (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse-blueprints/omniverse-dsx-blueprint-for-ai-factories)
- [high] NVIDIA kit-usd-agents (Apache-2.0) ships Kit MCP (12 tools), USD Code MCP (7), OmniUI MCP (10), Isaac Sim MCP (5) and Chat USD. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-usd-agents)
- [high] Community MCPs expose arbitrary code execution: blender-mcp's execute_blender_code (README warns it is 'potentially dangerous') and isaac-sim-mcp's arbitrary Python script execution. (https://github.com/ahujasid/blender-mcp ; https://github.com/omni-mcp/isaac-sim-mcp)
- [medium] glTF KHR_physics_rigid_bodies remains at 'Review Draft'. The extension index lists KHR_gaussian_splatting and KHR_interactivity among ratified extensions. (https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions)
- [low] H100/A100/B200-class datacenter GPUs lack RT cores and are unsuitable for Isaac Sim/RTX rendering. L40S has no MIG. RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell supports MIG (reportedly up to 4 instances). (General NVIDIA product knowledge; NVIDIA domains were egress-blocked this session)
## Quantitative data
- AWS us-east-1 on-demand, g6e.xlarge (1x L40S 48GB): $1.861/h (spot about $1.84) (SkyPilot catalog https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog (catalogs/v7/aws/vms.csv), fetched 2026-10-06)
- AWS us-east-1 on-demand, g7e.2xlarge (1x RTX PRO Server 6000, 96GB): $3.363/h (spot $1.91-2.20) (SkyPilot AWS catalog)
- AWS us-east-1 on-demand, g7e.48xlarge (8x RTX PRO 6000): $33.14/h, about $24.2k/month at 730 h (spot $13.6-26.5/h) (SkyPilot AWS catalog)
- AWS us-east-1 on-demand, p5.4xlarge (1x H100 80GB) / p5.48xlarge (8x H100): $6.88/h / $55.04/h, about $40.2k/month (spot about $3.4/GPU-h) (SkyPilot AWS catalog)
- AWS us-east-1 on-demand, g6.xlarge (1x L4) / p4d.24xlarge (8x A100 40GB): $0.805/h / $21.96/h (SkyPilot AWS catalog)
- AWS Seoul (ap-northeast-2) on-demand: L40S g6e.xlarge, RTX PRO 6000 g7e.2xlarge, 8x H100 p5.48xlarge, L4 g6.xlarge: $2.288/h, $4.135/h, $75.96/h, $0.990/h (Seoul about 23-38% above us-east-1; L40S spot about $1.25) (SkyPilot AWS catalog)
- Newton v1.6.0 hydroelastic contact speedup: 33-37% faster (https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases)
- Newton v1.2.0 SolverMuJoCo throughput gain: about 10% (https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases)
- OpenUSD 26.08 composition load time in heavy production rigs: up to 80% reduction; SdfPathExpression glob matching 5x-20x faster (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PixarAnimationStudios/OpenUSD/release/CHANGELOG.md)
- ROS 2 support windows: Lyrical to May 2031; Jazzy to May 2029; Humble to May 2027; Kilted to Dec 2026 (https://github.com/ros2/ros2_documentation/blob/rolling/source/Releases.rst)
- Isaac Sim hardware floor: RTX 4080 (local) or A40 (datacenter) minimum; RTX 5080 / L40S recommended; Python 3.12 (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim)
- NVIDIA fluid-sim digital-twin blueprint, standard profile: 2x RTX GPUs with 40GB or more each (or 1x 80GB or more), 128 GB RAM, 32 cores; lite profile 1x 16GB RTX (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse-blueprints/digital-twins-for-fluid-simulation)
- TimescaleDB columnstore compression: 90%+ typical (https://github.com/timescale/timescaledb)
- Kit major-version cadence: 108.0 (2025-08), 109.0.1 (2025-12), 110.1 (2026-04), 110.3 (2026-08): about 3 majors in 12 months (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-app-template/main/CHANGELOG.md)
- Community MCP adoption (GitHub stars): blender-mcp about 30k; unreal-mcp 2.1k; ros-mcp-server 1.5k; Omniverse kit-app-template 1.1k; PhysX 4.8k; ovrtx 226 (GitHub repo pages listed in options)
- Estimated cost of an interactive RTX streaming session (derived): About $1.86-2.29/h on a dedicated L40S. About $0.84/h if an RTX PRO 6000 ($3.36/h) is split 4-way by MIG, assuming a 24GB slice suffices (MIG profile unverified). $0 for client-side WebGPU sessions. (Derived from SkyPilot AWS
  catalog prices; MIG assumption is low confidence)


# TOPIC: training
## Options
- **NVIDIA Isaac Lab 3.0 (EA) / 2.3.2 (stable)** [RL/IL training framework + simulator integration] | status: v3.0.0-EA released 2026-09-16 (Isaac Sim 6.1, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.11, Warp 1.16, Newton 1.5.2), GA targeted end of Oct 2026; v3.0.0-beta ~2026-03-17 (GTC week), beta2 2026-06-17; last 2.x
  = v2.3.2 (2026-02-02), PyPI isaaclab 2.3.2.post1 (2026-02-11); isaacsim 6.1.0.0 on PyPI 2026-09-09. Owner: NVIDIA, open source on GitHub. | license: Isaac Lab framework BSD-3; isaaclab_mimic Apache-2.0; full workflows need Isaac Sim, whose source is Apache-2.0 but whose Kit/Omniverse binaries
  require accepting the proprietary Omniverse license terms (the PyPI wheel says 'NVIDIA Proprietary Software'). Kit-less Newton workflows do not need Isaac Sim. Free to use; check the terms for multi-tenant cloud hosting with NVIDIA. | strengths: Multi-backend physics (PhysX, OVPhysX, Newton
  MuJoCo-Warp/VBD/MPM) with guidance on moving policies between PhysX and Newton in both directions; Kit-less mode; zero-copy Warp/PyTorch data; 5 pluggable visualizers (Newton GL/RTX, Kit, Viser, Rerun), browser-friendly; One `isaaclab` training command over RSL-RL, skrl, RL-Games, SB3, plus RLinf
  for VLA post-training; multi-GPU and multi-node; 30+ ready-to-train environments, 16+ robots, tiled cameras, IMU, contact and ray-caster sensors, deformables; Mimic data generation, SkillGen (cuRobo), DexSuite with ADR/PBT, Isaac Teleop (CloudXR, MCAP record/replay), 17 agent skills for LLM-driven
  environment authoring; Published benchmark on one RTX 4090: G1 rough 82k steps/s and Shadow cube 170k steps/s including training; 4 nodes × 4 L40: 960k and 1.8M | weaknesses: Breaking 3.0 changes (quaternion WXYZ→XYZW, ProxyArray returns, actuator API move, built-in teleop deprecated); 3.0 is EA,
  not GA, as of 2026-10-06; Rendering paths depend on proprietary Omniverse; heavy install; NVIDIA lock-in; cuRobo (used by SkillGen) has proprietary license terms | best fit: Default RL/IL training and synthetic-data backbone for CEN: robots, humanoids, dexterous hands, AMRs, drones
  (multirotor/thruster actuators added in 2.3.2) | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-EA https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-
  learning/performance_benchmarks.rst
- **mjlab (MuJoCo Warp + Isaac-Lab-style manager API)** [RL training framework (lightweight, MuJoCo-native)] | status: v1.6.0 on PyPI 2026-08-09; MuJoCo/MuJoCo-Warp 3.15.0 released 2026-10-05; paper arXiv 2601.22074 (Zakka, Liao, Yi, Le Lay, Sreenath, Abbeel, 2026). Maintained by mujocolab (UC
  Berkeley/DeepMind-affiliated researchers). | license: Apache-2.0 (some utilities forked from Isaac Lab under BSD-3). Free; no proprietary runtime. | strengths: Same manager-based environment design as Isaac Lab, so skills and configs transfer; Minimal dependencies (uv), direct access to MuJoCo
  data structures, multi-GPU (--gpu-ids); No Omniverse EULA, which suits multi-tenant SaaS and on-prem customers; G1 velocity and motion-imitation (BeyondMimic-style) tasks built in; W&B integration | weaknesses: NVIDIA GPU needed for training (macOS only for evaluation); Fewer photoreal sensors and
  less synthetic-data tooling than Isaac Sim RTX; Smaller task zoo | best fit: Second, license-clean backend for locomotion and whole-body control jobs, and the CPU/macOS-friendly evaluation path | sources: https://github.com/mujocolab/mjlab
  https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mujocolab/mjlab/main/README.md https://pypi.org/project/mjlab/ https://pypi.org/project/mujoco-warp/
- **Newton physics engine (Linux Foundation)** [GPU physics backend for training] | status: v1.6.1 on 2026-10-05; v1.0 early 2026 (PyPI newton-physics 1.0.0, 2026-02-27); started by Disney Research, Google DeepMind and NVIDIA; ships with Isaac Lab 3.0 (Newton 1.5.2). | license: Apache-2.0 (docs CC-
  BY-4.0). Free. | strengths: Several solvers in one engine: MuJoCo-Warp, VBD (cloth/deformables), MPM, Style3D cloth, IPC, experimental Kamino; solvers can be coupled; Differentiable; CUDA-graph deterministic replay; OpenUSD/MJCF import; RL solver-reset APIs (1.3.0); Vendor-neutral governance
  lowers lock-in risk | weaknesses: Fast-moving 1.x API (monthly releases); Contact-rich fidelity versus PhysX still needs per-task validation | best fit: Physics backend for RL in Isaac Lab 3.0 Kit-less mode; deformables and cloth tasks | sources: https://github.com/newton-physics/newton
  https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases https://pypi.org/project/newton-physics/
- **rsl_rl (ETH RSL)** [RL algorithm library] | status: v5.5.1 on 2026-09-09 (bf16 mixed precision in 5.5.0; torch.compile in 5.1; Beta distribution 5.3; LogWriter 5.4; better multi-GPU gradient reduction). | license: BSD-3-Clause. Free. | strengths: De facto PPO for legged and humanoid robots;
  distillation (teacher→student), symmetry, RND; Fastest in Isaac Lab comparison: Isaac-Humanoid-v0, 4096 envs × 32 × 500 iterations in 198 s on RTX 4090; Multi-GPU; default in Isaac Lab and mjlab | weaknesses: Narrow algorithm set (PPO + distillation); Sparse docs, per Isaac Lab's own comparison |
  best fit: Default for locomotion, whole-body control and dexterous PPO jobs | sources: https://github.com/leggedrobotics/rsl_rl/releases https://pypi.org/project/rsl-rl-lib/ https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/rl_frameworks.rst
- **skrl** [RL algorithm library] | status: v2.1.0 on 2026-05-10; actively developed. | license: MIT. Free. | strengths: PyTorch, JAX and NVIDIA Warp implementations; Many algorithms, including multi-agent (IPPO/MAPPO); Supports Isaac Lab, MuJoCo Playground, ManiSkill, Gymnasium, PettingZoo; good
  docs; 201 s on the Isaac Lab humanoid benchmark | weaknesses: Smaller community | best fit: Multi-agent (fleets, multi-robot), off-policy (SAC/TD3) and JAX users | sources: https://github.com/Toni-SM/skrl https://pypi.org/project/skrl/
- **RL-Games** [RL algorithm library] | status: v1.6.5 on 2026-02-20; low release cadence. | license: MIT. | strengths: Highly optimized PPO/SAC; history of dexterous work (IsaacGymEnvs, DexPBT, DextrAH); 201 s on the Isaac Lab humanoid benchmark | weaknesses: Low documentation; slowing maintenance

- **Stable-Baselines3** [RL algorithm library] | status: v2.9.0 on 2026-06-15. | license: MIT. | strengths: Largest community, extensive docs, many algorithms; good for teaching and non-vectorized custom envs | weaknesses: No GPU-vectorized or distributed training: 287 s vs 198 s (rsl_rl) on the

- **BlenderProc 2** [Synthetic perception data (Blender)] | status: PyPI 2.8.0 (2024-10-22); repo active but release cadence slow. | license: GPL-3.0. | strengths: BOP/COCO export; Cycles path tracing; easy pose sampling | weaknesses: GPL-3.0 (fine as a server-side tool, risky if distributed); Not
  GPU-batched; separate from the physics twin | best fit: Optional renderer for object-pose (BOP) datasets | sources: https://github.com/DLR-RM/BlenderProc https://pypi.org/project/blenderproc/
- **Kubric** [Synthetic video data (Blender + PyBullet)] | status: PyPI 0.1.1 (2021); pinned to Blender 2.93. | license: Apache-2.0. | strengths: Rich annotations (flow, point tracks) | weaknesses: Stale stack | best fit: Do not adopt; reference only | sources: https://github.com/google-
  research/kubric https://pypi.org/project/kubric/
- **Auto-labeling: Meta SAM 3 / SAM 3.1 (+ VLM annotators such as Cosmos Reason2 and LeRobot VLM subtask annotation)** [Auto-labeling] | status: SAM 3 (2025, 848M, text/visual-prompt concept segmentation + tracking, 75–80% of human on SA-CO); SAM 3.1 Object Multiplex 2026-03-27 (faster multi-object
  tracking). | license: SAM License (custom; gated checkpoints). | strengths: Open-vocabulary detect/segment/track for labeling real data to close the sim2real loop | weaknesses: Custom license needs legal review for SaaS | best fit: Real-data auto-labeling inside the CEN marketplace pipeline |
  sources: https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3 https://github.com/huggingface/lerobot/releases
- **Detectors for synthetic-data training: RF-DETR vs Ultralytics YOLO26** [Perception model training] | status: RF-DETR N–L Apache-2.0 (COCO AP50:95 48.4–56.5 at 2.3–6.8 ms on T4 TensorRT FP16); XL/2XL under PML 1.0. Ultralytics YOLO26 current, YOLO27 in preview. | license: RF-DETR N–L Apache-2.0;
  Ultralytics AGPL-3.0 or paid Enterprise license. | strengths: Both export to ONNX/TensorRT for Jetson | weaknesses: Ultralytics AGPL is incompatible with closed SaaS unless an enterprise license is bought | best fit: RF-DETR (and RT-DETR/D-FINE-class Apache models) as CEN default detectors |
  sources: https://github.com/roboflow/rf-detr https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics
- **Evaluation suites: Isaac Lab Arena, LIBERO/LIBERO-plus, RoboCasa365, SimplerEnv, BEHAVIOR-1K, ManiSkill3, RoboTwin 2.0, RoboArena** [Benchmarks / closed-loop evaluation] | status: Isaac Lab Arena v0.3 alpha (Apache-2.0; GPU-parallel heterogeneous evaluation; GR00T/pi0.5/DreamZero via client-
  server; RoboFinals, RoboCasa 138+, LIBERO, RoboTwin 2.0; LeRobot EnvHub). RoboCasa365 v1.0 2026-02-18 / v1.0.1 2026-05-12: 365 tasks, 2,500+ scenes, 3,200+ objects, 600+ h human + 1,600+ h robot data, MIT/CC-BY-4.0. SimplerEnv (MIT; real2sim correlation via MMRV/Pearson). BEHAVIOR-1K (1,000
  activities, OmniGibson on Isaac Sim, MIT). ManiSkill3 3.0.1 (2026-04-21; RGBD+seg at 30,000+ FPS on a 4090; assets CC BY-NC). RoboArena (MIT; distributed real-world DROID evaluation). libero 0.1.1 (2025-11). | license: Mostly MIT/Apache; check asset licenses (ManiSkill CC BY-NC 4.0). | strengths:
  Standard comparability; Arena enables CEN-hosted leaderboards | weaknesses: LIBERO is saturated (about 97–99%), so harder suites are needed (LIBERO-plus, RoboCasa365, BEHAVIOR) plus real-world evaluation; Arena still alpha | best fit: Day-1 evaluation harness = Arena + LIBERO-plus + RoboCasa365;
  marketplace leaderboards later | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab-Arena https://github.com/robocasa/robocasa https://github.com/simpler-env/SimplerEnv https://github.com/StanfordVL/BEHAVIOR-1K
- **Humanoid whole-body controller references: NVIDIA GR00T-WholeBodyControl (GEAR-SONIC, Decoupled WBC), NVlabs HOVER, BeyondMimic** [Reference training recipes (humanoid WBC)] | status: GEAR-SONIC: motion-tracking foundation controller, Bones-SEED 142K+ motions (~288 h), 64+ GPUs recommended for
  fine-tuning, ONNX/TensorRT C++ on Jetson, 50 Hz latent tokens; first release Nov 2025, updated Aug 2026. HOVER: Isaac Lab 2.0 extension, teacher 100k iterations = 23.3 h on RTX 4090 / 44.6 h L40 / 52.8 h A6000, student 10k iterations = 0.27 h on 4090, 4096+ envs. BeyondMimic: Isaac Lab, LAFAN1,
  ONNX CPU inference in ROS 2 Jazzy on G1. | license: SONIC code Apache-2.0, weights NVIDIA Open Model License; HOVER Apache-2.0; BeyondMimic MIT. | strengths: Proven sim2real recipes (teacher-student, motion tracking) to package as templates | weaknesses: G1/H1-specific; foundation-scale training
  is expensive | best fit: Humanoid WBC templates in CEN's training catalog | sources: https://github.com/NVlabs/GR00T-WholeBodyControl https://github.com/NVlabs/HOVER https://github.com/HybridRobotics/whole_body_tracking https://github.com/HybridRobotics/motion_tracking_controller
- **Dexterous references: NVlabs DextrAH (G/RGB), Isaac Lab DexSuite** [Reference training recipes (dexterous)] | status: DextrAH: Kuka-Allegro; privileged RL teacher (4096 envs) → online distillation to camera student (256 envs) with torch.distributed multi-GPU; geometric fabrics. DexSuite with
  ADR/PBT in Isaac Lab 2.3. | license: DextrAH license not verified; DexSuite inside Isaac Lab (BSD-3). | strengths: End-to-end pixels-to-action dexterous recipe | weaknesses: Multi-GPU, multi-day compute; rendering-bound student | best fit: Dexterous-hand template (tier-2 jobs) | sources:
  https://github.com/NVlabs/DEXTRAH https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases

Not verified (memory, medium): Thor GA Aug 2025, dev kit about US$3,499, up to 2,070 FP4 TFLOPS, 128

  dataset ↔ checkpoint ↔ evaluation) must be built | best fit: MLflow (self-host) as registry + optional W&B; Ray/KubeRay + torchrun; ONNX→TensorRT packager for Jetson | sources: https://pypi.org/project/mlflow/ https://pypi.org/project/wandb/ https://pypi.org/project/ray/

## Key facts
- [high] Isaac Lab 3.0.0 Early Access was released 2026-09-16 for Isaac Sim 6.1 / Python 3.12 / PyTorch 2.11 / Warp 1.16 / Newton 1.5.2; GA is targeted for end of October 2026. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-EA)
- [high] Isaac Lab 3.0 has a unified `isaaclab` training command supporting RSL-RL, skrl, RL-Games, Stable-Baselines3 and RLinf (VLA post-training), multi-GPU and multi-node RL, Kit-less Newton workflows, and 17 agent skills; breaking changes include quaternion WXYZ→XYZW and ProxyArray data returns.
  (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-EA)
- [high] Isaac Lab framework is BSD-3, isaaclab_mimic is Apache-2.0; full workflows need Isaac Sim, whose binaries carry the proprietary Omniverse license terms (PyPI isaacsim/isaaclab wheels are labeled 'NVIDIA Proprietary Software'); Kit-less Newton workflows do not need Isaac Sim.
  (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab ; https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim ; https://pypi.org/project/isaacsim/)
- [high] Latest stable PyPI isaaclab is 2.3.2.post1 (2026-02-11); isaacsim 6.1.0.0 published 2026-09-09. (https://pypi.org/project/isaaclab/ ; https://pypi.org/project/isaacsim/)
- [high] Isaac Lab RTX 4090 benchmark (step+inference+train FPS): Cartpole 510k; Cartpole RGB camera 32k; G1 rough velocity 82k (4096 envs); Shadow cube repose 170k (8192 envs). On 4 nodes × 4 L40: 3.5M / 260k / 960k / 1.8M. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-
  sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- [high] Isaac-Humanoid-v0 (4096 envs × 32 steps × 500 iterations) on RTX 4090: RSL-RL 198 s, RL-Games 201 s, skrl 201 s, SB3 287 s (SB3 lacks vectorized/distributed training). (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/rl_frameworks.rst)
- [high] mjlab 1.6.0 (Apache-2.0) released 2026-08-09; MuJoCo and MuJoCo-Warp 3.15.0 released 2026-10-05; Newton reached v1.6.1 on 2026-10-05 (Linux Foundation, Apache-2.0, initiated by Disney Research, Google DeepMind, NVIDIA). (https://pypi.org/project/mjlab/ ; https://pypi.org/project/mujoco-
  warp/ ; https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases ; https://github.com/newton-physics/newton)
- [high] Current versions: rsl_rl 5.5.1 (2026-09-09, BSD-3), skrl 2.1.0 (2026-05-10, MIT; PyTorch/JAX/Warp), RL-Games 1.6.5 (2026-02-20), SB3 2.9.0 (2026-06-15), TorchRL 0.14.0 (2026-09-10), Brax 0.14.2 (2026-03-15), RLinf 0.3.0 (2026-07-15), PufferLib 3.0.0 on PyPI (GitHub branch 5.0).
  (https://pypi.org/project/rsl-rl-lib/ ; https://pypi.org/project/skrl/ ; https://pypi.org/project/torchrl/ ; https://pypi.org/project/rlinf/ ; https://github.com/PufferAI/PufferLib)
- [high] LeanRL was archived on 2026-10-01 (read-only). (https://github.com/meta-pytorch/LeanRL)
- [high] LeRobot 0.6.1 (Apache-2.0) released 2026-08-03; supports ACT, Diffusion, Pi0, Pi0.5, Pi0-Fast, SmolVLA, GR00T N1.7, X-VLA, Wall-X, EO-1, HIL-SERL; benchmarks include RoboCasa365, RoboTwin 2.0, LIBERO-plus, VLABench and Isaac Lab Arena integration. (https://pypi.org/project/lerobot/ ;
  https://github.com/huggingface/lerobot/releases)
- [high] LeRobotDataset v3.0 aggregates many episodes into Parquet (state/action) and MP4 (per-camera) shards with metadata-driven episode reconstruction and Hub streaming (StreamingLeRobotDataset). (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/lerobot/main/docs/source/lerobot-dataset-v3.mdx)
- [high] GR00T N1.7 is GA: 3B parameters, Cosmos-Reason2-2B (Qwen3-VL) backbone, flow-matching DiT action head, 20K hours of EgoScale human video in pretraining; fine-tune on 40 GB+ GPUs; inference on 16 GB+ including Jetson AGX Thor/Orin (JetPack 7.2); code Apache-2.0, weights NVIDIA Open Model
  License. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Isaac-GR00T)
- [high] openpi releases pi0, pi0-FAST and pi0.5 (pi0.5 + PyTorch in Sept 2025); inference >8 GB, LoRA >22.5 GB, full fine-tune >70 GB; pi0.5-LIBERO (30k steps) averages 96.85%. No pi0.6 / pi*0.6 weights in the repo. (https://github.com/Physical-Intelligence/openpi ; https://github.com/Physical-
  Intelligence/openpi/blob/main/examples/libero/README.md)
- [high] SmolVLA (450M) fine-tuning for 20k steps takes about 4 hours on one A100; recommended 50+ episodes. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/lerobot/main/docs/source/smolvla.mdx)
- [high] OpenVLA-OFT LIBERO fine-tuning uses 8 GPUs (A100/H100), batch 8/GPU (~62 GB), 150k steps, LoRA rank 32; about 97% average success (combined model 96.8%). OpenVLA LoRA uses about 72 GB on one A100. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/moojink/openvla-oft/main/LIBERO.md ;
  https://github.com/openvla/openvla)
- [high] Isaac Lab Mimic: 10 human demos (Franka) / 5 (GR1) → 1,000 generated demos in 18–40 min (state) or about 10 h (visuomotor); BC-RNN training about 30 min (state) / about 6 h (visuomotor); Apple Vision Pro hand tracking supported as a teleop device. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-
  sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/imitation-learning/teleop_imitation.rst)
- [high] MimicGen and DexMimicGen code is under the NVIDIA Source Code License (datasets CC-BY-4.0); SkillGen in Isaac Lab depends on cuRobo, which has proprietary license terms. (https://github.com/NVlabs/mimicgen ; https://github.com/NVlabs/dexmimicgen ; https://github.com/isaac-
  sim/IsaacLab/releases)
- [high] HOVER humanoid teacher (100k iterations) takes 23.3 h on RTX 4090 / 44.6 h on L40 / 52.8 h on RTX A6000; student distillation (10k iterations) 0.27 h on RTX 4090; 4096+ envs on a single GPU. (https://github.com/NVlabs/HOVER)
- [high] GEAR-SONIC (GR00T whole-body control) recommends 64+ GPUs for fine-tuning from released checkpoints; data: Bones-SEED 142K+ motions (~288 h); deployment ONNX + TensorRT on Jetson at 50 Hz. (https://github.com/NVlabs/GR00T-WholeBodyControl)
- [high] Cosmos 3 (released May 2026; Transfer2.5 README says launched June 1, 2026) has Super 64B (H200/B200/GB200), Nano 16B (RTX PRO 6000/H100/B200) and Edge 4B (Jetson Orin/Thor, July 2026) under OpenMDW-1.1, plus DROID policy variants; Cosmos-Predict2.5 and Transfer2.5 are no longer actively
  developed. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos ; https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos/cosmos-predict2.5 ; https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos/cosmos-transfer2.5)
- [high] V-JEPA 2 was released 2025-06-25 and V-JEPA 2.1 on 2026-03-16 (up to 2B); V-JEPA 2-AC zero-shot Franka: reach 100%, grasp 60%, pick-and-place 80%; mostly MIT. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/vjepa2)
- [high] Isaac Lab Arena is alpha v0.3 (Apache-2.0), evaluates GR00T, pi0.5 and DreamZero via client-server, and integrates the LeRobot Environment Hub; published benchmarks: RoboFinals, RoboCasa (138+ tasks), LIBERO, RoboTwin 2.0. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab-Arena)
- [high] RoboCasa365 (v1.0 2026-02-18): 365 tasks, 2,500+ scenes, 3,200+ objects, 600+ h human demos, 1,600+ h robot data; code MIT, assets CC-BY-4.0. (https://github.com/robocasa/robocasa)
- [high] Korean startup RLWRLD released RLDX-1 (6.9B/8.1B VLA, Qwen3-VL + MSAT) on 2026-05-06 with LIBERO 97.8% / LIBERO-Plus 86.7% / SIMPLER Google-VM 81.5%; weights are non-commercial. (https://github.com/RLWRLD/RLDX-1)
- [high] AgiBot World Beta has 1,003,672 trajectories; GO-1 is CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 (non-commercial); full fine-tune about 70 GB, action-expert-only about 24 GB. (https://github.com/OpenDriveLab/AgiBot-World)
- [high] Gemini Robotics On-Device is available via the Safari SDK (v2.4.1+) to Trusted Testers only; the SDK covers serving, evaluation, data upload and fine-tuning. (https://github.com/google-deepmind/gemini-robotics-sdk)
- [medium] Gemini Robotics 1.5 and ER 1.5 were announced in Sep 2025, with ER 1.5 offered through the Gemini API; pi*0.6 (RECAP) was announced about Nov 2025 without open weights; Figure Helix (Feb 2025) uses a 7B System-2 VLM + 80M 200 Hz System-1; Genie 3 announced Aug 2025 (720p/24 fps real-
  time). (From memory; deepmind.google, pi.website and figure.ai were blocked by the network proxy)
- [medium] Jetson AGX Thor became generally available in Aug 2025 (dev kit about US$3,499; up to 2,070 FP4 TFLOPS; 128 GB; 40–130 W). (From memory; nvidia.com blocked. GR00T repo confirms Thor support on JetPack 7.2/CUDA 13.2: https://github.com/NVIDIA/Isaac-GR00T)
- [medium] legged_gym trains ANYmal flat-terrain locomotion in under 4 min and rough terrain in about 20 min on one workstation GPU with 4096 envs (Rudin et al., CoRL 2021); the repo is now deprecated in favor of Isaac Lab. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.11978 (blocked; from memory) ;
  https://github.com/leggedrobotics/legged_gym (deprecation verified))
- [medium] Isaac Sim RTX rendering requires GPUs with RT cores; A100/H100-class data-center GPUs without RT cores are not supported for rendering workloads. (From memory of Isaac Sim system requirements (docs blocked))
- [high] ManiSkill3 renders RGBD + segmentation at 30,000+ FPS on an RTX 4090; ManiSkill assets are CC BY-NC 4.0. (https://github.com/haosulab/ManiSkill)
- [high] Ultralytics YOLO is AGPL-3.0 (enterprise license needed for closed commercial use); RF-DETR N–L are Apache-2.0 (XL/2XL under PML 1.0); BlenderProc is GPL-3.0; Kubric is stale (Blender 2.93, PyPI 2021). (https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics ; https://github.com/roboflow/rf-detr ;
  https://github.com/DLR-RM/BlenderProc ; https://github.com/google-research/kubric)
## Quantitative data
- Isaac Lab G1 rough-terrain velocity, RTX 4090, 4096 envs: env-step / step+inference / step+inference+train FPS: 94,000 / 88,000 / 82,000 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- Isaac Lab Shadow-hand cube repose, RTX 4090, 8192 envs: train FPS (single) vs 4 nodes × 4 L40: 170,000 vs 1,800,000 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- Isaac Lab camera-based task penalty (Cartpole state vs RGB camera, train FPS, RTX 4090): 510,000 vs 32,000 (~16x slower with cameras) (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- RL library wall-clock, Isaac-Humanoid-v0, 4096×32×500, RTX 4090: RSL-RL 198 s; RL-Games 201 s; skrl 201 s; SB3 287 s (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/rl_frameworks.rst)
- Quadruped locomotion policy (ANYmal, 4096 envs, single GPU): <4 min flat, ~20 min rough terrain (legged_gym); my estimate for Isaac Lab 3.0 on RTX 4090/L40S: 0.3–1 GPU-h per run, 20–60 GPU-h per project including sweeps (~US$20–60 at ~$1/GPU-h) (https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.11978 (memory, medium) +
  derived from Isaac Lab benchmark)
- Humanoid velocity-tracking controller (G1 rough, PPO): Estimate: 300–500M samples at 82k FPS ≈ 1–1.7 h on one RTX 4090; 50–150 GPU-h per project with DR/reward sweeps (Derived from the Isaac Lab performance benchmark (estimate))
- Humanoid generalist motion-tracking WBC (HOVER, H1): Teacher 100k iterations: 23.3 h RTX 4090 / 44.6 h L40 / 52.8 h A6000; student 10k iterations: 0.27 h RTX 4090 (https://github.com/NVlabs/HOVER)
- Humanoid foundation WBC fine-tuning (GEAR-SONIC): 64+ GPUs recommended; data 142K+ motions (~288 h); my estimate for full pretraining: ≥10,000 GPU-h (low confidence) (https://github.com/NVlabs/GR00T-WholeBodyControl)
- Dexterous in-hand reorientation (state-based): Estimate: 2–5B samples at ~170k FPS ≈ 3–8 h on one RTX 4090; vision-based teacher→student (DextrAH-style, 4096-env teacher, 256-env camera student, multi-GPU) ≈ 200–600 GPU-h (low-medium confidence) (Derived from
  https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst and https://github.com/NVlabs/DEXTRAH)
- Isaac Lab Mimic data multiplication: 10 human demos → 1,000 demos in 18–40 min (state) / ~10 h (visuomotor); generation success ~50% (Franka), 65–82% (GR1) (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/imitation-learning/teleop_imitation.rst)
- BC policy training on Mimic data (robomimic BC-RNN): ~30 min state-based, ~6 h visuomotor (Franka stack); ~29 min GR1 pick-place on RTX 6000 Ada; success 40–60% (Franka), 75–86% (GR1) (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/main/docs/source/overview/imitation-
  learning/teleop_imitation.rst)
- SmolVLA fine-tune: 20k steps ≈ 4 h on 1× A100 (≈4 A100-h); 450M params; ≥50 episodes (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/lerobot/main/docs/source/smolvla.mdx)
- pi0 / pi0.5 memory (openpi): Inference >8 GB; LoRA fine-tune >22.5 GB (RTX 4090); full fine-tune >70 GB (A100/H100 80GB); LIBERO config 30k steps → 96.85% average (https://github.com/Physical-Intelligence/openpi)
- pi0.5 fine-tune compute (estimate): ~10–40 H100-h for a 30k-step full fine-tune; LoRA on 1× RTX 4090 over ~1–2 days (low-medium confidence) (Estimate based on openpi requirements)
- GR00T N1.7 fine-tune: 1+ GPUs with ≥40 GB; example 2,000 steps, global batch 32 on 1 or 8 GPUs; inference ≥16 GB incl. Jetson Thor/Orin; my estimate: 2–16 GPU-h per task (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Isaac-GR00T)
- OpenVLA-OFT LIBERO fine-tune: 8× A100/H100, ~62 GB/GPU, 150k steps; my estimate ≈ 200–400 GPU-h; ~97% LIBERO average (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/moojink/openvla-oft/main/LIBERO.md)
- VLA pretraining from scratch (reference; not recommended): OpenVLA: 970K trajectories, ~64 A100 × ~14 days (~21.5k A100-h, memory/medium); openpi bases pretrained on 10k+ h of robot data; GR00T N1.7 on 20K h of human video plus robot data (https://github.com/openvla/openvla ;
  https://github.com/Physical-Intelligence/openpi ; https://github.com/NVIDIA/Isaac-GR00T)
- Perception detector on synthetic data (estimate): Replicator SDG of 50k labeled RTX images ≈ 5–30 RT-GPU-h; RF-DETR-S/M training on 50k images × 50 epochs ≈ 10–40 A100-h; real-data fine-tune <2 GPU-h; deploy RF-DETR-N/S at 2.3–3.5 ms (T4, TensorRT FP16) (Estimate; latencies from
  https://github.com/roboflow/rf-detr)
- ManiSkill3 GPU-parallel rendering: RGBD + segmentation at 30,000+ FPS on RTX 4090 (https://github.com/haosulab/ManiSkill)
- RLinf throughput claims: 2.434x embodied-RL throughput vs existing frameworks; 25x end-to-end speedup for BEHAVIOR; ~4x DreamZero SFT throughput (https://github.com/RLinf/RLinf)
- LeanRL torch.compile + cudagraph speedups: PPO-Atari 6.8x, SAC 5.7x, TD3 3.4x, PPO-continuous 2.7x (https://github.com/meta-pytorch/LeanRL)
- Cosmos 3 model tiers: Super 64B (H200/B200/GB200), Nano 16B (RTX PRO 6000/H100/B200), Edge 4B (Jetson Orin/Thor) (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos)
- RoboCasa365 scale: 365 tasks, 2,500+ scenes, 3,200+ objects, 600+ h human demos, 1,600+ h robot data (https://github.com/robocasa/robocasa)
- AgiBot World Beta scale: 1,003,672 trajectories (~43.8T tokens) from 100 robots (https://github.com/OpenDriveLab/AgiBot-World)
- RDT2 data scale: 10,000+ h of UMI human manipulation video, 100+ scenes (https://github.com/thu-ml/RDT2)
- Indicative Day-1 training cluster (estimate, low confidence): 1× 8-GPU H100/H200 node (VLA/world-model training; ~US$15–25k/month cloud or ~US$250–400k capex) + 2× 8-GPU RT-core nodes (L40S/RTX PRO 6000) for sim/SDG/RL (~US$8–15k/month each cloud). Total ≈ US$30–55k/month cloud-equivalent
  (Estimate (cloud prices not verified))
- Training-module team (estimate): 8–12 FTE ≈ ₩1.2–1.8B/yr fully loaded in Korea (low confidence) (Estimate)

  projects. | strengths: Own the customer relationship and procurement | weaknesses: Limited robot-learning and synthetic-data depth | best fit: Channel partners or subcontract route into chaebols; risk that they build it in house. | sources: Prior knowledge, not re-verified

  (cuRobo), a cuMotion driver and a Docker 'Dart robot simulator' (updated Sep 2026), so it is already on the NVIDIA stack (verified). Prior knowledge (medium/low): Samsung became Rainbow's largest shareholder (~35%, 2025); LG took a majority of Bear Robotics (2025); production Atlas shown at CES
  2026 for HMG plants; HMG-NVIDIA ~USD 3B physical-AI cluster; Samsung/SK/HMG/Naver Blackwell allocations; HD Hyundai FOS intelligent-shipyard programme; MASGA shipbuilding cooperation. | license: Typical entry via paid PoC/NRE, then enterprise or on-prem licence. | strengths: Concentrated, well-

## Key facts
- [high] Isaac Sim latest stable is 6.1.0 (GitHub release Sep 10, 2026); 7.0.0 Alpha 1 was released Sep 18, 2026; 6.0.0 GA was Jun 4, 2026; 6.0.0-dev2 coincided with GTC 2026 (Mar 16, 2026). (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/releases)
- [high] The Isaac Sim GitHub repo is Apache-2.0, but the PyPI 'isaacsim' metapackage (6.1.0.0, Sep 9, 2026) is labelled 'Other/Proprietary License (NVIDIA Proprietary Software)'. The code is open, the runtime is proprietary. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim ;
  https://pypi.org/project/isaacsim/)
- [high] Isaac Sim GPU requirements: workstation RTX 4080 minimum (RTX 5080+ recommended); datacenter A40 minimum, L40S or RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell recommended. Rendering needs RT-core GPUs, which drives cloud cost structure. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim)
- [high] Isaac Lab 3.0 beta (Mar 17, 2026, GTC 2026 week) introduced multi-backend physics (PhysX default; Newton/MuJoCo-Warp backend that runs without Isaac Sim), pluggable renderers (Isaac RTX, OVRTX, Newton Warp), four visualizers (Kit, Newton, Rerun, Viser), Warp-native data, and breaking
  changes (quaternion WXYZ to XYZW). 3.0.0-EA followed in Sep 2026 for Isaac Sim 6.1. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-beta ; https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases)
- [high] Newton physics engine: Apache-2.0, Linux Foundation project, founding contributors Disney Research, Google DeepMind and NVIDIA; built on Warp with MuJoCo Warp as primary backend; v1.0.0 released Apr 13, 2026; v1.6.1 on Oct 5, 2026; ~5.7k stars. (https://github.com/newton-physics/newton ;
  https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases ; https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases?page=2)
- [high] MuJoCo 3.15.0 released Oct 5, 2026. Recent releases added an experimental IPC penetration-free flex contact (3.14), a discrete integrator (3.13), a PID actuator (3.12) and conveyor surface velocity/adhesion (3.11). (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/releases)
- [high] Genesis World has ~30k stars, is Apache-2.0, is officially supported by Genesis AI, published a 'Genesis World 1.0' blog in May 2026 and integrates rigid/FEM/MPM/particle/IPC solvers with the Nyx renderer. (https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/Genesis)
- [high] NVIDIA GR00T N1.7 is the current GA open VLA (Cosmos-Reason2-2B/Qwen3-VL backbone, relative end-effector action space, pretraining including 20K hours of EgoScale human video). Code is Apache-2.0; weights are under the NVIDIA Open Model License. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Isaac-GR00T)
- [high] NVIDIA Cosmos world foundation models: Predict2.5, Transfer2.5 and Reason2 repos are Apache-2.0 and were updated Jun 2026; the org also ships Cosmos-RL and Cosmos-Xenna data pipelines. (https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos)
- [high] NVIDIA's open-source usd-content-agents (Apache-2.0) automates material assignment, physics-property classification, articulation inference, texture and geometry generation, and validation for USD assets. This directly commoditizes manual SimReady asset conversion.
  (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/usd-content-agents)
- [high] NVIDIA is modularizing Omniverse into Kit-less libraries (ovphysx physics; ovrtx real-time sensor simulation and rendering) and CAD-to-USD virtual-facility samples (vfi-samples), all updated Oct 2026. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse)
- [high] Current NVIDIA Omniverse blueprints on GitHub: Cosmos Dataset Search, digital twins for fluid simulation (CFD), DSX blueprint for AI factories, Earth-2 weather analytics, and synthetic manipulation motion generation. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse-blueprints)
- [high] Physical Intelligence openpi (14.1k stars, Apache-2.0) offers pi0, pi0-FAST and pi0.5 open weights; pi0.5 and PyTorch support landed Sep 2025. (https://github.com/Physical-Intelligence/openpi)
- [high] Lightwheel publishes LW-BenchHub (Isaac Lab-Arena based, 268 tasks), SimReady assets free for non-commercial use, leisaac teleoperation (741 stars) and MJCF/USD converters. (https://github.com/lightwheelai)
- [high] ManiSkill3 (Hao Su lab, linked to Hillbot) code is Apache-2.0 but its assets are CC BY-NC 4.0 (non-commercial); it was published at RSS 2025. (https://github.com/haosulab/ManiSkill)
- [high] MORAI's open-source GitHub org is active in 2026: ROS2 message set updated Aug 2026 and a MORAI SIM v2.1 control example updated Sep 2026. (https://github.com/MORAI-Autonomous)
- [high] Doosan Robotics already integrates with the NVIDIA stack (Isaac driver with cuRobo, cuMotion driver) and ships a Docker Compose 'Dart robot simulator' (updated Sep 2026). (https://github.com/DoosanRobotics)
- [high] Microsoft Fabric 'digital twin builder' was still in preview as of its doc dated 2026-05-18 and is explicitly 'different from the Azure Digital Twins service'. No ADT retirement notice was found in the docs. (https://github.com/MicrosoftDocs/fabric-docs/blob/main/docs/real-time-
  intelligence/digital-twin-builder/overview.md ; https://github.com/MicrosoftDocs/azure-docs/tree/main/articles/digital-twins)
- [medium] Parallel Domain's public GitHub shows no active product SDK (step-sdk archived Oct 2023; recent repos are forks), so its product momentum is uncertain. (https://github.com/parallel-domain)
- [high] CARLA 0.10.0 (Dec 19, 2024) moved to Unreal Engine 5.5 with native ROS2; no newer major release is listed. (https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla/releases)
- [low] Digital twin market: MarketsandMarkets ~USD 21.1B (2025) to ~USD 149.8B (2030), CAGR ~47.9%; Grand View Research ~USD 25.0B (2024) to ~USD 155.8B (2030), CAGR ~34.2%. Earlier MnM edition: USD 10.1B (2023) to USD 110.1B (2028), 61.3%. Scope differences explain the gap. (Prior knowledge of

# TOPIC: korea_policy
## Options
- **MSS Deep-tech TIPS (딥테크 팁스)** [Non-dilutive R&D + VC co-investment (startup-only)] | status: Run by MSS/KOSME/TIPS operators since 2023 for deep-tech fields including AI, robotics and mobility. 2026 continuation assumed; 2026 rule changes not verified. | license: Up to about KRW 1.5B R&D over 3
  years. Requires a TIPS operator (AC/VC) to invest at least about KRW 0.3B (equity dilution). Standard national-R&D matching share and 기술료 (royalty) rules apply. | strengths: Largest startup-only R&D ticket; Rolling recommendation through operators rather than one annual call; Signals deep-tech
  status to VCs and to the Super-Gap/Post-TIPS follow-ons | weaknesses: Requires an equity round with an operator; Competitive; strong technical-novelty bar; Funds can only be spent on R&D cost categories | best fit: Core platform R&D: physics/sensor-fidelity layer, synthetic data engine, robot-
  learning pipeline | sources: https://www.jointips.or.kr https://www.mss.go.kr https://www.smtech.go.kr
- **Super-Gap Startup 1000+ (초격차 스타트업 1000+, DIPS)** [Non-dilutive commercialization grant (startup-only)] | status: MSS program for 2023–2027 targeting 1,000 deep-tech startups across 10 fields: big data/AI, robotics, future mobility, aerospace and others. Annual call is usually Q1; the 2027 call
  is expected around Feb–Mar 2027 (unverified). | license: Up to about KRW 0.6B commercialization funding over up to 3 years, plus linked R&D, global and investment programs. For firms under about 10 years old. | strengths: Can be applied under the AI or the robotics field; Includes global
  acceleration and overseas-partner programs; Can be combined with TIPS R&D | weaknesses: Smaller ticket; Annual window only; Heavy reporting | best fit: Go-to-market for CEN as a sim/synthetic-data SaaS; overseas PoCs | sources: https://www.k-startup.go.kr https://www.mss.go.kr
- **NIPA AI Voucher (AI바우처) – AICHEMIST as supplier** [Demand-side voucher (revenue for supplier)] | status: Annual NIPA program run since 2020. Calls typically Jan–Mar. Whether 2026–2027 has a physical-AI or manufacturing track is unverified. | license: Government pays the AI supplier on behalf of
  SME/mid-cap demand companies, at roughly KRW 0.2–0.3B per voucher (low confidence). The demand company pays a small matching share. | strengths: Fastest subsidized revenue plus a named reference; Fits 'synthetic data + trained perception model' deliverables; Builds a pipeline of SME manufacturers
  and robot makers | weaknesses: Project-type, custom work; Demand company must apply jointly; selection can fail; Margin pressure | best fit: Factory vision QA, logistics robots and AMR perception trained on CEN synthetic data | sources: https://www.nipa.kr https://www.aivoucher.kr
- **K-DATA Data Voucher (데이터바우처) – synthetic-data supplier** [Demand-side voucher (revenue for supplier)] | status: Annual program of the Korea Data Industry Promotion Agency. Supplier registration around Dec–Jan, demand call around Jan–Feb. Synthetic data has been an accepted processing type
  (medium). | license: Per-task caps were roughly KRW 70M for AI processing, about 45M for general processing and about 18M for purchase in recent years (low confidence; 2026–2027 caps unverified). | strengths: Directly monetizes synthetic datasets; Many small, repeatable wins; Feeds the CEN
  marketplace | weaknesses: Small tickets; Price competition with labeling firms; Annual timing | best fit: Synthetic image/LiDAR datasets for SMEs and robotics startups | sources: https://kdata.or.kr/datavoucher https://www.kdata.or.kr
- **Government advanced-GPU allocation (첨단 GPU 확보·활용 / 고성능컴퓨팅 지원, MSIT–NIPA)** [In-kind compute] | status: The 2025 1st supplementary budget (about KRW 1.46T) secured about 10k+ GPUs. In Aug 2025, NHN Cloud (about 7,656 B200), Naver Cloud (about 3,056 H200) and Kakao (about 2,424 B200) were
  selected, about 13k in total. Distribution to industry, academia and research institutes started from late 2025, with priority to national projects. The 2026 government share of the NVIDIA deal (about 50k GPUs) is being phased in. Application windows and per-company caps for 2026–2027 are
  unverified. | license: Free or heavily subsidized GPU-hours via selected CSPs. Allocation is competitive. | strengths: Large in-kind value for VLA, perception and RL training; Signals alignment with sovereign AI | weaknesses: Hopper/Blackwell datacenter GPUs lack RT cores, so they cannot run
  Omniverse/Isaac RTX sensor rendering; Priority goes to foundation-model projects; Time-boxed allocations | best fit: Training robot foundation models, VLA and perception models on synthetic data; headless physics RL | sources: https://www.msit.go.kr https://www.nipa.kr
- **National AI Computing Center (국가 AI 컴퓨팅센터)** [Long-term sovereign compute] | status: A Samsung SDS-led consortium was selected in 2025, sited in Jeonnam Haenam (Solaseado), with a public-majority SPC and opening targeted around 2028 (medium/low confidence). Not usable for 2026–2027 workloads. |
  license: Expected to offer discounted access to industry and academia; pricing unknown. | strengths: Long-term cheap sovereign GPU; Likely priority access for national physical-AI projects | weaknesses: Too late for the 2026–2027 roadmap; RTX/visualization capacity unknown | best fit: 2028+ scale-
  out of training workloads | sources: https://www.msit.go.kr
- **AICA Gwangju National AI Data Center / KISTI 6th supercomputer** [Near-term public compute] | status: AICA (인공지능산업융합사업단) in Gwangju runs an AI data center with GPU support for companies. KISTI's 6th system (about 600 PF class, NVIDIA GPU-based) was planned to become operational around 2025–2026
  (medium-low). | license: Free or low-cost allocation through calls. | strengths: Additional free compute; The Gwangju route can pair with regional AI-industry grants | weaknesses: Older GPUs or limited quotas; HPC-style queueing, not interactive RTX | best fit: Batch RL and training jobs; regional

- **MSIT/IITP physical-AI, digital-twin and synthetic-data R&D** [Consortium national R&D] | status: IITP has run digital-twin and metaverse core-technology and synthetic-data programs. Physical AI became a top priority in the 2026 budget. Exact 2026–2027 new-task titles and sizes are unverified. |
  license: Typically KRW 1–5B per year per consortium over 3–5 years. Startup subcontracts or co-performer shares are typically KRW 0.2–1B per year. Matching: SME government share up to about 75%. | strengths: Funds frontier work: world models, sim-to-real, sensor physics; Partners with ETRI, KAIST

  autonomous vehicles, shipbuilding, humanoids, drones, home appliances, semiconductors and more (medium/low). Targets of hundreds of AI factories by 2030 have been reported (low). | license: Projects led by large or mid-sized manufacturers. Startup supplier share is usually KRW 0.3–3B per project
  (estimate). | strengths: Factory digital twins and synthetic vision data are core deliverables; Creates chaebol references | weaknesses: Integrators (Samsung SDS, LG CNS, SK AX, Siemens) may capture the prime role; Long procurement | best fit: Factory and warehouse digital twins plus synthetic

  references; Uses CEN's NeRF/3D asset pipeline | weaknesses: GIS/BIM-centric, low physics demand; Dominated by spatial-information firms such as E8 | best fit: Secondary; urban scenes for AV and drone sim | sources: https://www.molit.go.kr https://www.nipa.kr

  welding sim, yard logistics and AMR twins, synthetic vision for inspection | sources: https://www.motie.go.kr https://www.mof.go.kr

  (Silicon Valley and others). All long-running. | license: Small grants plus services; company co-pay of about 30% on vouchers. | strengths: Cheap market entry: legal, marketing, trade shows (GTC, Automatica, iREX Tokyo, CES); Local landing offices | weaknesses: Generic services; Small money | best
  fit: Japan and US launch in 2027–2028 | sources: https://www.exportvoucher.com https://www.kotra.or.kr https://www.k-startup.go.kr https://www.nipa.kr

  startup support in Korea as part of the Oct 2025 deal (low on specifics). | license: Free (Inception). NPN service-delivery or ISV partner tiers have requirements. | strengths: Co-marketing at GTC and AI Day Seoul; Introductions to Hyundai, Samsung and SK physical-AI teams and to Japanese OEMs;

  (medium). | license: Free credits, time-limited. | strengths: RTX-class instances (L4/L40S) for rendering; Marketplace listing for global sales | weaknesses: Credits run out fast at physics-and-render scale; Availability of RTX instances in Korean regions varies | best fit: Bridge RTX rendering

  industries was launched around Dec 2025 (medium/low). | license: Equity. | strengths: Large follow-on rounds for capital-intensive GPU platforms | weaknesses: Mostly flows to later-stage or large firms; Dilution | best fit: Series A/B to fund the GPU fleet and overseas expansion | sources:

## Key facts
- [high] No live verification was possible in this run. WebSearch returned 'web search budget is used up (limit 200 calls per turn, shared by every agent)'. WebFetch to nvidianews.nvidia.com, www.korea.kr and www.msit.go.kr returned EGRESS_BLOCKED. All other facts come from model knowledge and must
  be re-verified. (Tool errors in this run)
- [medium] NVIDIA and Korea announced (31 Oct 2025, APEC Gyeongju) supply of 260,000+ Blackwell GPUs: government about 50k (sovereign AI / national compute), Samsung about 50k, SK Group about 50k, Hyundai Motor Group about 50k, Naver Cloud about 60k. (https://nvidianews.nvidia.com/news/south-korea-
  ai-infrastructure (URL from memory; fetch blocked))
- [medium] Commercial uses in that deal center on physical AI and digital twins: the Samsung 'AI Megafactory' with Omniverse fab twins, Hyundai's physical-AI cluster (about USD 3B, including an NVIDIA AI Technology Center and a Physical AI Application Center), and SK's manufacturing AI cloud with
  Omniverse for Korean manufacturers. (Same NVIDIA newsroom release; Korean press Oct–Nov 2025)
- [medium] 2026 national budget: government proposal about KRW 728.0T (29 Aug 2025), passed by the National Assembly at about KRW 727.9T on 2 Dec 2025. AI budget about KRW 10.1T, roughly 3x 2025's about 3.3T. R&D about KRW 35.3T (+19.3%). (https://www.moef.go.kr (2026 budget press materials; not
  fetched))
- [medium] R&D budget history: about KRW 31.1T (2023) → cut to about 26.5T (2024) → about 29.6T (2025) → about 35.3T (2026 proposal). This volatility is a policy-cycle risk. (https://www.msit.go.kr / https://www.iris.go.kr)
- [high] The Framework Act on AI Development and Trust (AI Basic Act) was passed 26 Dec 2024, promulgated 21 Jan 2025 and effective 22 Jan 2026, with high-impact AI and generative-AI labeling obligations and a grace period for fines. (https://www.law.go.kr)
- [medium] 2025 government restructuring: the MSIT minister was elevated to a Deputy Prime Minister for science/AI, and MOEF was split into a finance/economy ministry and a separate budget office (기획예산처) from 2026, so the 2027 budget is compiled by the new budget office. (https://www.mois.go.kr
  (Government Organization Act amendment, Sept–Oct 2025))
- [low] The 2025 1st supplementary budget allotted about KRW 1.46T to secure about 10k advanced GPUs. In Aug 2025 MSIT selected NHN Cloud (about 7,656 B200), Naver Cloud (about 3,056 H200) and Kakao (about 2,424 B200), about 13.1k in total, for distribution to industry, academia and research from
  late 2025, with priority to national projects such as the sovereign foundation-model program. (https://www.msit.go.kr press releases (Aug 2025))
- [medium] Sovereign AI foundation-model project (독자 AI 파운데이션 모델): 5 teams selected Aug 2025 (Naver Cloud, SK Telecom, LG AI Research, NC AI, Upstage), with staged eliminations and government GPU, data and talent support. This is the main competing claimant on government GPUs.
  (https://www.msit.go.kr)
- [low] National AI Computing Center: a Samsung SDS-led consortium was selected in 2025 for a site in Jeonnam (Haenam/Solaseado), as a public–private SPC, with service opening targeted around 2028. (https://www.msit.go.kr)
- [medium] MOTIE launched the K-Humanoid Alliance on 10 Apr 2025 with robot OEMs, AI software firms, component makers, universities and institutes, targeting a robot AI foundation model and competitive humanoid hardware or components by 2030. (https://www.motie.go.kr (Apr 2025 press release))
- [low] MOTIE launched an M.AX (Manufacturing AX) Alliance around Sept 2025 grouping large firms and SMEs by domain (AI factory, autonomous vehicles, shipbuilding, humanoids, drones, appliances, semiconductors), building on the 2024 AI Autonomous Manufacturing strategy. (https://www.motie.go.kr)
- [low] I could not confirm that a 'Physical AI Global Alliance' (피지컬AI 글로벌 얼라이언스) exists under that name. Verify before citing it in proposals. (Not found in model knowledge)
- [medium] The Autonomous Driving Technology Innovation Program runs 2021–2027 with about KRW 1.1T (about 1.0974T) across MOTIE, MSIT, MOLIT and police. National target: Level-4 commercialization by 2027. K-City (Hwaseong) is the national AV testbed. (https://www.molit.go.kr ;
  https://www.kotsa.or.kr)
- [medium] Deep-tech TIPS gives up to about KRW 1.5B R&D over 3 years and requires operator investment of at least about KRW 0.3B. General TIPS gives up to about KRW 0.5B R&D over 2 years. (https://www.jointips.or.kr)
- [medium] Super-Gap Startup 1000+ (2023–2027) selects about 200 deep-tech startups per year across 10 fields including big data/AI, robotics and future mobility, with up to about KRW 0.6B commercialization funding over up to 3 years. (https://www.k-startup.go.kr)
- [medium] National R&D matching rules: government share is generally at most 75% of project cost for SMEs, at most about 70% for mid-caps and at most 50% for large firms. Most MSS/MOTIE R&D requires a KOITA-recognized corporate R&D lab or dedicated R&D department. Calls for most ministries are
  posted on IRIS; MSS R&D is on SMTECH. (https://www.iris.go.kr ; https://www.smtech.go.kr ; https://www.rnd.or.kr)
- [high] Omniverse/Isaac Sim RTX rendering (camera, RTX LiDAR and radar sensors) requires RT-core GPUs (RTX/RTX PRO/L40S class). A100/H100/H200/B200 datacenter GPUs lack RT cores and are not supported for RTX sensor rendering, so government B200/H200 allocations suit training, not sensor-realistic
  simulation. (https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/latest/installation/requirements.html)
- [medium] Digital-twin international standards: ISO/IEC 30173:2023 (concepts and terminology), ISO/IEC 30172:2023 (use cases), ISO 23247-1..4:2021 (digital twin framework for manufacturing), with further SC 41 work items (reference architecture) in progress. Korea participates actively via TTA.
  (https://www.iso.org/committee/6483279.html ; https://www.tta.or.kr)
- [medium] PIPC published a synthetic-data generation reference model (합성데이터 생성 참조모델) in 2024. It is useful for the 'privacy-safe' claim in proposals. (https://www.pipc.go.kr)
- [medium] Korea–US trade deal (30 Jul 2025): Korea committed about USD 350B of US investment, including about USD 150B for shipbuilding cooperation (MASGA). This creates US yard-modernization and digital-twin demand linked to Korean yards (Hanwha Philly Shipyard, HD Hyundai partnerships).
  (https://www.motie.go.kr ; https://www.whitehouse.gov)
- [medium] Precedent for Korean digital-twin export: Naver won a Saudi MOMRAH 5-city digital-twin platform project in 2024 (reported at about KRW 100B / USD ~100M scale). (https://www.navercorp.com (press 2024))
- [medium] Korea became an associated country to Horizon Europe (Pillar II) from 2025, allowing Korean entities to receive EU funding in collaborative projects. (https://research-and-innovation.ec.europa.eu)
- [medium] Domestic competitors and partners: MORAI (AV simulator, K-City twin, overseas sales), CyLab/씨이랩 (synthetic data, XAIVA, NVIDIA partner), E8/이에이트 (NDX PRO digital-twin platform), VIRNECT (XR twin). Samsung became Rainbow Robotics' largest shareholder (about 35%) at end-2024. (Company
  websites / DART filings (https://dart.fss.or.kr))
- [high] Annual timing pattern: ministries publish unified annual R&D and startup-support announcements around Dec–Jan. Most new-task calls run Jan–Apr, with second rounds Jun–Aug. The government budget proposal is published late Aug and the National Assembly passes it by 2 Dec. So the 2027 proposal
  is already public but unverified here. (https://www.iris.go.kr ; https://www.k-startup.go.kr)
## Quantitative data
- NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs committed to Korea (Oct 2025): 260,000+ (Gov ~50k, Samsung ~50k, SK ~50k, Hyundai MG ~50k, Naver Cloud ~60k) (https://nvidianews.nvidia.com/news/south-korea-ai-infrastructure (unverified this run))
- Hyundai Motor Group physical-AI investment with NVIDIA: ~USD 3B (physical AI cluster, AI Technology Center, Physical AI Application Center) (NVIDIA/Hyundai Oct 2025 announcements (unverified; medium-low))
- 2026 Korean national budget (final): ~KRW 727.9T (passed 2 Dec 2025; proposal 728.0T) (https://www.moef.go.kr (medium))
- 2026 AI budget (proposal): ~KRW 10.1T vs ~3.3T in 2025 (~3x) (2026 budget proposal, Aug 2025 (medium))
- 2026 national R&D budget (proposal): ~KRW 35.3T (+19.3% YoY); 2025 ~29.6T; 2024 ~26.5T (MSIT/MOEF (medium))
- 2025 supplementary budget for advanced GPUs: ~KRW 1.46T for ~10k+ GPUs; ~13.1k selected (NHN ~7,656 B200; Naver ~3,056 H200; Kakao ~2,424 B200) (MSIT Aug 2025 (low))
- Autonomous Driving Tech Innovation Program: ~KRW 1.1T, 2021–2027; L4 commercialization target 2027 (MOLIT/MOTIE (medium))
- Autonomous ship R&D program: ~KRW 160B, 2020–2025 (MOTIE + MOF) (MOTIE/MOF (medium-low))
- Deep-tech TIPS cap: ≤ ~KRW 1.5B R&D / 3 yrs; operator investment ≥ ~KRW 0.3B (https://www.jointips.or.kr (medium))
- General TIPS cap: ≤ ~KRW 0.5B R&D / 2 yrs (+ follow-on commercialization/overseas marketing add-ons) (https://www.jointips.or.kr (medium))
- Super-Gap Startup 1000+: ~200 firms/yr, 1,000 over 2023–2027; ≤ ~KRW 0.6B over ≤3 yrs (https://www.k-startup.go.kr (medium))
- Data Voucher per-task caps (recent years): AI processing ~KRW 70M; general processing ~45M; purchase ~18M (https://kdata.or.kr/datavoucher (low))
- AI Voucher per-demand-company cap: ~KRW 0.2–0.3B (varies by track/firm size) (https://www.nipa.kr (low))
- Export Voucher cap: ≤ ~KRW 100M per firm per year (company co-pay ~30%) (https://www.exportvoucher.com (medium))
- Government share limit in national R&D: SME ≤75%, mid-cap ≤~70%, large firm ≤50% of total project cost (National R&D Innovation Act rules via https://www.iris.go.kr (medium))
- Korea–US investment package / shipbuilding (MASGA): ~USD 350B total; ~USD 150B shipbuilding (Jul 2025 trade deal (medium))
- Naver Saudi digital-twin contract: 5 cities, ~KRW 100B scale (2024) (Naver press (medium))
- National AI Computing Center opening target: ~2028 (Jeonnam, Samsung SDS consortium) (MSIT 2025 (low))
- Realistic non-dilutive intake for AICHEMIST Q4 2026–2027 (estimate): ~KRW 3–6B cash + in-kind GPUs if 4–6 applications succeed (TIPS-class 1.5B, Super-Gap 0.6B, 2–3 consortium work packages 0.3–1B/yr each, voucher revenue 0.5–1.5B) (Analyst estimate (not a sourced figure))


# TOPIC: build_vs_buy
## Options
- **Strategy A - Build whole engine in-house (physics + renderer)** [Build strategy] | status: Not started. Benchmarks for effort: MuJoCo (Roboti LLC, about 2010-2021, then DeepMind acquisition Oct 2021 and open-sourcing May 2022). Newton (announced Mar 2025 by NVIDIA, Google DeepMind and Disney
  Research; 1.0.0 on PyPI 2026-03-10; v1.6.1 on 2026-10-05; built on Warp and MuJoCo-Warp). Genesis (academic project, then Genesis AI-backed; 1.0 in May 2026; 1.4.3 on 2026-09-30). Chrono (about 20k commits, v10.0.0). Drake (35,216 commits, copyright 2012-2025). Gazebo (16+ years). | license: Owned
  IP and no license fees. Estimated 150-300 engineer-years to reach rough parity on rigid and deformable contact physics, GPU batching, RTX-class path tracing and sensor models: about ₩400-600억 ($30-43M) over 3-5 years with 40-80 specialists. | strengths: Full IP ownership and no vendor lock-in or
  EULA risk; Could target non-NVIDIA hardware; Potential deep differentiation if it succeeds | weaknesses: Time-to-MVP 30-48+ months; competes against free engines backed by NVIDIA, Google DeepMind, Disney and Genesis AI; Korea has very few contact-dynamics, GPU-solver, path-tracing and sensor-
  physics specialists; Moving target: Newton shipped 1.0 through 1.6.1 in 7 months and MuJoCo ships about every 2-3 weeks; Burns most of a startup's runway on infrastructure rather than product | best fit: Not recommended. Only targeted in-house modules make sense: sensor noise and ISP models,
  domain-randomization engine, asset-physics property estimation, LLM scene compiler. | sources: https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/blob/main/doc/overview.rst https://pypi.org/project/newton/#history https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/Genesis https://github.com/projectchrono/chrono
- **Strategy B - Adopt NVIDIA Isaac Sim / Omniverse as the base** [Adopt strategy] | status: Isaac Sim 6.1.0 (PyPI 2026-09-09; GitHub GA 2026-09-10); 7.0.0a1 on GitHub 2026-09-18. Isaac Lab 3.0.0-EA (2026-09-16) requires Isaac Sim 6.1 for its PhysX/RTX/ROS backends. | license: Repo source is
  Apache-2.0. Kit SDK, RTX renderer, models and textures fall under the 'Isaac Sim Additional Software and Materials License'; the pip package is classified 'Other/Proprietary'. Isaac Sim itself costs nothing to download. Omniverse Enterprise (support/enterprise features) is about $4,500/GPU/year
  (LOW confidence, could not verify). Rendering needs RT-core GPUs at $1.09-4.50/GPU-h. | strengths: Fastest path to photoreal RTX camera/lidar/radar plus PhysX and Replicator SDG (MVP in 3-5 months); Largest robotics simulation ecosystem (Isaac Lab, GR00T, Cosmos, OpenUSD/SimReady); Korean
  industrial customers often already standardize on OpenUSD and Omniverse | weaknesses: Proprietary Kit/RTX terms: hosted multi-tenant SaaS, on-prem redistribution and asset resale need NVIDIA's written confirmation; Hard NVIDIA lock-in on both GPUs and software, plus Kit telemetry; Needs RT-core
  GPUs (L40S/RTX PRO 6000) for every rendering session; heavy containers; streaming has no authentication or encryption and needs --network=host; Hard to differentiate: NVIDIA's own blueprints compete with a thin wrapper | best fit: Phase-0 vehicle for the first paid pilots and photoreal sensor SDG.
  Not the long-term core. | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/blob/develop/LICENSE https://pypi.org/project/isaacsim/ https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/blob/develop/tools/docker/README.md
- **Strategy C - Open-source multi-engine + own orchestration + UE5 or web/3DGS rendering** [Open-source assembly strategy] | status: Components are current: MuJoCo 3.15.0 (2026-10-05), Newton 1.6.1 (2026-10-05), Genesis 1.4.3 (2026-09-30), Chrono 10.0.0, CARLA 0.10.0 (UE 5.5, Dec 2024) and 0.9.16,
  UE 5.8 is the current Pixel Streaming branch. | license: Physics is permissive: MuJoCo, Newton, MJWarp and Gazebo are Apache-2.0; Chrono and Drake are BSD-3; Genesis code is Apache-2.0. Unreal Engine charges about $1,850/seat/yr for non-game companies over $1M revenue (MEDIUM-LOW confidence, 2024
  terms not re-verified); Pixel Streaming infrastructure is MIT. CARLA code is MIT and its assets are CC-BY. | strengths: Minimal vendor lock-in at the software level (UE/web renderers run on any GPU); Low license fees; full control of the UX and web delivery (WebGPU/3DGS viewers); UE5 Lumen/Nanite
  visuals are strong for AV and city scenes | weaknesses: Two-engine coupling (MuJoCo/Newton physics plus UE Chaos-scene rendering) adds synchronization and maintenance cost; Physically based sensor models (lidar/radar, rolling shutter, ISP) must be built in-house; Time-to-MVP 9-15 months;
  integration-heavy team needed; UE EULA and Fab/Megascans asset terms restrict reuse outside UE and possibly AI-training use (unverified) | best fit: AV/city-scale visualization and web-delivered twins where photoreal path tracing is not required. | sources: https://github.com/google-
  deepmind/mujoco/releases https://pypi.org/project/newton/ https://pypi.org/project/genesis-world/ https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla
- **Strategy D - Hybrid: open physics/training core + selective NVIDIA RTX/UE rendering + own platform layer (RECOMMENDED)** [Hybrid strategy] | status: Feasible now because Isaac Lab 3.0-EA (2026-09-16) runs kit-less on Newton/MuJoCo-Warp and supports pluggable visualizers (Newton GL, Newton RTX,
  Kit, Viser, Rerun). Isaac Sim RTX, ovrtx and ovphysx stay optional extras. | license: Core stack is permissive: Newton, MJWarp, MuJoCo and Warp 1.18 are Apache-2.0; Isaac Lab is BSD-3; PhysX SDK is BSD-3; Cosmos 3 is OpenMDW-1.1. Proprietary pieces (Kit/RTX/ovrtx) are isolated behind a renderer
  interface and used only in a licensed tier. Cost: about ₩80억 over 24 months with ramped hiring (12→25 people) including compute. | strengths: Training path (RL/IL/VLA) has no proprietary dependency and runs on H100/B200/RTX 4090/5090 without RT cores; Best fidelity is available where needed (RTX
  sensors) and can be swapped (UE5, Genesis Nyx, 3DGS); Differentiation sits in AICHEMIST-owned layers: CEN data engine, LLM scene control, asset physics, marketplace; Phase 0 can reuse Isaac Sim directly, so MVP takes 4-6 months | weaknesses: Still NVIDIA/CUDA-locked for GPU physics (Newton, MJWarp
  and Warp need NVIDIA GPUs); Multiple backends raise QA and benchmarking costs (sim-to-sim consistency); Several key pieces are alpha (MJWarp alpha on PyPI, ovrtx 0.5 alpha, ovphysx 0.6 alpha) | best fit: AICHEMIST/CEN: a general-purpose physical-AI digital twin plus synthetic-data SaaS with on-
  prem option for Korean enterprises. | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-EA https://github.com/newton-physics/newton https://pypi.org/project/mujoco-warp/ https://pypi.org/project/warp-lang/
- **NVIDIA Isaac Sim** [Robotics simulator (PhysX + RTX, OpenUSD)] | status: 6.1.0 (PyPI 2026-09-09); 6.0.0 (PyPI 2026-03-16, GitHub GA 2026-06-04); 5.0.0 (2025-08-07, first open-source release); 7.0.0a1 (2026-09-18); develop VERSION = 6.1.0-rc.26. Owner: NVIDIA. | license: Apache-2.0 for the repo,
  plus a proprietary NVIDIA additional license for the Kit SDK, models and textures (URL in the LICENSE preamble); the pip metapackage is classified 'NVIDIA Proprietary Software'. Free to download; ACCEPT_EULA is required to run the container. | strengths: Photoreal RTX sensors, Replicator SDG, ROS
  2 bridge; Datacenter GPUs: A40 minimum, L40S/L20 recommended, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server best; supports Linux aarch64 | weaknesses: Proprietary runtime; unauthenticated WebRTC streaming that needs --network=host; Not usable on H100/H200/B200 for rendering because they lack RT cores | best fit:
  High-fidelity sensor SDG and validation tier. | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim https://raw.githubusercontent.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/develop/VERSION https://pypi.org/project/isaacsim/#history https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/blob/develop/tools/docker/README.md

  (isaaclab_mimic). cuRobo is now Apache-2.0. Proprietary only when the Isaac Sim, ovrtx or ovphysx extras are installed. | strengths: One task API across physics and rendering backends; Published throughput benchmarks (for example, G1 rough-terrain locomotion at 82k FPS training on an RTX 4090);
  Large community | weaknesses: 3.0 is still Early Access; API churn; Mostly NVIDIA-only GPUs | best fit: Default training layer of the platform (RL, imitation, mimic data generation). | sources: https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab
  https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/blob/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst https://github.com/NVlabs/curobo/blob/main/LICENSE

  Committee. PyPI status 'Production/Stable'. | license: Apache-2.0 (code), CC-BY-4.0 (docs). Free. | strengths: Vendor-neutral governance; MuJoCo-Warp primary backend plus VBD/XPBD cloth, MPM, cables, differentiable simulation; OpenUSD support; fast many-world setup | weaknesses: NVIDIA GPU only
  (Maxwell or newer, driver 545+); macOS is CPU-only; Young (1.0 seven months ago) | best fit: Primary physics core for robots, deformables and manipulation. | sources: https://github.com/newton-physics/newton https://pypi.org/project/newton/#history https://github.com/newton-physics/newton/releases

  MJX and MJWarp. | license: Apache-2.0; documentation CC-BY-4.0. Free. | strengths: Gold-standard contact model for robot learning; CPU version is vendor-neutral; MJWarp benchmark: humanoid with 8,192 worlds at about 2.73M steps/s (GPU not stated) | weaknesses: MJWarp alpha: some features
  unsupported (IMPLICITFAST, PGS/noslip, plugin actuators); NVIDIA GPU needed for speed | best fit: Reference physics, sim-to-sim validation and the JAX/TPU path. | sources: https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/releases https://pypi.org/project/mujoco-

  2026-09-25, alpha) declares NO license on PyPI, so treat it as not open until clarified. | strengths: Cross-vendor: CUDA, AMD ROCm, Apple Metal, Vulkan, x86, ARM64; Unified rigid/FEM/MPM/particle physics; good laptop-to-datacenter developer experience | weaknesses: Early marketing speed claims

  rendering GPUs × 2 years ≈ $144k (₩2.0억). | strengths: Enterprise support, SLAs, possibly clearer production-use rights | weaknesses: Recurring per-GPU cost on top of cloud GPU cost; deepens lock-in | best fit: Buy only if NVIDIA requires it for hosted commercial use or if an enterprise customer

  license: Cosmos 3 code and weights: OpenMDW-1.1. Cosmos 2.5 weights: NVIDIA Open Model License. Self-hosted GPU cost only. | strengths: Sim-to-real appearance transfer and scenario augmentation for CEN synthetic data; permissive license in v3 | weaknesses: Heavy GPUs; generated artifacts need

Owner: Epic Games. | license: Pixel Streaming infrastructure is MIT. Engine: free under $1M annual revenue; non-game companies over
  $1M pay about $1,850/seat/yr (2024 terms; 2026 price not verified, MEDIUM-LOW). Games pay a 5% royalty over $1M. | strengths: Best-in-class real-time visuals (Lumen/Nanite), large asset ecosystem, runs on any GPU vendor; Proven for AV simulation (CARLA 0.10 on UE 5.5) | weaknesses: Chaos physics

  strengths: Easy authoring, mobile/XR targets; may match CEN's existing tooling if CEN is Unity-based | weaknesses: Weaker robotics physics; higher seat cost; Unity Perception package history of deprioritization | best fit: Only if CEN already runs on Unity. | sources:

  builds. | strengths: Free AV scenario stack with traffic, maps and sensors; NVIDIA NuRec/Cosmos bridges | weaknesses: Single-node real-time bias; vehicle dynamics are simplified versus Chrono/PhysX vehicles | best fit: AV vertical starter content and benchmarks. | sources:

  granular media and FSI that no other engine here covers | weaknesses: CPU-heavy; not built for RL-scale batching | best fit: Off-road, construction-equipment and ship/FSI twins. | sources: https://github.com/projectchrono/chrono https://raw.githubusercontent.com/projectchrono/chrono/main/LICENSE

  best fit: Ground-truth and validation reference, planning/control customers. | sources: https://github.com/RobotLocomotion/drake https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RobotLocomotion/drake/master/LICENSE.TXT

  compatibility layer. | sources: https://github.com/gazebosim/gz-sim

  cheaper than hyperscalers; spot gives 50-65% further discounts | weaknesses: Seoul regions cost 23-38% more; RT-core GPU supply is thinner than H100 supply | best fit: Split pools: RT-core GPUs (L40S/RTX PRO 6000) for rendering, H100/B200 for training, plus a Korean sovereign/CSAP cloud for
