## Key facts
- [high] Isaac Sim's LICENSE file says the repo software is Apache-2.0, but 'Building or using the software requires additional components licenced under other terms', including the Omniverse Kit SDK and 3D models/textures, under the 'Isaac Sim Additional Software and Materials License' (nvidia.com
  agreements page). (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/blob/develop/LICENSE)
- [high] The isaacsim pip metapackage is classified 'Other/Proprietary License (NVIDIA Proprietary Software)'. Latest 6.1.0.0 released 2026-09-09; 6.0.0.0 on 2026-03-16; 5.0.0.0 on 2025-08-07. (https://pypi.org/project/isaacsim/#history)
- [high] Isaac Sim datacenter GPU guidance: minimum A40, recommended L40S or L20, best RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server. Workstation minimum is RTX 4080. Supports Linux aarch64. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim)
- [medium] H100, H200, B200 and B300 datacenter GPUs have no RT cores, so they cannot be used for Isaac Sim/Omniverse RTX rendering. They remain fine for kit-less physics RL and model training. (NVIDIA architecture specifications (not re-fetched; nvidia.com blocked); consistent with the Isaac Sim
  README GPU list, which names only RT-core GPUs)
- [high] Isaac Sim's container streaming has no authentication or encryption ('designed for use on private/trusted networks'), requires --network=host for WebRTC, and requires ACCEPT_EULA to run. (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacSim/blob/develop/tools/docker/README.md)
- [high] Kit apps (kit-app-template) are governed by the 'NVIDIA Software License Agreement and the Product-Specific Terms for NVIDIA Omniverse'. The Kit SDK collects anonymous usage data. Documented deployment paths are Kit App Streaming on Kubernetes and NVCF / DGX Cloud.
  (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/kit-app-template)
- [high] ovrtx (kit-less RTX camera/lidar/radar sensor simulation library) 0.5.1 was released 2026-10-06 as alpha under LicenseRef-NvidiaProprietary (NVIDIA SLA plus Product Specific Terms for NVIDIA AI Products). ovphysx 0.6.3 (2026-09-16, alpha) is LicenseRef-NVIDIA-Omniverse.
  (https://pypi.org/project/ovrtx/ ; https://pypi.org/project/ovphysx/)
- [high] Isaac Lab 3.0.0-EA (2026-09-16) introduces a multi-backend architecture. Newton (MuJoCo-Warp, VBD, MPM), OVPhysX and OVRTX run kit-less. The Isaac Sim PhysX backend, Isaac Sim RTX rendering and the ROS bridge require Isaac Sim 6.1. Visualizers: Newton GL, Newton RTX, Kit, Viser, Rerun.
  (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/releases/tag/v3.0.0-EA)
- [high] Isaac Lab main code is BSD-3 and isaaclab_mimic is Apache-2.0. cuRobo's LICENSE is now Apache-2.0 (NVIDIA, 2023-2026). (https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab ; https://github.com/NVlabs/curobo/blob/main/LICENSE)
- [high] Newton is Apache-2.0 (code) and CC-BY-4.0 (docs), a Linux Foundation project started by Disney Research, Google DeepMind and NVIDIA, with MuJoCo-Warp as its primary backend. It needs an NVIDIA GPU (Maxwell or newer, driver 545+). v1.0.0 on PyPI 2026-03-10; v1.6.1 on 2026-10-05; PyPI status
  Production/Stable. (https://github.com/newton-physics/newton ; https://pypi.org/project/newton/#history)
- [high] MuJoCo 'Initially developed by Roboti LLC, it was acquired and made freely available by Google DeepMind in October 2021, and open sourced in May 2022.' Apache-2.0. Latest 3.15.0 on 2026-10-05, with releases every 2-3 weeks (3.11 Jul 27, 3.12 Aug 20, 3.13 Sep 8, 3.14 Sep 22).
  (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/blob/main/doc/overview.rst ; https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/releases)
- [high] MuJoCo-Warp (MJWarp) 3.15.0 is Apache-2.0, maintained by Google DeepMind and NVIDIA, classified Alpha on PyPI, and requires an NVIDIA GPU for fast simulation. Its benchmark shows a humanoid with 8,192 worlds at 2,729,192 steps/s (GPU not stated). (https://pypi.org/project/mujoco-warp/ ;
  https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/blob/main/benchmarks/README.md)
- [high] Genesis World code is Apache-2.0 and now officially supported by Genesis AI; latest 1.4.3 on 2026-09-30; 1.0 announced May 2026. Backends: CUDA, AMD ROCm, Apple Metal, Vulkan, x86, ARM64 via the Quadrants compiler. The Nyx renderer (gs-nyx 0.1.7) declares no license on PyPI.
  (https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/Genesis ; https://pypi.org/project/genesis-world/ ; https://pypi.org/project/gs-nyx/)
- [high] Genesis's launch README claimed 'Over 43 million FPS when simulating a Franka robotic arm with a single RTX 4090'. This is a contact-light best case, not representative of manipulation workloads. (https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/Genesis/blob/v0.2.1/README.md)
- [low] Genesis was reported as a roughly 24-month collaboration of 20+ research labs before its Dec 2024 launch, and Genesis AI reportedly raised about $105M seed in 2025. Both claims come from memory and could not be re-verified. (Not verified in this session (web search unavailable))
- [medium] NVIDIA PhysX repository (PhysX SDK, Blast, Flow) is BSD-3 with GPU source included; compatible with PhysX SDK 5.11 / ovphysx 0.6 / Isaac Sim 6.0. (https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/PhysX)
- [high] Warp (warp-lang) 1.18.0, released 2026-10-05, is Apache-2.0 and requires an NVIDIA Turing (sm_75)+ GPU and an R580+ driver for CUDA; macOS is CPU-only. (https://pypi.org/project/warp-lang/)
- [high] Cosmos 3 (May 2026; Edge variant July 2026) is released under OpenMDW-1.1 for code and weights: Super 64B, Nano 16B, Edge 4B. Cosmos-Predict2.5 weights are under the NVIDIA Open Model License and that repo is no longer actively developed. (https://github.com/NVIDIA/Cosmos ;
  https://github.com/nvidia-cosmos/cosmos-predict2.5)
- [high] CARLA code is MIT and its assets are CC-BY. Unreal Engine follows its own license. 0.10.0 (Dec 19 2024) moved to UE 5.5 with Chaos physics; 0.9.16 added Cosmos Transfer1 and NuRec integration. (https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla ; https://github.com/carla-simulator/carla/releases)
- [high] Project Chrono is BSD-3 (Copyright 2016 Project Chrono Development Team), version 10.0.0, about 20,031 commits, with an AMD ROCm GPU path. Drake is BSD-3 (MIT CSAIL, 2012-2025) with 35,216 commits. Gazebo gz-sim is Apache-2.0, maintained by Intrinsic, with 16+ years of history.
  (https://github.com/projectchrono/chrono ; https://github.com/RobotLocomotion/drake ; https://github.com/gazebosim/gz-sim)
- [high] Epic's Pixel Streaming infrastructure (signalling server, SFU, frontend, Matchmaker) is MIT-licensed. UE5.8 is the current branch, UE5.7 is supported and UE5.6 is end-of-life. (https://github.com/EpicGames/PixelStreamingInfrastructure)
- [medium] Unreal Engine: free under $1M annual gross revenue; non-game companies above that pay a seat subscription of about $1,850/seat/year (introduced 2024); games pay 5% royalty over $1M per product. 2026 price not re-verified. (Epic pricing (unrealengine.com blocked by egress proxy; from 2024
  terms))
- [low] Omniverse Enterprise list price is about $4,500 per GPU per year. Unity Industry is about $4,950/seat/year and Unity Pro about $2,200/seat/year. None of these could be re-verified for 2026. (Vendor pricing pages blocked (nvidia.com, unity.com))
- [high] The SkyPilot catalog GPU prices used here were refreshed by bot on 2026-10-05/06 (AWS, GCP, RunPod, Shadeform). The Lambda and Azure files may be older. (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog/commits/master)
- [high] AWS and GCP Seoul regions are 23-38% more expensive than US regions for the same GPU instances (AWS g6e/g7e/g6 +23%; AWS p5/p5en +38%; GCP A3-high and L4 +28%). (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog (catalogs/v8/aws/vms.csv, catalogs/v8/gcp/vms.csv))
- [low] Korea secured large NVIDIA GPU allocations in 2025: about 260k GPUs announced at APEC on 2025-10-31 (government about 50k; Samsung, SK and Hyundai about 50k each; Naver about 60k). A government GPU-leasing program runs via NHN Cloud, Naver Cloud and Kakao. This could provide subsidized
  H100/H200/B200 access for startups. (Not verified in this session (news sites blocked))
## Quantitative data
- AWS H100 on-demand (p5.48xlarge, us-east-1): $55.04/h per 8 GPUs = $6.88/GPU-h; Seoul ap-northeast-2 $75.96 = $9.49/GPU-h; spot min about $26.03/8 = $3.25/GPU-h (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog (v8/aws/vms.csv, 2026-10-06))
- AWS H200 on-demand (p5en.48xlarge): $63.30/h = $7.91/GPU-h (us-east-1); $87.35 = $10.92/GPU-h (Seoul) (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- AWS B200 / B300 on-demand: p6-b200.48xlarge $113.93/h = $14.24/GPU-h (spot about $6.70); p6-b300.48xlarge $142.42/h = $17.80/GPU-h (Seoul $26.15) (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- AWS L40S on-demand (g6e): g6e.xlarge $1.861/h (us-east-1), $2.288 (Seoul); g6e.48xlarge 8×L40S $30.13/h = $3.77/GPU-h (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- AWS RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (g7e): g7e.2xlarge $3.363/h (us-east-1), $4.135 (Seoul), spot about $1.91; g7e.48xlarge 8 GPUs $33.14/h = $4.14/GPU-h (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- AWS L4 / A10G on-demand: g6.xlarge (L4) $0.805/h US, $0.990 Seoul; g5.xlarge (A10G) $1.006/h US, $1.237 Seoul, spot about $0.41 (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- GCP H100 (a3-highgpu-8g, us-central1) VM + GPU: $9.46 + $78.37 = $87.83/h = $10.98/GPU-h; Seoul asia-northeast3 $112.79 = $14.10/GPU-h; spot $49.94 = $6.24/GPU-h (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog (v8/gcp/vms.csv, 2026-10-05))
- GCP H200 (a3-ultragpu-8g) / B200 (a4-highgpu-8g): H200 $86.76/h = $10.85/GPU-h (spot $6.46); B200 $90.22/h = $11.28/GPU-h (spot $4.95) (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- GCP RTX PRO 6000 (G4) and L4 (G2): g4-standard-48 (1 GPU) $3.40 VM + $1.10 GPU = $4.50/h, spot $1.71/h; 8-GPU g4-standard-384 $36.0/h = $4.50/GPU-h. g2-standard-4 (1×L4) $0.71/h US; L4 GPU-only $0.72 in Seoul (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- Azure on-demand (eastus): NC40ads H100 v5 $6.98/GPU-h; ND96isr H100 v5 $98.32/h; NV36ads A10 v5 $3.20/h (spot $0.80); NC24ads A100 v4 $3.67/h (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog (v8/azure, freshness uncertain))
- Lambda on-demand: 8×H100 SXM $31.92/h = $3.99/GPU-h; 1×H100 PCIe $3.29; 8×B200 $53.52 = $6.69/GPU-h; GH200 $2.29; A10 $1.29 (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog (v8/lambda, freshness uncertain))
- RunPod Secure Cloud on-demand: H100 PCIe $2.89, H100 SXM $3.49, H200 $4.59, B200 $6.79, B300 $7.89, L40S $1.09, RTX PRO 6000 $2.09, L4 $0.49, RTX 4090 $0.74, RTX 5090 $0.99 per GPU-h (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog (v8/runpod, 2026-10-06))
- Other neoclouds / marketplaces: Nebius H100 $4.50 (spot $2.15), H200 $5.40, B200 $8.50, L40S $1.55. Vast.ai lows: H100 $1.80, H200 $2.35, L40S $0.60, RTX 4090 $0.35, RTX PRO 6000 WS $0.93. OCI H100/H200 $10.00, L40S $3.50, including Seoul (https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- Seoul region premium over US: AWS g6/g6e/g7e +23%; AWS p5/p5en +38%; GCP A3/L4 +28% (Computed from https://github.com/skypilot-org/skypilot-catalog)
- Isaac Lab training throughput (step + inference + train, env-steps/s): RTX 4090: G1 rough locomotion 82k, Shadow-hand repose 170k, Cartpole 510k, Cartpole-RGB-camera 32k. L40: 62k / 120k / 260k / 21k. 4 nodes × 4 L40: G1 960k, Shadow 1.8M (https://github.com/isaac-
  sim/IsaacLab/blob/main/docs/source/overview/reinforcement-learning/performance_benchmarks.rst)
- Cost per 1B RL env-steps: humanoid G1 rough-terrain locomotion: RTX 4090 3.39 GPU-h = $1.17-2.51. L40S-class 4.48 GPU-h = $2.69 (Vast), $4.88 (RunPod), $8.34 (AWS US), $10.25 (AWS Seoul) (Derived from Isaac Lab benchmarks + SkyPilot prices)
- Cost per 1B RL env-steps: dexterous Shadow-hand manipulation: RTX 4090 1.63 GPU-h = $0.57-1.21; L40S-class 2.31 GPU-h = $1.39-5.30 (Derived from Isaac Lab benchmarks + SkyPilot prices)
- Cost per 1B RL env-steps with one camera in the loop (Cartpole-RGB): RTX 4090 8.68 GPU-h = $3.00-6.42; L40S-class 13.2 GPU-h = $7.94-30.26. Realistic multi-camera manipulation is likely 5-20x higher (estimate) (Derived from Isaac Lab benchmarks + SkyPilot prices)
- MuJoCo-Warp sim-only humanoid throughput: 2.73M steps/s with 8,192 worlds, about 0.10 GPU-h per 1B steps (well under $1 per 1B steps on any cloud GPU) (https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco_warp/blob/main/benchmarks/README.md)
- Cost per 1M synthetic 1080p images, rasterized (assumed 20-60 img/s/GPU incl. annotators): 4.6-13.9 GPU-h: $5-15 (L40S RunPod), $16-47 (AWS RTX PRO 6000), $21-62 (GCP G4 on-demand) (Modeling assumption (throughput) × SkyPilot prices; LOW-MEDIUM confidence)
- Cost per 1M synthetic 1080p images, RTX real-time ray-traced (assumed 5-15 img/s/GPU): 18.5-55.6 GPU-h: $20-61 (L40S RunPod), $62-187 (AWS RTX PRO 6000), $83-250 (GCP G4) (Modeling assumption × SkyPilot prices; LOW-MEDIUM confidence)
- Cost per 1M synthetic 1080p images, path-traced 64-256 spp denoised (assumed 1-4 s/img): 278-1,111 GPU-h: $303-1,211 (L40S RunPod), $934-3,737 (AWS RTX PRO 6000), $1,250-5,000 (GCP G4 on-demand) (Modeling assumption × SkyPilot prices; LOW-MEDIUM confidence)
- Storage/egress per 1M multimodal frames (RGB + depth + seg + labels, about 5.5 TB): About $126/month on S3 Standard ($0.023/GB-mo) and about $495 one-time internet egress ($0.09/GB). Exceeds raster render compute. (AWS list prices from prior knowledge (aws.amazon.com blocked); MEDIUM confidence)
- AV full sensor-rig simulation (8 cameras + lidar + radar, real time): 2-4 RT-core GPUs per ego plus CPU for traffic, about $2.7-14.5 per sim-hour; a 1,000-hour regression suite costs about $2.7k-14.5k per run (Modeling assumption × SkyPilot prices; LOW confidence)
- 24-month team cost, LEAN (12 people, full team from month 1): Annual base payroll ₩13.0/16.2/19.4억 (low/mid/high). 24-month people cost incl. 1.18× statutory load + severance, ₩25M/head/yr overhead and recruiting: ₩37.7/45.4/53.2억. Compute ₩4.1억 ($0.29M). Mid total about ₩49.6억 ($3.5M) (Own model;
  salary bands are planning assumptions (LOW confidence); FX 1,400 KRW/USD assumed)
- 24-month team cost, STANDARD (25 people): Base payroll ₩25.5/31.7/38.1억 per year; 24-month people cost ₩74.6/89.7/105.3억; compute ₩13.7억 ($0.98M; 32 render GPUs at 70% + 16 H100 at 40%). Mid total about ₩103억 ($7.4M) (Own model; LOW-MEDIUM confidence)
- 24-month team cost, AGGRESSIVE (45 people): Base payroll ₩46.9/58.2/70.2억 per year; 24-month people cost ₩136.7/164.3/193.4억; compute ₩44억 ($3.14M; 64 render GPUs + 64 H100/B200 at 50%). Mid total about ₩208억 ($14.9M) (Own model; LOW-MEDIUM confidence)
- Strategy D ramped plan (12 people months 1-12, then 25 people months 13-24): About 480 head-months × ₩1,495만 ≈ ₩72억 people + ₩8-12억 compute ≈ ₩80-84억 ($5.8-6.0M) over 24 months (Own model)
- Senior KRW salary bands used (2026 annual base, planning assumptions): CTO/architect ₩1.8-2.6억; senior physics/sim ₩1.2-1.8억; senior rendering/sensor ₩1.1-1.7억; senior RL/VLA ML ₩1.2-1.8억; PhD research scientist ₩1.5-2.5억; senior infra/MLOps ₩1.0-1.5억; senior web-3D/full-stack ₩0.9-1.3억; mid
  engineer ₩0.65-0.95억; tech artist ₩0.6-0.9억. Employer load ≈ 4대보험 about 10-11% + severance 8.33% ≈ 1.18× (Unverified (salary sites blocked); LOW confidence; validate with Wanted/Remember/headhunters)
- Strategy A effort estimate: 150-300 engineer-years (40-80 specialists × 3-5 years) ≈ ₩400-600억 ($30-43M) to approach Newton/MuJoCo + RTX-class parity (Own estimate anchored on MuJoCo/Newton/Genesis/Chrono/Drake histories; LOW-MEDIUM confidence)
- Time-to-MVP by strategy: A: 30-48+ months; B: 3-5 months (single vertical), 9-12 months to hardened multi-tenant SaaS; C: 9-15 months; D: 4-6 months MVP, 12-15 months to multi-domain GA (Own estimate)
- Decision matrix weights: Physics 15%, visual/sensor fidelity 15%, time-to-MVP 15%, 24-month cost 10%, licensing/SaaS legal safety 10%, strategic control/lock-in 10%, ease of use 10%, training integration 10%, Korean talent feasibility 5% (Own framework)
- Decision matrix scores (1-5 per criterion, in the order above) and weighted totals: A: 2,2,1,1,5,5,3,2,1 = 2.40. B: 4,5,5,3,2,1,3,5,4 = 3.70. C: 4,3,2,3,4,4,3,3,3 = 3.20. D: 5,4.5,4,4,4,3,4,5,4 = 4.23 (Own scoring)
